# Test Writing Guide

## Framework

- Bun Test (`bun:test`) — `describe`, `test`, `expect`, `beforeEach`
- Run all tests: `bun test`
- Run one file: `bun test <file>`
- Run `bun test` only from a directory whose `bunfig.toml` loads the host-safety preload guard: the repository root, `tests/` or `tests/integration/`. Bun reads only the `bunfig.toml` in the directory `bun test` starts in (it does not search upward). The repository root and every directory holding a test file have one whose first `[test]` `preload` entry names the guard, which loads before any test file and any other preload (see Host safety under Isolation). Started in a directory with no `bunfig.toml` (outside the repository, or one holding no test file, such as `src/`), the run has no guard. Start every run with its own scratch HOME, outside your home directory, and `SLACK_STATE_DIR` under it, as `tests/README.md` shows: the guard refuses to start (exit 78) when the home the run was launched with could reach the operator's real state (see The launch-home refusal under Host safety)
- When stdout is not a TTY (piped, or run by an agent), Bun prints only failing test titles. To see the passing titles, such as a `test.each` matrix's rows, run it in a TTY or add `--reporter=junit --reporter-outfile=<path>`

## Test File Organization

Each source module has a corresponding test file under `tests/`. Every `tests/*.test.ts` file appears in exactly one row below; a new test file gets a row, and a deleted one loses it:

| Source | Test File | What It Tests |
|--------|-----------|---------------|
| lib.ts, liveness-reading.ts, server.ts | server.test.ts | Lossless chunkText reconstruction, Unicode code-point limits and retained whitespace in both modes; AD liveness/readiness adapters, fresh waiting-only guards, typed refusals and existing non-screen recovery gates. |
| config.ts | config.test.ts | expandTilde; resolveServerStateDir / resolveServerConfigPath (`SLACK_STATE_DIR` made absolute, the `<XDG config home>/claude-slack-channel-bots` default (`~/.config/claude-slack-channel-bots`, bug b.fc4) when it is unset or empty, read at call time); the start-path primitives (b.av2 SR-8.7 as amended by b.deo SRI-403): readPersonaConfigBytes returns the file's exact bytes (a byte order mark and invalid UTF-8 included), and a missing file, a path under a regular file or a directory throws a read error whose start message (configFileReadFailureMessage) names the path and says what failed, while a read error with no errno code is "cannot be read" and not a missing file; its stat-first rule, through a recording `PersonaConfigFs` seam: the file opened once, its descriptor stat'ed, only a regular file read through it, and the descriptor always closed (a directory, `/dev/zero`, an injected FIFO, an injected fstat, read or close failure), plus a real FIFO in a child process; parsePersonaConfigBytes resolves bytes and text to what loading by path gives, and in record mode accepts a real-path collision (the same literal or a symlinked working_directory or credentials_file; the bring-up fails those personas instead) while still rejecting every other rule (duplicate name or key, a pre-persona key, an unknown key, a per-entry violation) with the default-mode error naming the record path; a malformed JSON file is reported at its line and column (CRLF, a byte order mark, a character outside the BMP) and echoes no file content, checked with `assertNoLeak` over a pasted fake token; resolveRealPath (real path, lexical fallback, injected realpath); resolveRealPathStrict (bug b.g57, no lexical fallback), on the real file system and through injected realpath and lstat: an existing directory or a symlink to one gives its real path, a directory not created yet (several levels down, a trailing slash, under a symlinked parent) gives its nearest resolvable ancestor's real path plus the rest, and a symlink that points to nothing (or a path under one), a path under a regular file (`ENOTDIR`), a symlink loop (`ELOOP`) and an injected `EIO`, `ESTALE` or `EACCES` are unresolvable with that code (`danglingSymlink` only for the dangling symlink), while an error with no code, a thrown string or a code that is not an errno code (a fake token) is `unknown` and never echoed; persona loader (resolvePersonaConfig, loadPersonaConfig), cross-persona rejections (duplicate name or key, shared working_directory or credentials_file), non-collision controls, SR-14 rejection table (as amended by b.deo SRI-1305); the prefix-related key rule (b.1ix follow-up): `dev`/`dev_2` pinned word for word (the one literal pin of the rule's reason), then either order, `horde`/`horde_admin`, an in-form name beside a hashed key that starts with it, pairs among three, a suggestion numbered past a taken `_main` and none when another key is a proper prefix, each naming both personas and no other and carrying the reason (b.jg5 SRJ-1102) as `prefixRelatedKeysReason` builds it from the two personas' `personaTmuxSessionName`s, followed by the hint; every rejected pair (AC 78) keeps none of the replaced reason's clauses, names no agent-director, CSCB, server, bare name or version, and says "by prefix" exactly once; `dev_a`/`dev_b`, `alpha`/`beta`, same-stem hashed keys and the suggested renames loading, equal keys keeping the uniqueness message, the check order (per-entry and duplicate rules first, the shared-path rule after), record mode rejecting with the default-mode error and a token-shaped name leak-checked; `personaKeysPrefixRelated` and `suggestNonPrefixingName` directly (either order, the `_main` numbering, a candidate over 40 characters checked by its derived key, no candidate equal to another persona's name or key); two personas sharing a channel both load it as `delivery: all` (AC 3 / AC 16); credentialsFilesToProtect (applied and current-file credentials paths, tolerant of an unreadable or unparseable file; a config.json that is not a regular file, injected, `/dev/zero` or a real FIFO in a child process, is never read and adds nothing, so only the applied paths are returned); referencedCredentialsPaths (b.av2 SR-8.3), reading no file: each `credentials_file` in declaration order with duplicates kept, from bytes or text alike, relative and `~user` paths made absolute as written, entries without a usable `credentials_file` skipped, and a top-level array, a top-level string or bytes that are not UTF-8 giving no paths without throwing (its `~` expansion and validation tolerance are covered through credentialsFilesToProtect); every persona rejection passes `assertNoLeak`; the 64 KiB cap (`MAX_RELOAD_FILE_BYTES`, b.av2 SR-8.1 as amended by b.deo SRI-401): a config file of exactly 65,536 bytes is read in full with the read asking for at most 65,537, one byte more is refused on its stat and never read (`EFBIG`, the path loader failing the same way) with the limit-naming read error and start message, which carry none of its sentinel-bearing content (`assertNoLeak`), and a 192 KiB file whose injected stat under-reports or omits its size, or whose read double returns more than it was asked for, is still refused by the bounded read. Unknown keys at every level (top level, persona entry, `dm`, channel entry), `bot_token` and `app_token` holding fake tokens included, are rejected naming the level and the key, never the value; a token-named key at any level is counted, not shown. Token-shaped persona names (b.av2 SR-1.2 as amended by b.deo SRI-102, SRI-202: no format rule; the AC 20 config leg): a name that is a fake token, or holds one, loads from `config.json`, from the record (record mode) and through `resolvePersonaConfig`, as written and keyed as usual, as do names near the token shape ("Xoxo bot", `inboxapp-bot`, `sandboxapp-1`, a prefix glued after a letter, a prefix with no dash or followed by a space or a letter, an upper-case prefix); every error of such an entry, from every stage (an unknown key, a `bot_token` or `app_token` value in the entry or in `dm`, a bad channel ID, a missing `permission_prompts`), names `personas[i]` with the name and key as written, and the same name twice gives the duplicate-name error naming it as written. Because the name carries the sentinel on purpose, each artifact is checked with `assertNoLeak` after the name and its key are masked (`withoutName`), which proves no credential value got in. The file's `loadError(input, name?)` expects a rejection, runs `assertNoLeak` over the whole error (with `name` given, over the error with that name and its key masked by `withoutName`) and returns its message, so every rejection case built on it is leak-checked. The server-wide reply settings (b.av2 SR-1.6 as amended by b.deo SRI-101, SRI-201; b.av2 SR-10.1, SR-10.2; AC 49): `ack_reaction`, `reply_chunk_limit` and `reply_chunk_mode` load as set, default to no reaction, 4000 and `newline`, and an empty `ack_reaction`, a non-positive or fractional limit or an unknown mode is rejected naming the key; the old `access.json` names (`ackReaction`, `textChunkLimit`, `chunkMode`) at the top level are rejected as unknown keys, and a leftover `access.json` beside `config.json` supplies none of the settings and is left byte-identical, with no file added to the directory. The server-wide `agent_director_call_timeout_ms` (b.jg5 SRJ-213): the default input resolves to 60000 (the one pinned literal; every other case uses the exported constants), both range ends (`MIN_AGENT_DIRECTOR_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, `MAX_AGENT_DIRECTOR_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`) load, and one below the minimum, one above the maximum, zero, a negative value, a non-integer, a numeric string and a non-numeric string (a placeholder, checked never to be echoed) are each rejected with exactly the full message, the loader's prefix naming the file (`loadPersonaConfig: invalid persona config in "<dir>/config.json": `) followed by the validation error naming the setting and both ends of its range; a persona entry that sets it is rejected as an unknown key of the entry; `agentDirectorCallTimeoutMsOf` returns a configuration's value, and the default for a null or undefined configuration. The channel-mode switch `allow_invited_channels` (b.deo SRI-101): `true` resolves to fungible mode, `false` and absent to the same declarative configuration, and every other value (a string, a number, `null`, an object, an array, `LEAK_SENTINEL` and a fake token among them) is rejected with one error that names the setting and no persona, never echoing the value, the switch validated before any persona entry. The `invited` section and the resolved form (b.deo SRI-102): `invited` is a `PERSONA_ENTRY_KEYS` key loading in both modes, every `PERSONA_INVITED_KEYS` key is accepted in fungible mode; in fungible mode `fungible_destination` is `invited.permission_prompts` (`"dm"` by default), `channels` is `[]` and `permission_prompts` undefined, in declarative mode `fungible_destination` is undefined and the declarative section is parsed, and `sections` holds the three recorded keys as written in both modes; a parity case loads `makeFungiblePersona` entries through the real loader and compares them with what `makeMultiPersonaConfig` builds. In declarative mode `invited` is neither checked nor read, a malformed one included (b.deo SRI-103). Fungible-mode validation (b.deo SRI-104): each shape, format and destination row is rejected naming the persona and the setting, a destination error saying whether `"dm"` is the default; a persona with no channels, no top-level `permission_prompts`, DMs off and a channel fungible destination loads; the cross-persona rows are rejected with the declarative-mode error, and in record mode as in declarative mode |
| config.ts (cron settings) | config-cron.test.ts | The top-level `cron_table_path`, `cron_log_path` and `cron_log_max_bytes` through `loadPersonaConfig` and `resolvePersonaConfig`, each case in its own `mkdtempSync` config directory with an injected temp home: accepted values load (paths made absolute, `~` expanded without mutating the input, a byte limit of 1 accepted); bad paths and limits are rejected, and a rejection message does not embed the offending JSON value; with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` set (the preload sets it), absent paths default under the loaded config's directory (or an explicit `configDir`); `cron_log_max_bytes` has no default; an unknown key is still rejected beside the cron keys. The crontable's default outside `~/.claude` (bug b.avm), each case with an explicit environment object and its own temp home, never `process.env`: `resolveDefaultCronTablePath` over `SLACK_STATE_DIR` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` unset (`<home>/.config/claude-slack-channel-bots/crontab`), an empty `SLACK_STATE_DIR` counted as unset, an absolute `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` honoured and an empty or relative one ignored, and `SLACK_STATE_DIR` set (`<config dir>/crontab`, whatever `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` says), also over `resolveServerConfigPath`'s own directory; the loader with no key and `SLACK_STATE_DIR` unset (the new default, the cron log still beside `config.json`); one row per shape of the key: `null`, and absent with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` set, take the default, while the default's own file written with `~` or absolute, another path with `~` and an absolute path whatever `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` says are each used as written; and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` read from the environment `parsePersonaConfigBytes` is given; and, through `runInFakeHome`, the default's home is the OS home read at call time |
| registry.ts, persona-bringup-controller.ts (the session drop) | registry.test.ts | Persona-keyed session registry CRUD (newer session replaces older, stale-close guard), pending sessions and in-place promotion, closePendingSession (removes the pending entry, stops the keep-alive, then closes the transport, in that order; a rejecting close is swallowed), matchPersonaByRootsPath (real-path roots cwd → exactly one persona), the posting-scope check checkPersonaTarget (a table over target kind, `dm.enabled` and the `post`/`act` action, with the allowed kind or the refusal reason) and the persona-scoped tools' refusals as tool errors; the compact MCP instructions' nine ordered points, all tools and five `via` values, `channel_name` and `part`, the `MCP_INSTRUCTIONS_MAX_CHARS` cap (2,048), and absent `claude/channel` capability through the negotiated transport (b.omv SR-7.1, SR-7.3). The unadvertised reload gesture (b.av2 SR-8.8, AC 74; see Unadvertised Reload Checks): each listed tool's whole definition, serialized to JSON (name, description, every schema property name and description), carries none of the reload terms; calling a `reload` or `apply_config` tool gets the unknown-tool refusal itself (a tool error whose content is a single text item matching `/unknown tool/i`, or a protocol error matching unknown tool or not found; any other tool error fails, so a hidden tool that refuses is caught) with no Slack call, no fetch and nothing leaked; the reload terms come from the shared `tests/test-helpers/reload-terms.ts`. Not offered to a bot (b.jg5 SRJ-511, AC 47; see Not-Offered Checks): each listed tool's whole serialized definition and the instructions the session receives carry none of the `clear-latch` terms (`clearLatchTermsIn`, `tests/test-helpers/clear-latch-terms.ts`), compared as a per-tool map so a failure names the tool and the terms. The instructions sent: a session's instructions equal the exported `MCP_INSTRUCTIONS` exactly, so the content audit in shipped-docs.test.ts (reload terms, forbidden terms) reads what instances receive; the instructions point to the launch system prompt's `<channel>` tag; the instructions still say to pass `chat_id` back. DM targets through the MCP server (b.av2 SR-5.1 as amended by b.deo SRI-601): with DMs off, every tool given a `D…`, `U…` or `W…` target is refused with a tool error naming the persona and target and no Slack call on any client, `reply` included, before the dry-run branch (AC 36); with DMs on, a reply in the delivered DM posts there through the persona's own client and removes its ack there (AC 37); a reply to a user ID runs `conversations.open` then posts to the returned conversation on the persona's own client, and a multi-chunk reply with a file opens once and sends everything there, removing the ack in the opened DM (AC 38); two replies to the same user open twice (nothing cached); a comma-separated user list is refused; a refused file stops a user-ID reply before any open; a post that fails after the open is a tool error naming the user ID and the opened conversation; the four act tools act on a `D…` conversation but refuse a user ID with no Slack call; a failed open (`missing_scope` with the `im:write` re-install hint, `user_not_found`, no conversation ID returned) is a token-safe tool error with nothing posted; a `D…` conversation Slack refuses is a tool error naming the DM target with no open fallback or retry; a zero-channel persona with DMs on answers its DM in place and is refused on any channel (AC 40/41); an unconfigured channel or group DM stays refused with DMs on; `dm.enabled` is read per call, so turning DMs off between two replies refuses the second; dry run for a `D…` conversation and for a user ID makes no Slack call. `download_attachment` token scope: isSlackHostedFileUrl accepts only https://files.slack.com; the persona's own bot token is sent as the bearer there, with manual redirects; external or third-party files are refused before any fetch; off-origin redirects are refused and never fetched; at most `MAX_DOWNLOAD_REDIRECTS` redirects are followed; a redirect with no `Location` or a non-2xx response skips the file; refusals are tool errors naming the persona that never echo the URL. A non-Slack failure (a fetch that throws) is a tool error with the generic `tool call failed` wording, nothing saved, and one log line that keeps the thrown message redacted. Not-up personas (b.av2 SR-6.3, SR-6.4): decideSessionAdmission over a stubbed up check and not-up description refuses a not-up persona's session with exactly one token-free line naming it and its cause, keeps its older registered session and neither promotes nor maps the pending one, and admits an up persona with no line; over the real bring-up controller and connection manager, a persona whose controller was cancelled is refused while its connection still serves, a roots path under a missing working directory (in several spellings) maps to that persona only and is refused with nothing thrown, a healthy persona beside a not-up one is admitted and replaced by a newer session as before, a Slack-unreachable persona that comes up on its own retry timer is admitted with no restart, and dry run; dropPersonaSession removes the entry and its MCP session IDs before closing the transport, so the close restarts nothing, and a persona with no session is a no-op; after a drop a new session is refused until the up query reports the persona up; a persona that stops being up with no session drops nothing. The held directory (b.jg5 SRJ-810, SRJ-1505; AC 53), through the real `decideSessionAdmission` with a stubbed held-directory query: two holds on D (two old instance ids) refuse a session from D with one line naming both ids in begin order (`sessionHeldRefusalLine`); D reached through a symlink, and a hold begun through a symlink with the session from D itself, are both refused as held, naming D by its real path; a missing D compares by its lexical path, so a session from it, with or without a trailing slash or a `..` segment, is refused as held, never as not up, with nothing thrown; the held check comes before persona matching (two personas sharing D, which the matcher would log, answer held with the one refusal line only); a query that throws refuses the session as held at its real path with no id, in one line naming what it threw, and an up persona is not admitted; the query is asked with the roots real path, and an answer of undefined, or one naming no id, is not held, the persona decided as before; with no query given, a hold changes nothing. The reopen cases are in persona-connections.test.ts. Ack removal by the calling persona (b.av2 SR-4.5), with the entry tracked under the session persona's key and the settings injected through the local `depsWithReplySettings(overrides)` over the harness's `getReplySettings`: a tracked ack is removed once, by the configured `ack_reaction` name, through the persona's own client; a failing `reactions.remove` doesn't fail the reply; no removal for an ack tracked on another message, a reply without `message_id` or no `ack_reaction`; two personas tracking one message each remove only their own reaction through their own client; a `message_id` only another persona tracked removes nothing on any client; a multi-chunk reply removes it once, after its first chunk. Chunking by the server-wide settings (b.av2 SR-1.6 as amended by b.deo SRI-101, SRI-201; b.av2 SR-5.2; b.iiz), a table through the real `reply` tool: defaults and configured modes/limits; retained split newlines, hard-split oversized lines, CRLF, whitespace-only chunks and supplementary Unicode characters at limits down to one. Assert the exact posted texts, `posts.map(p => p.text).join('') === text`, each chunk's `Array.from(chunk).length` within the limit, exact reconstruction of the input's code points and no other persona's client called (`chunkText`'s own edge cases are in server.test.ts). The bring-up controller it builds injects `checkConfigDir` over a scratch home (`configDirCheckUnder`), never the default's OS home. The channel mode (b.deo SRI-601), through the mode seam (`h.mode`, read by `h.modeDeps.getChannelMode` at each call): `checkPersonaTarget` per mode, a table each, and the fungible refusal `FUNGIBLE_TARGET_REFUSAL`; each tool's posting scope in fungible mode through the MCP server (an unlisted `C…` channel and a `G…` ID pass and land on the persona's own client only; a malformed, empty or lower-case ID, a comma-separated list and a `D…` ID with trailing text are refused as neither a channel ID nor an allowed DM target, a `D…` or `U…` target with DMs off is refused, and a `W…` user ID is refused for every tool but `reply`, each with a tool error naming the persona and the target and no Slack call on any stub; a user-ID reply with DMs on opens the DM on the persona's own client); the mode read at each call (flipping it between calls flips the outcome; deps with no mode reader decide as declarative mode); dry run in fungible mode; fungible mode never reading the declarative section on the outbound path, through the real loader too (b.deo SRI-202); Slack's refusals as tool errors in fungible mode, built by `slackRefusalToolErrorText` (b.deo SRI-602); the listing of exactly the six tools with their inputs unchanged and no reload wording (AC 74); and one instructions text, `MCP_INSTRUCTIONS`, for a session opened in either mode, saying where the persona may act with invited channels off and on and what the channel-delivery tool sets (b.deo SRI-604). |

Its cases pass no `fungible` argument, so they decide declarative mode; the fungible inputs are tested in delivery-decision-fungible.test.ts |
| delivery-decision.ts (fungible mode) | delivery-decision-fungible.test.ts | The decision table of b.deo SRI-1301: `decideDelivery` called directly in both channel modes with the rows of `tests/test-helpers/fungible-decision.ts` (`FUNGIBLE_DECISION_GROUPS`, turned into arguments by `decisionArgsOf`), one `test.each` per group, each outcome compared whole with strict equality: (1) declarative mode (switch absent or false) unchanged by every fungible input (b.deo SRI-308, SRI-202); (2) fungible mode with no stored choice and the flag false (b.deo SRI-306); (3) the persona's stored `all`; (4) every declarative section ignored, listed, malformed or absent (b.deo SRI-305); (5) the fungible path's five conditions, the first failure deciding, beside the same events in declarative mode (b.deo SRI-303); (6) group DMs; (7) the loop guard under a stored `all`; (8) non-message, `no-author` and own messages decided before the fungible conditions; (9) an unreadable store applying no stored choice; (10) a DM decided by `dm.enabled`. Completeness: every switch form × `channel_type` × flag form (`SWITCH_FORMS`, `CHANNEL_TYPE_FORMS`, `FLAG_FORMS`; 60 combinations) appears in some row, and every `FUNGIBLE_REFUSALS` entry is some row's expected outcome. Purity (b.deo SRI-309), over comment-stripped `src/delivery-decision.ts` through `tests/test-helpers/source-audit.ts`: type-only imports; no console, process, environment, file system, network, Bun, timer, `Date`, random number or `globalThis`; no module-scope `let` or `var`; both functions give every row its result with deep-frozen arguments, forwards then in reverse. No stub, server, timer or `mock.module`; the only file read is the decision's source. Declarative mode's own rules are delivery-decision.test.ts's |
| channel-delivery.ts, tests/test-helpers/channel-delivery.ts | channel-delivery.test.ts | The stored-choice store (b.deo SRI-1303). Fixed values in one pin: `CHANNEL_DELIVERY_FILE_NAME`, format version 1, the class list holding only `channel-delivery-unreadable`, `CHANNEL_DELIVERY_LOG_PREFIX` and the three `CHANNEL_DELIVERY_DROP_REASONS`. Location (b.deo SRI-401): `channelDeliveryPath` under a given state directory, following `SLACK_STATE_DIR` at call time when none is given; no other `src/` module spells the file name or names the serialiser or the parser. Format (b.deo SRI-402): a round trip through `serializeChannelDelivery` and `parseChannelDelivery`, the same bytes whatever the order entries were added in, a key with no channels left out, and every refused form (made by altering the serialiser's output, its bytes for the UTF-8 rule) read as unreadable with the file left byte-identical. The helper's contract (b.deo SRI-1204): `writeChannelDeliveryRecord` writes the serialiser's bytes at the module's path (`SAMPLE_SET_AT` when a seed gives no `set_at`) and refuses the real home, the OS temp directory itself and any directory outside it; `readChannelDeliveryRecord` answers `null` for no file and throws on a refused file naming the path, never its content; `declarationOf` equals the declaration the store records. The read at start (b.deo SRI-403, SRI-904): a missing file is an empty, readable record with nothing written; a file that cannot be read, parsed or validated, a directory, and a real FIFO with no writer (in a child process; skipped where `mkfifo` is unavailable) each stay in place with one line and give an unreadable store for the run, which stores, drops and writes nothing; a record over 64 KiB is read whole; the bytes are parsed only; the `channel-delivery-unreadable` line is pinned per kind. Writes (b.deo SRI-404, SRI-905): an accepted set is one durable write of the whole record and no store operation returns a promise; a failure at each `DurableWriteFs` step before the rename, an unsynced rename whose write-back fails too, and one over a file that did not exist leave memory as it was; the failed-write line is pinned. The file holds the credentials file's path, never its content (b.deo SRI-907). Drops (b.deo SRI-405): in memory at once, one write per batch, a key named twice dropped once, an unknown reason refused with a `RangeError` before any change, unwritten drops carried by the next successful write (`writeUnwrittenDrops`), the drop line pinned. Start rules (b.deo SRI-407): each rule's drop beside its kept control, a path respelled through a symlink whose target later changes, and a failed start-rule write. Retiring keys (b.deo SRI-502) are held in memory only, never written or logged. Every file sits under a per-case `mkdtempSync` root with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` restored after each case, the clock is `createFakeClock`, and every case runs `assertNoLeak` over its lines and its state directory's files; no `mock.module` |
| registry.ts (`set_channel_delivery`), channel-delivery.ts | set-channel-delivery.test.ts | The `set_channel_delivery` tool (b.deo SRI-1304). Driven through the reload harness's `run.callTool` (kept sessions over `createSessionServer`, the tool deps bound as `server.ts` binds them: the mode in effect, the run's stored-choice store, the routing's heard set and the applied personas), with channels heard through `run.deliver`; the listing and what `run.callTool` cannot express (a session matched to no persona, no store bound, a stand-in store's `invalid` answer) go through a direct in-memory client over `createSessionServer`. Listing (b.deo SRI-501): every session lists the tool with its exported name and descriptions and its two required inputs, the same in both modes. Resolution (b.deo SRI-502), before the refusal order: an unmatched session, a persona that is not up beside an up one that is accepted, an unapplied key, a retiring key (accepted once its retiring ends) and a session the registry dropped or replaced; a source audit that nothing is awaited from resolution to the store write. Refusals (b.deo SRI-503): rule 1 (declarative mode), rule 2 (an unreadable store, or none bound, naming the file), rules 3 and 4 (the delivery and the channel) with each row's echo rule, the known-channel branches and the order with several causes at once; `LEAK_SENTINEL` and a fake token as `delivery` or `channel` appear in no tool error (b.deo SRI-907). Accepted calls (b.deo SRI-504): the first creates the file in one write with the clock's `set_at` and the applied declaration, the choice decides the next event, and the value already stored is written again; exactly one `persona-channel-delivery-set` line per accepted call and none per refused one (b.deo SRI-505, SRI-902), whose pin holds the suite's one typed wording. A failed write through `h.failChannelDeliveryWrites` gives the write-failed tool error (`channelDeliveryWriteFailedText`, naming the persona and the file) and exactly one `[slack] channel-delivery:` store line, with the file and the stored choices in memory unchanged; a store's `invalid` answer gives the not-stored tool error (`channelDeliveryNotStoredText`) with no line (b.deo SRI-506); dry run applies the same rules and stores the choice (b.deo SRI-507), with `SLACK_DRY_RUN` saved, cleared and restored per case. Call logs: no Web API call is added to any stub's log (every persona's and the direct client's) during a tool call, while a case's own setup deliveries make their own calls; `afterEach` asserts that no run posted and runs `assertNoLeak` over every run's `captured()`, tool result and console line. | permission-poller.ts, permission-click-handler.ts, permission-trail.ts, permission-action-id.ts (permission relay) | permission-poller.test.ts, permission-click-handler.test.ts, permission-poller-trail-file.test.ts, block-action-received.test.ts, action-id-parser.test.ts, relay-repro.test.ts, permission-relay-wiring.test.ts, permission-trail.test.ts, permission-trail-concurrent.test.ts | SR-2.1 poller loop (rows resolved to personas by the `persona` label, prompts and the wedge warning sent to the persona's destination through its client) and Block Kit click handler (resolved and updated through the receiving persona); trail events; action-id parsing. relay-repro.test.ts's SR-8.4 capstone (two rows, the plural projection end to end) passes every post, update, `decide` and `get-permission` call to `assertNoLeak`. A row with no `persona` label (b.1ix) logs its skip line once per instance ID across ticks with no `get` or Slack call; a failed list forgets nothing, and a row no longer listed logs again when it comes back. Destinations (b.av2 SR-7.1 as amended by b.deo SRI-701, SRI-702): a channel destination posts with no `conversations.open`; a `dm` destination with DMs on runs `conversations.open` for its contact, then `chat.postMessage` to the returned `D…`, both on the persona's own client (AC 29), including a DM-only persona's first prompt with no DM yet (AC 44); one open serves every prompt of the persona, across ticks, and an injected destination resolver's cached DM is reused; two personas in one tick each route by their own setting on their own client (AC 34); a failed open or post is not tracked and emits one `ok: false` trail event; a `dm` destination with DMs off or no contact (which the loader rejects) makes no Slack call; the wedge warning opens and posts in the DM and latches, a refused one neither calls Slack nor latches, and a failed one logs the episode line, not a per-attempt line, and does not latch. Destination failures (b.av2 SR-7.1 as amended by b.deo SRI-702, SRI-703; b.av2 SR-3.2; b.av2 SR-10.3 as amended by b.deo SRI-906), each poller built with a `destinationHold` on a fake clock: `missing_scope` on a DM-only persona's first open (AC 44) and `not_in_channel` on a channel post each hold the persona's prompts, with one `persona-destination-failed` line in all (the `missing_scope` line names `im:write` and the re-install), no Slack call, trail event or line inside a backoff window, failed retries at 5 s and a further 10 s, then, once the cause clears, the prompt posted exactly once and one cleared line; a held persona doesn't hold another persona's prompts; a request that closes while held is never posted or updated; a payload error (`invalid_blocks`, `msg_too_long`) is not held and its message is never posted again (b.zmq): one line and trail event, then the next tick posts the short prompt naming the tool and the error, tracked so it closes; a short prompt rejected too makes two posts in all, each logged and trailed once, nothing tracked, and once the request is no longer observed it is forgotten; a failing stuck-prompt warning joins the persona's episode, a throttle window inside the backoff attempts nothing and doesn't move the throttle, and exactly one warning posts once it clears; a warning whose due retry finds the client unavailable gives its attempt back, and one warning posts once the client returns and the cause clears (wedge test 12). A token-shaped Slack error is logged and trailed as `unknown_error`, checked with `assertNoLeak` (the trail-event table and wedge test 6b). A throw while a persona's retry is due (a row the prompt builder rejects, a trail emitter or logger that throws, a whole tick that throws) leaves no unhandled rejection and the persona retrying: another persona's row in the same tick still posts (except after a whole-tick throw), and its prompt, or its warning at the next throttle window, still posts; these cases run the real poller in a child `bun` process that counts unhandled rejections (see Process-Level Listeners). One failure stays with its row or request (b.zmq): a request that throws is logged once while it stays open and the row's next request posts, and logs again if listed again after it is no longer observed; a row that throws counts as not read (its tracked prompt neither closed nor posted again), logged once while it keeps failing, and keeps its stuck-prompt count (the tick it throws neither counts nor resets it, so the warning trips on schedule); a closed entry whose closing throws is kept and logged once while another closes; a whole-tick throw is logged once until a tick runs to its end; a prompt that posts but whose success trail event throws is tracked, posted once over three ticks and logged once; a closing failure has its own record (`failedClosings`), so a request whose trail event threw still logs its closing failure once, then closes once the throw stops; a payload rejection is recorded before its line, so a logger throwing on that line cannot bring the rejected message back: the next tick posts the short prompt, and the throw is logged once as the request's failure. The six b.zmq lines are pinned through their exported builders (`buildPromptFailedLine`, `buildSpawnFailedLine`, `buildClosingFailedLine`, `buildPromptRejectedLine`, `buildFallbackPromptRejectedLine`, `buildTickFailedLine`) against the text the debugging skill quotes (b.jg5 SRJ-1014). The prompt's section (b.zmq) stays within `SLACK_SECTION_TEXT_MAX` with the truncation marker when cut, never splitting a surrogate pair; a 10 000-character Bash command posts once across several ticks, tracked; a tool name over `PROMPT_TOOL_NAME_MAX` ends `…` in the text and the header. Wiring (permission-relay-wiring.test.ts, source audit): the poller and the notifier get the one shared resolver and the one `createPersonaDestinationHold` built over it, and shutdown calls its `cancelAll()` once. Updates after a destination change (b.av2 SR-5.1 as amended by b.deo SRI-601): the closing update of a DM prompt after DMs are turned off, and of a channel prompt after the destination changes to `dm`, is one `chat.update` in the conversation it was posted in, with no post or open (AC 36); a click on a DM prompt is decided once and updates that `D…` conversation through the persona's own client only, and after DMs are turned off still makes that one update and no post or open; A's channel prompt and D's DM prompt, clicked in one run, each update through their own client. A persona that is not up (b.av2 SR-6.4, each not-up state through the real createPersonaUpPredicate): its rows get no `get`, post, update or trail event while an up persona's row in the same tick posts; a skipped request posts once after it comes up; no stuck-prompt warning while it is not up (after, K fresh empty ticks); its wedge count and a posted prompt (even one whose row closed) are held, not advanced, dropped or re-posted, until it is up; a request not posted because its client is unavailable keeps its one line; the skip is logged once when it starts and once when it ends, and a persona dropped from the applied set ends it with one line, its row then logged as not applied on each tick. In-place changes (b.av2 SR-8.6 as amended by b.deo SRI-802; AC 58): a prompt posted before a `dm.contact` change closes with one `chat.update` on its recorded DM and ts, with no open and no post, while the persona's next prompt opens the DM with the new contact and posts there, and another persona's tracked prompt stays put; with the applied set swapped mid-tick (a confirmed reload's step 1 landing during an await of the tick), a prompt goes to the destination applied now when the destination or DM settings change while the row's `get` is in flight (a DM destination moved to a channel, a channel moved to a DM with a new contact) and when the destination changes between two prompts of one row; a persona removed during the `get` (on the prompt path or the stuck-prompt path) or between two prompts posts nothing more, logs the not-applied line once and keeps its tracked prompt. A click on a prompt posted before an in-place change (its channel dropped from `channels` and its destination moved, or `dm.contact` changed) is decided once and makes its one `chat.update` where the prompt was posted, through the persona's own client, with no post and no open, and another persona's prompt clicked in the same run is unaffected. `forgetPersonaPrompts(key)` at a teardown (b.av2 SR-6.5 as amended by b.deo SRI-307; AC 57): drops B's tracked prompts (matched by key or `cscb_<key>`), its not-posted record, payload-rejection record (b.zmq: B's next attempt posts its full prompt again), stuck-prompt count and not-up skip episode, none of A's, with no Slack call and no trail event; a later tick makes no closing update for B, and a leftover row still labelled for B gets no get, post or tracked prompt. AC 20 (b.av2 SR-10.3 as amended by b.deo SRI-901 to SRI-906): an agent-director call failing with an error that is not an agent-director error, carrying fake tokens, is logged as its `describeThrownValue` description (type, safe code, redacted message, frames) on each of the poller's three read sites (`list`, `get`, `getPermission`) and on the click handler's `decide failed` line, and an agent-director error whose `errName` is token-shaped falls back to the description (`AgentDirectorError` and its redacted message, no `errName`) on `get`, `getPermission` and `decide failed`, while one with a safe `errName` gives a line that is exactly the `errName` and its redacted description, with no frames, on the same three, with no leak in any log line, trail event or Slack call; the decide failure's `cscb.ad_decide.attempted` trail event keeps `raw_error_message` only after `redactSlackLogText` (URL and fake token shown as the redaction placeholders), on the `other` branch and both carve-outs (`ErrSystemInstallDisappeared`, `ErrTmuxNotAvailable`). The trail writer alone (permission-trail.test.ts, permission-trail-concurrent.test.ts, into a temp `SLACK_STATE_DIR`): one JSON line per event with every envelope field and per-event field verbatim, `ts` stamped in RFC 3339 with milliseconds (a caller's valid `ts` kept, a missing or empty one stamped), lines appended in call order, the file and a missing parent directory created on first write; the poller, click-handler and decide call sites writing back to back, and 30 interleaved writes, give complete, separately parseable lines. `decide` answering `ErrInvalidFlags` (b.jg5 SRJ-204, SRJ-104, SRJ-122): through the real installed re-check (the default `ClickDeps.recheckAdVersion`) over `makeStubResolveSystemBinary`, flushed with the fake clock's `flush()` after the handler returns: exactly one resolve call with no stop or startup error, the handler's one `ErrInvalidFlags` log line, the trail event's `result_class` `ErrInvalidFlags` with no `raw_error_message`, one `decide` call (no retry), and no `chat.update` or `markHandled`; an injected `recheckAdVersion` runs once, after the log line, in place of the installed one; one whose call never settles does not hold the handler's return (the Slack ack): through the real installed re-check over `makeStubResolveSystemBinary({ outcomes: [{ never: true }] })` on a fake clock, the handler returns true with the clock unmoved and one resolve call; advancing the clock by `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_TIME_LIMIT_MS` fires only the call's time-limit timer, with still one call and no stop (the test is bounded by bun's per-test timeout, not a real timer it races); `ErrAmbiguousRequest`, `ErrAlreadyDecided`, `ErrRelayFallenBack`, another agent-director error and a plain error make no re-check, installed or injected. The wedge warning's text (b.jg5 SRJ-1012, SRJ-1001; AC 78), checked through the exported `buildWedgeWarningBody` and `buildWedgeWarningText`: one pin case holds SRJ-1012's text, the instance id in both commands, and the full text as the persona prefix and that body; for A's and B's instance ids the body's only two code spans are `agent-director read-pane` and `agent-director kill` for that id, the kill followed by "check the result"; the text has no "tmux-kill", no tmux command or mention and no respawn step; and the body carries SRJ-1001's human-only sentence, matched by the shared `HUMAN_ONLY_SENTENCE` (tests/test-helpers/conflict-cases.ts). The tripped persona's posted warning equals the builder's text for that spawn's own instance id: A's in the detector's case 1, and B's in case 5b (to B's destination, not naming A's instance, no Slack call on the other personas' clients, the trail naming B's instance). During a persona teardown (b.jg5 SRJ-1003; `teardownNotices`): with A's window open at the trip, the warning's body, equal to `buildWedgeWarningBody`'s for A's instance, is handed to the notifier once, with one line, nothing reaches A's destination or the hold, and the latch holds across later ticks; a window read that throws is logged token-safely and read as not open, so the warning posts to A's destination; a notifier that rejects logs the handed line, then one token-safe failure line, the warning still latched and never posted. Fungible mode (b.deo SRI-702, SRI-906): the tick posts each prompt once at the persona's fungible destination (a channel, or its DM by `"dm"`) on the persona's own client, the live entry recording that conversation and the persona key; a refused fungible destination gives one line per open request naming the setting in force, with no Slack call, live entry or trail event. |

| clear-latch.ts, server.ts (the `/clear-latch` route and the `server.port` record), cli.ts (the `clear-latch` command) | clear-latch.test.ts | Both halves of `clear-latch` (b.jg5 SRJ-509, SRJ-510, SRJ-511): the server's route and record, and the command's lines, exit codes and request. The route: the real `handleClearLatch` over injected deps (a two-persona config from `makeMultiPersonaConfig`, a recording clear by hand answering true or false, held pending or failing, a capturing logger): any method but POST → 405, checked before the caller; a non-loopback, empty or absent address → 403 before the body is read; a missing, empty or non-string `persona` (a JSON array, string, number or null body included) → 400 naming `persona`; invalid or empty JSON → 400; an unreadable body → 400; a body over `/interject`'s 32 KB cap → 400, one at the cap served; none of these clears anything or logs a line. A token typed by mistake, the name in another case, a key with a trailing space, a config with no personas or none loaded → 404 with one line that never repeats the argument. A persona by name or key → exactly one clear by hand of its key and 200 with `cleared` as the clear answered, the not-latched line only for `false`; 127.0.0.1, ::1 and ::ffff:127.0.0.1 are each served; the 200 waits for a pending clear by hand; a clear that rejects or throws → 500 and one leak-free line naming the persona. One real request over a port-0 `Bun.serve` on 127.0.0.1 clears only the named persona. The record, in a `mkdtempSync` stand-in for the state directory: its name and path beside `server.pid` and its text (the JSON object and a newline); a write-then-read round trip leaving no temporary file; a second write replacing the first whole; one call of the injected write with the whole text; a failed write or rename rethrown, the earlier record whole and the temporary file removed; the write-failure line; every unusable read (absent, gone before the read, unreadable, malformed, out of range, another PID) as its cause without throwing, the causes distinct; in-range ports with extra keys ignored; removal of the record, an absent file and a refused delete tolerated. A comment-stripped source audit of `src/server.ts`: the names are imported from their modules; `main()`'s `fetch` has one `/clear-latch` branch before the `/mcp`-only 404, returning the handler over the request, the caller's address, the applied config and the clear by hand; that clear is `clearByHandOf` applied once in `main()` to its one `buildLatchRecheck` result; the record is written once in `main()` after `Bun.serve(` and before `writePidFile`, with this PID and the listener's bound port, in a top-level `try` whose catch logs one described line and goes on to the PID file, at `serverPortFilePath` of the PID file's state directory; `shutdown()` removes it once beside the PID file and nothing else touches its path. A load audit (`forbiddenServerLoads`): `src/clear-latch.ts`, which the CLI imports, loads no server-only or Slack module. The route name, log lines, causes and cap are imported from `src/`; the response bodies are the handler's own literals, pinned; every argument that must not be repeated is built with `fakeToken`, and `assertNoLeak` runs over every line and body. The clear by hand itself is `conflict-latch.test.ts`'s. The command (describe `clear-latch: the command`): `createCli(…).clearLatch` over a local `CliDeps` whose members `clear-latch` must not use throw (the loaders, spawns, kill, director verbs, the credentials runner, the client init, `unlinkSync`), a recording dial with scripted answers, a `createFakeClock` clock (no timer left pending) and the same stand-in state directory; every run checks that it writes and removes nothing, and `assertNoLeak` runs over stderr. SRJ-509's literal lines appear only in its one pin table (`SRJ-509's lines, pinned once`), which compares each line, the usage entry and the 30000 ms wait with the exported builders (`CLEAR_LATCH_USAGE`, `CLEAR_LATCH_USAGE_ENTRY`, `clearLatchCli*Line`, `clearLatchStatusCause`, `CLEAR_LATCH_WAIT_MS`); every other case imports them from `src/cli.ts`. One case per row: no argument, an empty one, two, or a token typed twice → the usage line, exit 2, nothing read or dialled; an absent, unreadable (a directory), non-numeric or stale PID file → the no-server line, exit 1, only the PID file read, no request and a valid `server.port` beside it neither used nor removed; each unusable `server.port` cause (absent, unreadable, malformed, out of range, another PID) → the not-answered line with that cause and no request, and a record naming another PID is not used even while that PID runs too; a failed dial (a refused connection or a thrown string carrying the sentinel, a `TypeError`) → the not-answered line with `describeThrownValue`'s description, redacted; a 201, 400, 403, 405, 500 (one body carrying a fake token, one a well-formed answer) or 503 → `HTTP <status>`, never the body; a malformed 200 (empty, not JSON, null, an array, no `cleared`, a string `cleared`, `ok` false or absent, an empty or numeric `persona`, a token for `cleared`) → `HTTP 200`; a dial that never answers ends at exactly `CLEAR_LATCH_WAIT_MS` on the fake clock with the did-not-confirm line, never the not-answered one; a sleep that wakes early (`maxStepMs`) is slept again until the bound; an answer after the bound adds no line; an answer 1 ms before the bound is the answer; a 404 → the no-persona line, the argument dialled as typed and never printed; a 200 by name or by key, latched or not → exit 0 at once with the answered name and its `personaKey`; a name holding a quote, a tab or a line break prints escaped as `renderPersonaRef` renders it. The CLI dials the port in `server.port` while `config.json` and the last-applied record name other ports, and reads only the PID file and the record. Against the real handler: a clear received before the wait ran out (its clear held in P's turn) and one received only after it each run once, the CLI having ended on did-not-confirm; over real HTTP, the production dial reaches a port-0 `Bun.serve` on 127.0.0.1 at the recorded port and clears the persona, and once that server stops (its `stop()` awaited until the port is released) the same run gets the not-answered line with the connection's failure, the released port held meanwhile by `holdResettingListener(port)` (a loopback TCP listener the case holds, counting each connection and closing it unanswered), so no other process can take the port and answer, and the dial is seen to reach it. `dialClearLatch` (describe `dialClearLatch: the production dial`), over a recording `ClearLatchRequest` (`fakeHttp`, an emitter-backed stand-in for `node:http`'s `request` whose response the case drives): one call whose options equal `clearLatchRequestOptions(port, bytes)` (a `POST` to `CLEAR_LATCH_ROUTE` on `CLEAR_LATCH_DIAL_HOST` at the port, `Content-Type: application/json`, `Content-Length` the body's UTF-8 byte length (a persona name holding a two-byte character, so bytes and characters differ) and `agent: false`), naming the same place as `clearLatchUrl(port)`; the request ended once with the whole `{"persona"}` body; the answer's status and body text, the body arriving split inside a two-byte character, as a Buffer and then as text; a non-200 status (404) answered as is, not rejected; the request call throwing synchronously, the request emitting `error` (a failed connection) and the response emitting `error` (a failed body read) each rejecting with that same error; a response aborted before its end rejecting at the abort, with no `end` following, with a plain `Error` of the dial's own naming neither the persona nor the body read, and, as a control, the same body ended with no abort answered as read. The proxy case (SRJ-510): with `HTTP_PROXY` and `http_proxy` naming a `holdResettingListener()` the case holds (answering nothing) and no `NO_PROXY`, `fetch` to the route fails, reaches the proxy and clears nothing (so the case is not vacuous), while the production dial still reaches a real port-0 handler on 127.0.0.1 and clears the persona, the proxy seeing no further connection; the variables are restored in `finally`. Two Bun quirks shape it: it sets the proxy variables through `Bun.env`, the runtime's own environment, because some suites replace `process.env` with a plain copy the runtime no longer reads; and it unsets a variable by setting it to `''` before deleting it, because after a bare delete Bun's `fetch` keeps the old proxy for the rest of the process, poisoning later suites. The usage text, the entry point and `realDeps`' dial are `cli.test.ts`'s A `server.port` write that fails is rethrown, and the line `server.ts` logs for it holds the record's path, the safe code and the redacted message (`serverPortWriteFailedLine`; `assertNoLeak`). |

| state-dir-migration.ts | state-dir-migration.test.ts | The move of the state directory from the old defaults, `~/.claude/channels/slack` (`L1`) and `<XDG config home>/cscb` (`L2`; 0.14.0's crontable and `prompts/`, 0.15.0's whole state directory), to `<XDG config home>/claude-slack-channel-bots` (`N`; bugs b.fc4, b.smv): `prepareStateDir` (one step, its old directory `L` below), `migrateStateDirAtStart` (`L2` then `L1`), `stateDirMigrationSteps`, `resolveCliStateDir` and `resolveCliConfigPath`. The b.smv cases cover each starting point: `L1` only (0.13 and earlier), `L1` and a partial `L2` (0.14.0, with and without its crontable), a full `L2` with `L1` linked to it (0.15.0), a rerun, a server of the old release on either old directory, a conflict, `SLACK_STATE_DIR` set and an absolute `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`. Every case runs under its own `mkdtempSync` root holding a fake home, with an explicit environment object (never `process.env`) and every log line through `assertNoLeak`; failures are injected one file-system operation at a time through `deps.fs`, and every other operation is the real file system on the scratch tree. Paths: `SLACK_STATE_DIR` set and non-empty is `overridden` and moves nothing; an absolute `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` moves `N`, an empty or relative one is ignored. Nothing to move: `L` absent or a dangling link (`no-legacy`), `L` a link to `N` or `N` a link to `L` (`already-migrated`), neither logging. `N` absent: `L` is renamed whole and becomes a symbolic link to `N`. `N` present (a host with b.avm's crontab and prompts links into it): entries absent from `N` move (a file as a hard link, a directory renamed, a link re-made with an absolute target), entries equal in `N` (b.avm links, one inode, equal bytes, an empty directory) are dropped, an entry that `N` holds as a link back to `L`'s own entry (b.avm's cross-file-system `prompts/` fallback) is moved over the link, and `L` becomes a symbolic link to `N`; each leftover is compared again before it is removed, so a write through the old name since leaves it as a `leftover` (`pending`). Symbolic links in `L`: one pointing at its own destination in `N` is dropped (a real file there is untouched), a dangling one moves as it is (a relative one re-made absolute) and settles against the same target at `N`, and a different target or a regular file at `N` is a conflict. A conflict (different contents or kinds): `N`'s entry wins, `L`'s is untouched, one WARN, `pending`, and a later start finishes the move once the operator settled it. An `L` that cannot be a source: a link to another directory, a file. A server of the old release running on `L` (a live PID in the regular file `L/server.pid`, also through a link to another directory, with no WARN) stops the start; a stale PID, the process's own PID, unreadable text and a PID file that is a link do not. Failures: `failed` (stops the start, nothing lost; its line's hand-fix follows `newDirExists`: `mv L N && ln -s N L` with `N` absent, a per-entry `mv` with `N` present) and `pending` (a WARN, the data is at `N`). Across file systems (`EXDEV` and the other link-unsupported errnos): a checked copy placed from a temporary name, a directory copied to a temporary name beside its destination and renamed into place, a copy of a file that changed discarded. Re-runs change and log nothing. The repoint of `L1` (0.15.0: a full `L2`, `L1` a link to it) is covered both ways: `L1` is repointed straight at `N` after `L2` is renamed, with its one information line and a second start changing nothing; a failure to repoint is one WARN with the `ln -sfn` to run, the outcome unchanged and the start not stopped; and an `L1` that is not exactly the chain the migration made (`L1` a link to exactly `L2`, `L2` a link to exactly `N`, both really reaching `N`) is never repointed and logs nothing: an operator's own link to another directory (it keeps its own WARN), a link through another link, the reverse arrangement (`L2` a directory, `N` the link), `L2` and `N` both links to an operator's directory, `L2` reaching `N` through another link, a chain with `N` absent, and an `L1` whose path passes through a link. A fresh 0.15.0 install (only `L2`) ends with `N` alone, `L1` absent and nothing else logged. A stop is recorded in `startupErrorDir`, `resolveCliStateDir`'s answer computed before the move: the step's old directory on a 0.15.0 host, `L1` and not the stopping `L2` on a 0.14.0 host (so 0.14.0's `L2` failure is recorded in `L1` and its line has no `SLACK_STATE_DIR=<L2>` hint, since `L2` holds no configuration), `N` itself when it already holds `config.json` and no old server runs, and the stopping step's old directory when `startupErrorDir` is absent, so a stop never creates `N`. `stateDirMigrationStopsStart` and `stateDirStopMessage`. `resolveCliStateDir` (the commands other than the server's start, which only read) answers `L` while `L/server.pid` is a regular file naming a live process (an injected `isRunning`), whatever `N` holds, and otherwise while `L` is a directory, or a link to another directory, holding a configuration (or a PID file) and `N` holds none; a PID file that is a link does not count. `L2` is checked before `L1`. |

| persona-start.ts (steps 1–3), persona-bringup-controller.ts (the server's start) | persona-bringup.test.ts | The real controller over the real connection manager, as `startupSessionManager`'s `bringUp`, launching through `spawnForPersona`: the SR-6.1 order per persona (credentials, directory, Slack, then the launch once that persona is up, with its own file's tokens); a Slack step that throws (broken, class `error`, one token-free line naming the error with its message redacted, not retried or launched); a credentials path that is a directory, character device or FIFO refused unread; held notices flushed per persona when it comes up; end to end, no agent-director call for one persona's connection trouble. The `claude_config_dir` hold (bug b.g57), through the real controller with the session manager's launch check as its re-check and the manager's `stop` recorded: a relaunch that finds A's directory unresolvable while A is up makes no agent-director call, stops A's connection once, drops its MCP session once, keeps its row, leaves A `retrying` with the `configDir` cause and not up, tells `onLeftUp` once and logs one line, B unchanged; while it stays unresolvable A is re-checked on 5 s doubling to 300 s with no cap, connecting, launching, calling and logging nothing; once it resolves, one cleared line, then Slack validation with the held credentials (the file not re-read), then one launch that resumes A's row (no kill, delete or fresh spawn) with no confirmation; with Slack unreachable at that point A is Slack-retrying and the launch waits for the manager's `up`, then runs once; a recovery launch whose ladder finds the directory unresolvable again keeps the row and holds A again, its new connection closed, and resumes the same row once it resolves; a hold that starts while A retries its bring-up for Slack closes it, so no Slack retry runs while held, and A connects and is launched once the directory resolves; a persona Slack had refused keeps the refusal when held (connection closed, then one `stays broken` cleared line, no Slack step, no launch); `cancel(A)` during the hold leaves no timer and nothing is re-checked, connected or launched afterwards, and A dropped from the applied set ends its retries at its next re-check with one line; `holdForConfigDir` for a persona never brought up or cancelled returns false and holds nothing (no line, no timer, no stop), so the launch path logs the line itself; a bring-up whose directory is a symlink pointing to nothing never connects A (no Slack step, no client) or launches it, and A ends `retrying` while B is launched, then connects and is launched once the drive is back; a directory retry for A with a missing working directory and a dangling `claude_config_dir` checks the latter only once the working directory is back, holds A there with no connection, and connects and launches it once both resolve. A confirmed credentials change while A is held (up before, or retrying for Slack) resolves `retrying` with no connection: nothing opens, no swap hook runs, the new file is held and A returns on the new tokens; a new file Slack would refuse is taken with no Slack call and leaves A broken by its credentials at the return (not launched, not retried); a missing new file fails with one change-failed line and A returns on its held tokens; a hold that starts during a confirmed change's reconnect (its first attempt in flight, or retrying with the old connection kept) closes the reconnect with the connection, with no swap ever, and A returns on the new file, except in the row where Slack refused A's own connection while the reconnect's first attempt was in flight: that change resolves `skipped`, A stays `broken` with the old content held (the change pending again), and the recovery makes no Slack step, swap or launch. Notices during the hold: a notice for A raised while held posts nothing, and after the recovery reconnects A it is posted exactly once, through A's client to A's destination (none on B's); an event on B during A's hold is still delivered, tagged with B. A hold while A's lost connection is still reopening ends that episode with one `cleared: the persona's Slack connection was closed while its claude_config_dir cannot be resolved` line; the recovery connects on a fresh episode (no restored line), and a later drop opens and restores a new one. A Slack launch queued (after the manager's `up`) behind an operation that holds A does nothing when its turn comes, though the directory resolved by then; the recovery's own launch runs once, after the reconnect |
| persona-notifier.ts | persona-notifier.test.ts | Per-persona notices through `makeNotifierHarness` (its destination hold on a fake clock): destination and identity, the persona prefix, dry run, `dm`-destination notices (SR-7.2: one held before validation makes no Slack call, then on flush opens the DM on the persona's own client and posts there (AC 44); several held notices flush with one open, in raised order, and a later notice posts with no second open; several flushed onto a failing shared open log one episode line, not one per notice, and post in raised order once the open succeeds; a refused `dm` destination makes no Slack call, no callback and holds nothing; dry run makes no Slack call of any kind and holds nothing), held notices flushed per persona in raised order, the `MAX_HELD_NOTICES_PER_PERSONA` cap (oldest dropped with one line, never posted). Destination failures (SR-7.1 as amended by b.deo SRI-702, SRI-703; SR-10.3 as amended by b.deo SRI-906): a failed open or post, on a channel or `dm` destination, is held, not dropped, with one `persona-destination-failed` line per episode (naming `im:write` for `missing_scope`), retried on the backoff and posted once the cause clears, with one cleared line; a notice already under way when the episode opened keeps its raised order; two failing personas each get their own line and schedule; the hold's own cap and a persona no longer applied at retry time each drop with one line. The failure callback runs once, at the notice's first failed attempt, never on later retries, and a throwing callback is logged once with the notice still held and posted; a payload error (`invalid_blocks`, `msg_too_long`) logs one line with the short reason and the error's message redacted (`<redacted-token>`, `<redacted-url>`) and is dropped, not held. `forget(key)` at a teardown (b.av2 SR-6.5 as amended by b.deo SRI-307) drops the persona's pre-validation queue unposted with one count line and no Slack call (nothing logged when it held none), leaves the destination hold alone (a held notice still posts once its cause clears) and touches no other persona's queue. The persona teardown window (b.jg5 SRJ-1003, SRJ-1013; its own describe, over the harness's real recorder, `h.openTeardown` / `h.duringTeardown`, `teardownNoticeEntry` and `teardownNoticeLine`; the route's two occasions, an entry's text and both forms of its line, the notice's first line only with its Slack escapes undone, pinned as literals in one case): a notice raised in the window is one log line and one `persona-teardown-notice` entry, never posted, held or dropped, also in dry run (the window comes before the dry-run branch, no dry-run line); the survivor option (`teardownEntryClass`) writes one `persona-kill-survivor` entry and no `persona-teardown-notice` one, and outside a window changes nothing; `forget` in the window drops none raised in it, a notice queued before the window is dropped by it with its one line, and a `flush` in the window posts nothing; after the window closes the key's notices route as before (posted for an applied key, dropped with its line for a removed one), and another persona's notices in the window post as usual; an onset routed by the window marks its outage, so its all-clear after the window closed is written with the all-clear occasion and never reaches the new half's destination, while a later all-clear of that class posts as usual; a removed key's marked all-clear is written whenever it comes, naming the persona as the window did; `teardownWindowState` answers `none`, `submitted` from the submit, `open` while open and `none` once closed and settled, submits and opens counted per key; a redacted error quoted by a notice leaks no token into the line, the entry or the written file. No token in any line or post. Also in the window's describe: a CONFLICT notice built for Slack, its session name, its description and the persona's name holding `&`, `<` and `>`, is written with those characters in the entry and the line, never `&amp;`, `&lt;` or `&gt;`, and nothing posts; `PERSONA_NOTICE_SUBMIT_MUTED_OUTAGE_CLASSES` is pinned (`ad-config-malformed` only); `ad-config-malformed`'s onset for an applied key between its teardown's submit and its window makes no Slack call and writes no entry, only the muted line (its first line unescaped), and its all-clear after the teardown is written, never posted; controls: with no teardown submitted that onset posts, and for a submitted key no longer applied it is dropped as before, with no muted line and no entry, and so is its all-clear; an all-clear listing a window-routed class and a posted one, with `allClearOf`, writes the marked class's all-clear (the all-clear occasion) and posts the other class's to the destination, each rendered for its own class. Each kind of notice the notifier carries (`test.each`: a plain notice, one with a failure callback, the kill-failure alert's survivor version, an outage onset, the onset of an outage a submitted teardown mutes, an outage all-clear), raised once in A's window while B beside it has none: A's is one line and one entry of its class (read from a temporary log directory), never posted, held or given to its failure callback; B's is posted (b.jg5 SRJ-1002, SRJ-1003, SRJ-1013).
