| persona-episodes.ts | persona-episodes.test.ts | The once-per-episode latch of every per-persona notice kind (b.jg5 SRJ-1016, AC 41, AC 67), one instance per test over `createFakeClock`, a recording sink and a line capture, with kinds read from `PERSONA_EPISODE_KINDS` so a later kind joins every table; `afterEach` asserts no timer is pending and runs `assertNoLeak` over every post and line. Once per episode: the kind labels are distinct; for each kind, a post with no open episode posts nothing, the first post of an episode posts and later ones (a kept `begin` included) do not, `end` is silent and false when nothing is open, and a new episode posts again; a post with no mark uses `PERSONA_EPISODE_DEFAULT_MARK`; a sink that posts again for the same episode from inside its call posts nothing twice. Cases and marks: the same case, or no case, keeps the episode (`kept`, same view), a different case begins a new one (`new-case`, new start time, posts again), and so does a case given while the open episode has none; each of two marked texts (the stuck-launch kind) posts at most once per episode, and a new episode, by `end` then `begin` or by a new case, resets both marks. Isolation: each persona has its own latch for every kind; ending one kind (the first of `PERSONA_EPISODE_KINDS`, in one case) leaves every other kind of the persona open and posted; `forget(key)` ends only that persona's episodes, posting and logging nothing, and its later episodes post again; `forget` of a key with no episode changes nothing; `forgetAll()` ends every persona's episodes silently and later episodes post again; two instances keep separate episodes. Per-persona, per-kind counts (b.jg5 SRJ-610): `addCount` and `resetCount` answer the new and the prior count for each persona and kind, every count starting at 0, and post and log nothing; a count is independent of the open episode of its kind (`begin`, a post, a new case and `end` leave it, and adding or resetting leaves the episode open and posted); `forget(key)` clears that persona's counts of every kind and no other key's (another key's open episode kept), and `forgetAll()` and `close()` clear every persona's counts, all posting nothing. The slow-recovery tracker that drives the slow-recovery kind's count is tested in `slow-recovery.test.ts`. Start time: an episode's `startedAt` is the clock's time at `begin`, kept by a kept episode and retaken by a new one. A failing sink: one that throws or rejects is logged once, redacted (`[slack] persona-episodes: persona=<key> <kind> notice failed: …`), and the text still counts as posted. Close steps, `openKeys` and `close()`: a `test.each` over every close path (`end`, a new case in `begin`, `forget`, `forgetAll`, `close`) finds a `whenClosed` step runs exactly once when the episode closes, not while it is kept, and never again by a later close path; closing by `end`, a new case or `forget` runs only the closed episodes' steps (`forget` closing every kind of the persona), the others staying registered; `whenClosed` answers false and registers nothing with no episode of that persona and kind open, and after `close()`; a step that throws is logged once, redacted (`episode close step failed`), and every other step still runs, and with a throwing log too no close path throws; `openKeys(kind)` lists only the keys with an open episode of that kind; after `close()`, `begin` answers `closed` for every kind (with or without a case), opening, listing and posting nothing. The `tmux-unresponsive` condition's own rules on this instance: `start` answers `started`, then `continued` with the first refusal's time kept, and `end` answers `ended-after-notice` once the onset was posted, then `not-holding`; a throwing log or condition-end hook breaks neither; `forget` and `forgetAll` drop a holding condition with no hook call and no ended line; each end reason logs its text (the one pinned literal: the text for an unknown reason). Its alert check (SRJ-308 to SRJ-310), at agent-director's default threshold: `cancelAlert` with a non-terminal stop reason (`UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_RUN_FAILED`) cancels a pending check, answers true and logs one `alert check cancelled — its retry timer stopped: <reason>` line, a second cancel answering false and logging nothing, and nothing posts past the threshold; after such a cancel the episode's next `continued` start arms the check again from the first refusal with one `alert check armed again` line (a further refusal arming nothing more), the alert posting 1 ms past the threshold, or at the next clock turn when the threshold has already passed; a cancel with no stop reason cancels the check silently and the next refusal still arms it again; a `test.each` over `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_TERMINAL_STOPS` finds a terminal stop cancels the check with its line and a later refusal never arms it again (the end answering `ended`); a cancel after the alert has posted answers false, logs nothing and leaves the alert posted, and a later refusal arms nothing; a cancel with no episode open (before a start, after an end) answers false and logs nothing, leaving another persona's check pending; the re-arm mark is cleared when the episode closes (by `end` or `forget(key)`), so a new episode arms its check normally and once; once the alert has posted, the onset check at a health tick or at a retry posts no onset and logs one `onset not posted — its alert already posted` line per episode; an end after the alert alone answers `ended-after-notice` and posts the recovery, its `recovery posted` line after the ended line, and a silent end after it answers `ended-after-notice` and posts nothing. The onset while the retry timer is stopped (SRJ-305, SRJ-308), each case started and moved to the onset floor, at both onset checks (a health tick with the health check on, a retry with it off): a `test.each` over the non-terminal stops (`UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_CAPPED`, `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_NOT_UP`) with the alert check pending finds the stop holds the tick's onset back with one `onset not posted — its retry timer stopped and no refusal has re-armed it` line after the alert check's cancel line, and a later refusal re-arms the check (its `alert check armed again` line unchanged) and lets the next tick post the onset; with the health check off a retry past the floor after such a stop posts nothing, one line, until a refusal; a `test.each` over the non-terminal stops with no alert check pending (no threshold accessor), the cap and a cancel with no stop reason at all, finds the stop holds the onset back, `cancelAlert` answering false and the stop itself logging nothing, with one stopped line, until a refusal lets it post; after each of `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_TERMINAL_STOPS`, no refusal lets the onset post in the episode (one line, the alert check cancelled and never armed again, the end answering `ended`); a terminal mark is not weakened by a later non-terminal stop (the cap, or no stop reason) then a refusal; the stopped line is logged once per episode, a second stop after a refusal included, and a new episode logs it again; once the alert has posted, a later stop (the cap, a teardown) leaves the `onset not posted — its alert already posted` line in place of the stopped line; a stop after the onset has posted adds no post and no line; a stop holds back only its own persona's onset, another persona's tick posting it; and a terminal stop's mark does not reach the next episode, closed by `end` or `forget(key)`: its tick posts the onset with no stopped line, while another persona's terminal stop, left open, still holds its own onset back (its tick logs its stopped line once and posts nothing). Its failure-only lines, each logged and never thrown: `alert check not armed` (a threshold accessor answering NaN, the arm's `RangeError`), `alert check failed` (an alert fire whose post, or whose threshold read, throws), `alert check cancel failed` (a clock whose `clearTimeout` throws; an end meeting the same failure logs `episode close step failed` and still completes), `onset check at a health tick failed`, `onset check at a retry failed`, and `health-check mode read failed … — taken as on` at a tick (the onset posts) and at a retry (none posts). b.f2b's not-connected latch is separate (real `notifyPersonaNotConnected` behind `setSessionNotifier`): with it raised every kind still posts and posting raises it for no persona; `forgetNotConnectedEpisode` ends no episode here and `forget` leaves the not-connected latch raised. The unclassified-error episodes (b.jg5 SRJ-313, SRJ-1009, SRJ-1016), direct over `createUnclassifiedErrorEpisodes` with an injected threshold accessor (agent-director's defaults and AC 80's settings, through `adAlertThresholdMs`), a recording log-only route and `assertNoLeak` over its calls after each test: the kind is in `PERSONA_EPISODE_KINDS`; the alert rule, at both thresholds: the first report answers `begun` and opens the episode at its start, a report at exactly the threshold answers `continued` and posts nothing, the first strictly past it answers `alerted` and posts one alert quoting that report's classification (`unclassifiedErrorAlertText(classifyAdError(err))`), and later reports post nothing, with the started and posted lines exact (the `<met>` tail's whole seconds since the first outcome and of the threshold); the accessor is read at each check and not at the first report or once the alert is posted (raising it holds the alert, lowering it posts at the next report); a site's own classification is quoted in place of the value's, in the started line and the alert. The text (SRJ-1009): for `ErrInternal`, `ErrSchemaMismatch` and `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared` the alert quotes the classifier's reported name and rendered message; a name that carries a fake token is left out (by the classifier and by the builder given it raw) and the message still quoted; a message with a fake token and a URL is redacted in the alert and every line; an over-long message is capped at `MAX_LOGGED_MESSAGE_LENGTH`; the builder with a name alone, a message alone, both or an empty message is the bare text with only that quote added, and a quote whose fields throw reads as absent. Routing, decided when the alert is posted: a configured key's alert goes to the sink only (the lookup asked once, for that key), its quoted message escaped for Slack (`<!channel>`, `<@U…>` and `&` escaped through `escapeSlackControlCharacters`); with no lookup every key is configured; an unconfigured key's alert goes to the log-only route only, with the key and the unescaped text (`escapeForSlack: false`), and its line names `PERSONA_UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_LABEL`; a key removed after its episode began takes the log-only route; one latch for both routes (after a log-only alert the key configured again gets no post in that episode); a lookup that throws gives one redacted `configured-key lookup failed` line and the log-only route; with no log-only route installed an unconfigured key gets one `alert not routed` line, nothing posted, and the alert counts as posted; a log-only route that throws gives one redacted `log-only alert failed` line after the written line, and the alert counts as posted. Ends: `end` for `UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_END_RECOVERED`, `UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_END_ROW_LIVE`, `UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_END_CONDITION_ENDED`, `UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_END_ROW_GONE` and `UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_END_CAPPED` gives one ended line, posts nothing and leaves no state, another persona's episode staying and a later report beginning a new episode that alerts again past the threshold; `retryStopped` ends the episode silently, leaving no state, for `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_RECOVERED` (`UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_END_RECOVERED`), `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_ROW_LIVE` (`UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_END_ROW_LIVE`), `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_TMUX_UNAVAILABLE_CLEARED` and `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_TMUX_UNRESPONSIVE_ENDED` (both `UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_END_CONDITION_ENDED`) and `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_ROW_GONE` (`UNCLASSIFIED_ERROR_END_ROW_GONE`), each reason's text and ended line pinned exactly, a second call answering false and logging nothing; after a condition-end or row-gone stop ends an episode before its alert, a much later outcome begins a new episode (its started line, its start the later outcome's time) that posts nothing, routes nothing to the log-only route, and alerts only 1 ms past its own threshold; every other stop (not up, not applied, launch skipped, run failed, capped, torn down, shutdown, and a reason no module names) answers false, logs nothing and leaves the episode open with its start kept, its alert still posting past the threshold; a stop or an end with no episode open answers false and logs nothing; `forget(key)` and `forgetAll()` drop the episodes they cover with nothing posted or logged, the others kept, and a later report begins a new one that alerts again; after `close()` a report answers `closed`, opens nothing and logs nothing. Faults: an episodes member or threshold accessor that throws makes a report past the threshold answer `continued`, throw nothing, post nothing and log one redacted `report failed` line; a log that throws breaks no report (the episode begins, the alert posts and the end ends it). The kill-failure alerts (`createKillFailureAlerts`, b.jg5 SRJ-704, SRJ-1007, SRJ-1016; texts built with `src/kill-failure-alert.ts`'s builders): two ordinary alerts in one episode post once, the second answering `held`, the episode open; a `none` decision (a kill success while the row stays live) does nothing, so the episode stays open and the next ordinary alert is held; a teardown's `forget` ends the episode silently and the next ordinary alert posts again; the survivor version, with an ordinary episode open or not (`test.each`), posts once per raise through `postWithoutEpisode`, leaves the open or closed state as it was and is not the episode's alert; stopped tries (b.jg5 SRJ-702), with the last outcome's class and the cause or with neither (2 cases), give a configured persona the one line only and one no longer configured the line and one `persona-kill-failed` entry holding it with no alert text, both answering `stopped` with no post and no episode; stopped tries whose ordinary decision carries an earlier survivor-naming description, with a last description or alone (2 cases), quote it in the line, with no entry for a configured persona and the entry quoting it, with no alert text, for one no longer configured; a stopped raise's entry the log-only route refuses gives the line, then one token-safe failure line, still `stopped`; P's episode is independent of B's (the open query answers per key, and B's end leaves P's open); after the episodes' `close` either version answers `closed` and nothing is posted. A persona teardown's submit and window (b.jg5 SRJ-1003; the injected `teardownWindow` query): from the submit until the window opens, no post of any kind reaches the sink (one line each, the post still counted as posted in its episode, another persona posting), and no `postWithoutEpisode` of any kind, with options or none (true, one line, no episode read or begun); while the window is open every kind's post and `postWithoutEpisode` reach the sink as given, options only when given, with no line; `teardownWindowState` answers the query, `none` with no query or an unknown answer, and a throwing query is logged redacted and read as `none`; a destructive modify's old half with an open `tmux-unresponsive` condition after the submit posts its onset at a health tick, its alert past the threshold and its recovery to no destination, one muted line each and the condition's own lines saying not posted, while another persona's onset at the same tick posts. During a teardown, the unclassified-error alert: a key whose window opened after its episode began, still applied or not (2 cases), sends the alert once, unescaped, to the sink and never to the log-only route, its line naming `persona-teardown-notice`; a configured key submitted but not yet in its window reaches no sink and no log-only route, the muted line then its own not-posted line, counted as posted; control, no window open, a configured key's alert posts escaped and a key no longer applied writes `persona-unclassified-error` while another persona's window is open. During a teardown, the kill-failure alerts: each version raised in the window with each context the window takes, configured or not (`test.each`), is `logged` and handed to the sink with its class (`teardownEntryClass` for the survivor version), never the log-only route, no episode opened; the two contexts the window does not take keep their own log-only entry, nothing handed to the sink; a stopped retry, in the window or after the submit, gives a removed persona the stop line and one `persona-kill-failed` entry with no alert text and a configured one the line only (both built with `killFailureStoppedRetryText`, whose line, entry and default cause `KILL_FAILURE_STOP_CAUSE_DEFAULT` are pinned as literals in one case), never a `persona-teardown-notice` entry, nothing handed to the sink and neither version; after the submit, each version's destination post is muted with its not-posted line; a latched raise in the window still takes the window, none held back, and once the window closes the next ordinary alert opens its episode at the destination; another persona's alert during K's window goes to its own destination; controls with no window open: each version for a configured persona in recovery goes to its destination, and each version with the persona-teardown context writes its log-only entry and hands nothing to the sink. Over the real persona notifier's window (`makeNotifierHarness`): in a removed persona's open window an ordinary alert is one notifier line and one `persona-teardown-notice` entry with no Slack call, while a stopped retry beside it writes only its stop line and its `persona-kill-failed` entry with no alert text; control, a destructive modify's old half with no window open gets its ordinary alert at its destination through its own client, and once its window is open a stopped retry posts and writes nothing more than its stop line. In the window a kill-failure alert of each context but a start-sweep or CLI teardown kill (`persona teardown`, `old-life wait`, `stuck-launch abort`, `recovery`; each version, configured or not, one `test.each`) is handed to the sink as `killFailureAlertText` with the log-only closing, prefixed `(<context>) ` for any context but `persona teardown`, answers `logged` and opens no episode. The unclassified-error episodes' `ref` and `notConfiguredWording` hooks (b.jg5 SRJ-1007, SRJ-1014): with neither, every line names `persona=<key>` and the log-only line gives `UNCLASSIFIED_NOT_CONFIGURED_WORDING`; a `ref` hook names the key in every line (started, log-only alert, ended) and on a configured key's posted line while the route, the post and the episode stay keyed by the key; a throwing `ref` hook gives `persona=<key>`; the wording hook replaces the default in the log-only and the not-routed lines and is never used for a posted alert. A removed R with its window open takes the teardown route (one `persona-teardown-notice` line, never the log-only route) while a configured K beside it posts, escaped. |
| slow-recovery.ts | slow-recovery.test.ts | The slow dead-session recovery post (b.jg5 SRJ-610, SRJ-1010, SRJ-1016; AC 66), one `createSlowRecoveryTracker` per test over a real `createPersonaEpisodes` on `createFakeClock` with a recording sink and a line capture; `afterEach` asserts no timer is pending and runs `assertNoLeak` over every post and line. The pin, the one case that holds SRJ-1010's text literally: `slowRecoveryText('sample')`, naming the session `"slack_bot_sample"`, and `SLOW_RECOVERY_POST_THRESHOLD` is 3; a second pin holds each tracker line's wording (count, notice posted, notice not posted, count reset, episode ended, failed) and every reset reason's text in `SLOW_RECOVERY_RESET_TEXT`, the pending-probe and healthy ones included; every other case, here and in `restart.test.ts` and `conflict-latch.test.ts`, compares with `slowRecoveryText` imported from `src/slow-recovery.ts` and steps by the imported threshold. Once per episode: live notes (`noteLive`) below the threshold answer `counted` and post nothing, the note at it answers `posted` and posts the builder's text once to the persona, and later live notes post nothing while the episode is open; a dead note (`noteDead`) resets the count and ends the episode silently, so the next threshold-many live notes post once more; a direct compatibility note (`noteInstallGone`, never called by restart work), an other note (`noteOther`, for each reason: another verdict or a session already connected, `SLOW_RECOVERY_RESET_OTHER_VERDICT`; a `pending` first probe, `SLOW_RECOVERY_RESET_PENDING_PROBE`; and a `pending` re-probe, `SLOW_RECOVERY_RESET_PENDING_REPROBE`) and a healthy note (`noteHealthy`, `SLOW_RECOVERY_RESET_HEALTHY`) each reset the count with its reason's line and leave an open episode open, so threshold-many live notes after them post nothing, and with none open (one `describe.each` row per note) a reset from threshold-minus-one logs its reset line, the next live note counts 1 and posts nothing, and the post comes only on the threshold-th live note in a row; an other note given a reason that is none of its own resets with the other-verdict reason; the latch end (`endForLatch`) and the teardown's `forget` each reset the count and end the episode silently, below the threshold and after the post, and the next threshold-many live notes post once more; after shutdown's `close` the count is cleared and the note at the threshold answers `closed` and posts nothing; Q's notes count and post apart from P's. Lines and failures: a note with no count and no episode logs nothing, and every line carries the persona key only (`[slack] slow-recovery: persona=<key> …`); with an episodes member that throws (`addCount`, `resetCount`) each note (`noteHealthy` included) logs one redacted `failed` line, throws nothing and changes no state, and nothing posts; a log that throws is swallowed and the post is still made once. What the restart work notes for each reading is tested in `restart.test.ts`, the count's own rules in the episodes instance in `persona-episodes.test.ts`, and the latch end through the latch in `conflict-latch.test.ts`. A persona teardown (b.jg5 SRJ-1003; its own describe): with P's teardown submitted, the note at the threshold reaches no sink and logs the muted line (`slowRecoveryNoticeMutedLine`), not the posted one, and counts as posted, so later live notes post nothing, while Q's notice posts as usual; with P's window open the notice goes to the sink and the posted line is logged. |

| persona-destination-hold.ts | persona-destination-hold.test.ts | The hold over the real `createPersonaDestinations`, one `makeStubSlack` stub per persona (leak marker on) and a fake clock; `afterEach` cancels every persona and asserts no timer is left. Episodes: `missing_scope` on a `dm` destination's open (AC 44, the line naming `im:write`) and `not_in_channel` on a channel post each hold the persona, retried on its own schedule (5 s doubling to 300 s, never giving up, failed retries logging nothing), one start line and one cleared line per episode, and a later failure opening a new episode with its backoff back at 5 s; held notices post once each in submission order after the cause clears, and a later notice queues behind them with no attempt of its own. An attempt that started before the episode opened neither logs, moves the due time nor ends the episode; one that fails after a retry cleared the episode opens no second episode and its notice posts at once. The gate (`begin`): refused until the due time and while a retry is in flight; a gated success posts the held notices at once; a failed gated retry only moves the due time; `release()` gives an attempt back; a gated retry whose resolver throws still ends. Held-notice retries: one in flight at a time; no client at retry time waits again with no line and no Slack call; a throwing persona or client lookup waits the current backoff step with one line per run of throws, which keeps the thrown message redacted; a persona no longer applied drops its notices with one line. The `MAX_HELD_NOTICES_PER_PERSONA` cap drops the oldest, never the one being retried. `cancel` and `cancelAll` drop notices and timers with no further call and one `hold cancelled` / `shutting down` line per persona with notices held (none otherwise; named by key when the name is unknown), and an attempt in flight at cancel changes nothing when it settles. Payload errors and a refused `dm` destination open no episode. Isolation (SR-3.3): one persona's episode never delays, holds or logs for another. Secrecy: a code that is not a short identifier is logged as `unknown_error`. A destination changed in place during an open episode (`permission_prompts` moved): a failure at a destination the episode has not named logs exactly one more opening line naming it, and never again for that destination (a failure back at one already named logs nothing); a stale failure at the new destination logs nothing and moves nothing; the backoff is not reset; the cleared line names the destination the successful post went to, with the latest named cause, and no line names that destination as failing when it never failed. Fungible mode (b.deo SRI-702, SRI-906): a fungible destination channel the app is not in (`not_in_channel`, `channel_not_found`), entered by a held notice or by a re-derived prompt, is held and retried on the fake clock, its lines naming the fungible setting; each cause given the declarative setting reproduces the declarative line byte for byte |
| persona-destination.ts | persona-destination.test.ts | The destination resolver over `makeStubSlack` stubs: a channel destination makes no open and posts exactly `{channel, text}` synchronously; a `dm` destination opens the DM with its contact on its own client and posts to the returned `D…`, never the user ID, with no username or icon override (AC 29/44); the per-persona cache keyed by persona and contact (a second post reuses it, concurrent posts share one open, a changed contact opens again, two personas open their own conversations on their own clients); `forget(key)` clears only that persona's entry, and an open in flight across a `forget(key)` (an in-place update, with or without a new contact) that succeeds afterwards is never reused: the post that started it finishes there, the next post opens for the current contact, and another persona stays cached; a failed open or post (each class, and one shared by concurrent posts) is returned with its step and code, not cached and leaves other personas' entries alone, and a late failure never evicts a newer entry; `channel_not_found` on the cached DM drops, at once, the entry that post used and never one that now names another conversation; nothing rejects; a `dm` destination with DMs off or no contact is refused with no Slack call, even with a DM cached; describeDestinationFailure is token-safe: a token in the thrown message never appears, and the message is logged redacted (`<redacted-token>`, `<redacted-url>`); a platform error carries its Slack reason beside the redacted message. The channel mode, on the configuration in effect (b.deo SRI-701, SRI-702, SRI-906): `destinationSettingOf` pinned per mode (`permission_prompts` in declarative mode and with no configuration in effect, `invited.permission_prompts` in fungible mode); the one destination rule per mode as a table; in fungible mode the resolver posts to the fungible destination on the persona's own client with no line; the switch read at each attempt, never when the resolver is built, so the same persona moves with it and opens its DM once; and the `"dm"` refusal in fungible mode naming `invited.permission_prompts` |

| server.ts (session-disconnect handler, `tmux-unavailable` retry check) | session-disconnect.test.ts | `armMissingTmuxUnavailableRetry` and `_buildRestartDisconnectedPersona` called directly over recording dependencies that record every member asked, in order (b.jg5 SRJ-311, SRJ-502, SRJ-315, SRJ-1501; 19 tests). The check: a branch table (`test.each`) in which each row sets every later check to the answer that would pick another branch, so a reordered check fails it: the flag not raised answers `not-raised`, a timer armed `retry-armed`, latched (not armed or no controller) `latched`, in flight (not armed or no controller) `in-flight`, and no controller `no-controller`, each asking only the checks up to its own, logging and arming nothing; raised, not armed, not latched and nothing in flight logs the arm line, then arms the persona's timer once and answers `armed`, the armed read made once, before the latch and in-flight checks. The handler: a key that is not an applied persona (with and without `via`) gets one line naming the key and nothing else asked; while shutting down, flag raised or not, the persona line then `[slack] Skipping restart — server is shutting down (persona=<key>)` and nothing more (no flag read, no restart, no arm); the flag not raised (with and without `via`) schedules the restart in the persona's working directory; the flag raised logs, after the persona line, one line per branch (a timer armed, latched, work in flight, no retry controller) with no restart and no arm, and with no timer, not latched and nothing in flight the arm line and one arm. Expected lines are built from `via`, `renderPersonaRef` and a shared head; the branch table holds the only literal suffixes. Which production holders `server.ts` binds is pinned in server-startup-wiring.test.ts |

| atomic-write.ts | atomic-write.test.ts | durableWriteFileSync, the reload files' atomic, durable write (b.av2 SR-8.1 as amended by b.deo SRI-401), also the cron log's prune rewrite (its use there is cron-log.test.ts's): on the real file system in a temp directory (any bytes written exactly: CRLF text, invalid UTF-8, none at all; an overwrite leaving no trailing old bytes; a failure the file system raises itself and a missing parent (ENOENT) reaching the caller and changing nothing); through a recording `DurableWriteFs` seam (`makeRecordingFs`, which delegates to the real `node:fs` and can make chosen steps throw): the call order (write and fsync the temporary file, close it, rename it over the target, fsync the directory), the target keeping its old bytes until the rename, short writes continued from the next offset, a unique `.tmp` sibling that is none of the reload file names; a failure at any step up to the rename reaching the caller with the previous bytes kept, the temporary file removed and every descriptor closed (and the original error still thrown when the removal also fails); a directory open or fsync failure after the rename throwing `DurableWriteUnsyncedError` (its `path`, errno `code` and `syncError`) with the new bytes in place and nothing removed; a directory close failure after a good fsync ignored; a directory fsync failure thrown as a non-`Error` whose code is a number giving an unsynced error whose `code` is undefined. durableUnlinkSync, the durable delete of `config.json.pending` (b.av2 SR-8.1 as amended by b.deo SRI-401; b.av2 SR-8.3), on the same seam: on the real file system it removes the file and returns true, leaving its siblings; the order is unlink, then open, fsync and close of the directory, with no descriptor left open; a file already gone, a missing parent (`ENOENT`) or a parent that is a regular file (`ENOTDIR`) returns false and syncs nothing; any other unlink failure (an injected `EIO` or `EACCES` standing for every other code, a real directory's `EISDIR`, and an `Error` with no code whose message names `ENOENT`, one whose code is a number or a thrown string) reaches the caller exactly as raised, the file kept; a directory open (`EIO`) or fsync (`ENOENT`) failure after the unlink throws `DurableUnlinkUnsyncedError` keeping its errno `code` and `syncError`, and a non-`Error` fsync failure whose code is a number gives `code` undefined, each with the file already gone, every descriptor closed and no retry; a directory close failure after a good fsync is ignored. The recording seam's `codes` option picks the injected errno code per step (`EIO` by default) and `thrown` throws a given value as is. reloadFilePaths (the three SR-8.1 names (as amended by b.deo SRI-401) beside `config.json`). `atomicWriteFileSync` is covered by the stop-hook and reply-guard suites |

| reload.ts (start rules, file guard source, detection tick, the preview on the tick path, confirmation processing and the core apply), reload-apply.ts (step order) | reload.test.ts | The reload controller's start, driven end to end through `makeReloadHarness` (see Reload Tests): with no record, a valid config file is recorded byte for byte and exactly its personas are brought up (AC 71), while an invalid one (a JSON syntax error with line and column and no content, an SR-1.5 violation (as amended by b.deo SRI-103 to SRI-105), the SR-1.7 pre-persona message), a missing one or a directory refuses the start with a `Fatal: configuration error` line and no record; a record that can't be written (the write seam failing at `openSync`, `writeSync`, the temporary file's `fsyncSync` or `renameSync`) refuses with one `reload-record-write-failed` line and leaves only `config.json` in the directory; a record written but whose directory fsync fails after the rename refuses with a line saying the next start will run it, and the next start does (from the record, whatever the config file then holds); with a record, the start runs it whatever the config file holds (a persona added, removed (AC 70, start half) or modified; the config file missing, malformed or a directory, AC 13), reads credentials files as they stand (AC 72: a rotated token reaches the Slack clients, a now-invalid file breaks that persona only), arms no detection tick and makes no teardown, reconnect or in-place update; a record that can't be read or validated refuses with a `Fatal: last-applied record error` line naming the record and saying that deleting it makes the next start apply the config file (no fallback to a valid config file; once the record is deleted, the next start applies and records the config file); zero personas, from the config file or the record, start and bring nobody up (AC 54); a record-start real-path collision (the same path or a symlink) makes both personas involved fail their bring-up (a shared working directory `retrying` with `persona-directory-unusable`, a shared credentials file `broken` with `persona-credentials-invalid`) while a third comes up and is the only one launched; the SR-5.2 guard source lists the applied and current config file credentials paths, and only the applied ones once the config file is malformed; an applied outcome's `config` is the very object the start pass got; the controller's call order (`resolveStart` once; `runStartBringUp` once, only after an applied start, a concurrent second call rejected); `~` persona paths, from the record or the config file, expanding under the injected home; a dry-run start, with and without a record, following the same rules and building no Slack client; a record or config file that is not a regular file refusing the start unread (no fallback from the record), through the `configFs` seam and with a real FIFO in a child `bun` process with a time limit. Detection (b.av2 SR-8.2, SR-8.3; b.av2 SR-8.7 as amended by b.deo SRI-403, SRI-407), each check a manual tick: `startDetection` arms nothing before the start bring-up pass returns, after `stopDetection` or after a refused start, and arms one tick once; a start pass that throws has still returned, so detection arms and its first check runs; with the production driver on the run's fake clock, the first check comes exactly 5 s after `startDetection` and a later check finding the file current writes nothing; a clean start (one persona up, one retrying, one broken) does nothing over many checks (`NO_RUN_ACTIVITY`); an edit held over many ticks (a persona added, removed or changed, a server-wide setting changed) writes the pending file once, logs its preview once and makes no lifecycle call, Slack client or Slack call (AC 55), and one newline added is previewed and logged once as `no effective change`; a revert removes the pending file and logs `reload-nothing-pending` once, leaving no temporary file (AC 62); an unconfirmed destructive edit survives a restart from the record, which brings the applied set back, and the first check leaves the edit pending (AC 70); a leftover pending file is removed by the first check with one line, one placed later while nothing is pending silently, and a missing, stale or hand-edited one is rewritten with no line while its change stays pending; the preview is logged once per change of the pending state, not per check (a second edit logs once more, and so does a return to the first); an INVALID candidate while a record exists (a JSON syntax error around a pasted token, a schema error, the pre-persona shape, a real-path collision, an added persona whose key starts with an applied persona's key (the file's `prefixRelatedKeysRejection()`, also the expected start refusal from the config file and from the record, takes its reason sentence from `prefixRelatedKeysReason` over the two `personaTmuxSessionName`s, b.jg5 SRJ-1102), a missing config file or a directory at its path) puts `INVALID:` with the loader's full error, or the missing or unreadable sentence, in the pending file, logs exactly one `reload-invalid` line and no `reload-preview` line, and applies nothing; a valid edit after it logs its preview once and the record bytes clear it, and each pending file is leak-checked before the revert removes it; an unknown top-level or persona key named with a pasted token is INVALID, its name never echoed in the pending file or the log while a typo beside it is named; the fingerprint is stable over unchanged checks and follows the config bytes and each referenced credentials file (bytes, empty, missing, unreadable), never an unreferenced one. Credentials (AC 67, AC 72 pending half): a token rotated in an up persona's file is previewed as one credentials line naming the persona and path (`credentialsChangedLine`), with no `DESTRUCTIVE:` line, no reconnect before confirmation, no token in the pending file or log, and nothing else written anywhere; the held-state matrix is pending, with the exact line from `credentialsChangedLine(persona, effect, cause)`, and restoring the file with `h.saveCredentials` clears it: up, with its token rotated (reconnect), or with its file deleted, made unreadable, rewritten as malformed JSON holding a token or given a wrong-prefix bot token (the current connection kept, with the cause); retrying (Slack unreachable), with its token rotated or its file deleted (retries with the new content, with the cause for the deleted file); broken by Slack refusing its token, re-saved with only a newline added (brought up) or rewritten as malformed JSON holding a token (stays broken, with the cause); broken by a missing file, made unreadable, and broken by an unreadable file, deleted (stays broken, with the cause); each cause is the credentials check's token-free wording over the bytes the tick read (a missing file then created and invalid content then fixed are the AC 65 cases below); an unreferenced file is never compared; two personas broken by a shared credentials file each hold the digest of their own path's bytes, so re-saving the file with different bytes is a pending credentials change for them, though it can't bring either up while the collision stands (Director decision 1 reversed); a changed `credentials_file` path is pending by the config bytes only, its new file never compared with the old one's held content; the next start from the record applies a pending rotation and its first check removes the leftover pending file. A real FIFO at the config or a referenced credentials path keeps each check bounded and counts as unreadable: one pending-file write and one line, with no lifecycle call, Slack client or Slack call (child process through `runHarnessInChild`, 10 s bound). In dry run no credentials file is compared (`run.tickCredentialsReads` stays empty) and the fingerprint covers the config file alone: a credentials file rotated, then made malformed, deleted or a directory, gives no credentials line, no cause and no read. The preview on the tick path (b.av2 SR-8.4 as amended by b.deo SRI-803, SRI-804; b.av2 SR-10.3 as amended by b.deo SRI-901 to SRI-906; its wording is pinned in reload-preview.test.ts): removing B and moving C's `working_directory` gives a `DESTRUCTIVE:` line for each (AC 56), logged once as `reload-preview` lines equal to the file's body, the header ending with the pending-file suffix, and nothing applied over many ticks; a restart from the record with an unconfirmed edit brings back every recorded persona with no operator step, and its first check previews the edit (AC 13, tick path); a persona broken by a missing credentials file that is then created, or by invalid content that is then fixed, is previewed as brought up, not reconnected, leak-checked while the pending file exists, and stays `broken` before confirmation, and putting the broken file back removes the pending file (AC 65, preview half); the tick compares real paths, so a working directory rewritten through a symlink to the same directory previews `no effective change` with no `DESTRUCTIVE:` line; when only a fact changes (an added persona's missing working directory created) the file is rewritten with no log line and the fingerprint is unchanged; a preview or `reload-invalid` line logged while the pending file can't be written has no pending-file suffix, and the write that succeeds later logs nothing more; a fact the tick can't gather (the bring-up state lookup throwing through `opts.bringUpState`) logs one `reload: cannot check` line, pinned by prefix, persona and closing clause, is previewed as "could not be checked", never as an answer, while the pass still writes and logs the preview; a later pass that gathers every fact rewrites the file with no log line and re-arms that line, so a later failure logs it once more; with the state unknown and the new content malformed, the line still gives the cause (`credentialsChangedLine(persona, 'unchecked', cause)`); an added persona is previewed with every cause that stops it coming up (credentials missing, invalid or a directory; working directory missing or a plain file; both), with a no-cause control; bug b.g57: a `claude_config_dir` that is, or sits under, a symlink pointing to nothing adds `claude_config_dir cannot be resolved to a real path (ENOENT: a symlink on its path points to nothing)` after the credentials and working-directory causes (all three listed together), one not created yet under an existing ancestor adds nothing, and the preview never creates the directory; once the link's target exists, the next pass previews the persona as able to come up, with no second preview log; a persona's own `claude_config_dir` changed to a symlink pointing to nothing, or a directory under one, is previewed with `; but at that launch it cannot come up: <problem>` on its next-launch line (a directory not created yet under an existing ancestor gets no warning), the preview creating nothing, and one rename still applies it (`reload-applied`, nothing pending); a changed top-level `claude_config_dir` pointing to nothing ends its settings line with the warning naming both inheritors; once a changed directory's link target exists the next pass drops the warning, with no second preview log; in dry run an added persona is never judged by its credentials (no credentials file read, only a working-directory cause); and unchanged or modified personas are not probed: each check reads each referenced credentials file once and checks only the added persona's working directory (`run.tickCredentialsReads`, `run.tickDirectoryChecks`). Pending-file write and delete failures (`h.failWrites`, `h.failRemoves`) are logged once per episode and retried at each check, and the next success ends the episode, so a later failure in the same pending state is logged again; a write whose directory sync fails is written again at the next check; a delete whose directory sync fails counts as removed (`unsynced`), is logged once, followed by `reload-nothing-pending`, and never retried; each change of the pending state opens a new episode. A failure line is pinned by its `[slack] reload:` prefix, the pending path and the errno code (`fileFailureLine`), not its whole wording. A detection check that throws (the held-digest lookup failing through `opts.heldCredentialsDigest`) is caught by the controller: one `detection check failed` line over many checks, nothing written, and the next good check writes the pending file. Every detection test ends asserting no Slack post and `assertNoLeak` per run. Confirmation and the core apply (b.av2 SR-8.5; b.av2 SR-8.6 as amended by b.deo SRI-805; `confirmation and apply`): one rename of the pending file (`h.confirm()`, AC 73) applies a removal or an addition at the next check: the record becomes the config bytes, the applied set follows (`run.appliedKeys()`, `run.appliedConfigs`), the confirmation is deleted before the record is written and the server told, nothing is left pending and one `reload-applied` line is logged; afterwards detection compares with the new applied set; a confirmation made while the server was down is applied by the first check after a start from the record; a confirmed INVALID candidate logs exactly one `reload-invalid` line (AC 63); a confirmation made stale by a later edit applies nothing, is logged once as `reload-stale-confirmation` and the pending file is rewritten on that check (AC 69, AC 56), and a revert needs a fresh rename; a directory at the apply path is logged once as stale and once as undeletable over many checks; a confirmation that cannot be deleted (`h.failRemoves`) is applied once, then ignored with its delete retried silently until its content changes, and an undeletable stale one still applies nothing once the config matches it; a consumed confirmation whose change stays pending rewrites the pending file on the same check with no second preview; a confirmed removal writes the retired-key record before the last-applied record (`run.writes`, the store's `recorded` line before `reload-applied`; what step 1 records and restores is reload-apply.test.ts's); a last-applied write failing at apply (before the rename, or at the directory sync with the previous record written back) applies nothing, logs one `reload-record-write-failed` line, removes the retired-key record step 1 wrote (the empty record held before the apply, the store's `restored` line) and rewrites the pending file on the same check (AC 56); a server-wide-only change logs `reload-applied` with no lifecycle call; an untouched persona gets no lifecycle call, Slack client or `auth.test` from an apply of another change; steps 2–6 run after step 1, in order, each settled before the next, and a failing step is logged while the later ones still run (`opts.applySteps`); the default step 3 body, `lifecycleApplySlots` called directly with recording members and no run, gives each persona modified in place exactly one `updateInPlace` call (its first entry when the list names it twice) and runs no other member; the step inputs are split by class (`recordingSlots` records step 2's `removed` and `destructiveOld`, step 4's `credentials` and `credentialsBroken`, step 6's `added` and `destructiveNew`): a destructive modify is only its two halves (old and new declaration) and a rotation of a persona broken by its credentials only `credentialsBroken`; what the default step bodies do to the personas is pinned in `tests/reload-apply.test.ts`; a confirmed no-op whose config directories changed lexically runs only the template refresh and logs `reload-noop`. Confirmed credentials changes end to end (AC 65, b.av2 SR-6.4, SR-8.3): a persona credentials-broken at start (its file missing, locally invalid, or its token refused by Slack; one row each over the recorder's stand-ins and over the real composition) is previewed as brought up once a good file is placed, stays broken over many ticks and a long clock advance, and after one rename comes up with the new tokens, with no restart and nothing done to the healthy persona; a persona Slack refused at start stays broken with no `auth.test` retry after its app is fixed on Slack's side, until a byte-only re-save of its file is confirmed; a Slack-unreachable persona whose confirmed new file is unusable keeps retrying with its current content, the change pending, and a good file confirmed then is what its next retry comes up with. The 64 KiB cap on the tick path (`h.writeOversized`): an oversized config file logs one `reload-invalid` line and previews INVALID naming the limit; an oversized referenced credentials file is previewed as an unusable credentials change and never leaks; a persona whose credentials file is oversized at the start is broken, holds the unreadable marker and leaves nothing pending. Token secrecy on the reload path (b.av2 SR-10.3 as amended by b.deo SRI-901 to SRI-906; AC 20): a fake token pasted under a `bot_token` or `app_token` key (in a persona entry or at the top level) is, on a running server, an INVALID candidate naming the key and never the value, and its confirmation applies nothing; a start from `config.json` or from the record with it refuses the same way. A throw from the server's `onApplied` is logged with its message redacted and the apply goes on (steps 2–6 run, `reload-applied` is logged). A token-shaped persona name (b.av2 SR-1.2 as amended by b.deo SRI-102, SRI-202: no format rule) is an ordinary name: renaming a persona to one (a removal plus an addition) or adding one is a valid candidate, previewed with the name as written and applied by one confirmation, and a start from the config file or the record with one starts. The preview is asserted line for line (the header with its counts, a removed line for a rename, then the added line with the name as written). The name comes from `fakeToken`, so those runs' artifacts are asserted to hold it; their leak check masks the name and its key with `withoutName` and runs `assertNoLeak` over the rest (`run.captured()` and the server-side files, the credentials files the runs read included), before and after the confirmation. Every case leak-checks `run.captured()` while the pending file exists, and the operator's own files (the config file, credentials files, a hand-edited record) are left out of the check. A confirmed removal also logs apply step 1's old-life hold began line between the store's `recorded` line and `reload-applied`, matched by `OLD_LIFE_HOLD_LOG_PREFIX` only (its wording is old-life-wait.test.ts's and which holds begin reload-apply.test.ts's). The channel mode: a start without a record judges the configuration file by the mode its own switch picks, and the tick judges a candidate by the mode its switch turns on, so a candidate breaking the section it puts in force previews INVALID naming the persona and the setting and its confirmation applies nothing, while a fault only in the section it leaves out is a valid candidate (b.deo SRI-105); an edit of the switch or of `invited` is held over many checks, with delivery, posting scope, `set_channel_delivery` and the notice destination unchanged until it is confirmed (b.deo SRI-801); the sentinel pasted into the switch or into `invited.permission_prompts` is an INVALID candidate naming the setting only, reaching no preview, pending file or log line (b.deo SRI-907). |

Success (plain, a `~/` target landing under the temp HOME, a path with spaces and a single quote, and the shell started with `-a -x`): exit 0, both `ok` lines and the `Wrote … with mode 0600` line; a strict-JSON file with exactly `bot_token` and `app_token` that `checkPersonaCredentials` accepts, already 0600 before `chmod` ran despite `umask 000`, written by `mkdir`, `mktemp`, `chmod`, `mv` in that order with no temp file left; exactly two curl calls, `auth.test` with the bot token then `apps.connections.open` with the app token, each argv with `-q` first, `--config -` and the method URL last, the bearer header only on stdin. With `-a -x` no token reaches curl's environment or a trace. The wizard's line end to end, through the real CLI (`bun src/cli.ts`, as the installed binary, on the sandbox PATH with a `bash` wrapper, both recording their argv) over a temp `SLACK_STATE_DIR` whose `config.json` declares the persona with a `~/` `credentials_file`: the line filled in as the skill says (a name that is its own key, pasted with trailing spaces; a name with a space, single-quoted) prints `Credentials file of persona …: <path>`, runs `bash <script> <expanded path>` and writes the 0600 file, and the recorded argv (the command, the bash hand-off) carry no token; a persona `config.json` doesn't declare exits 1 naming the declared ones, before any prompt, with no script, curl call or file. A `~/.curlrc` asking for a trace is ignored (real curl, when installed, behind the closed proxies: both keys `could not reach Slack`, no trace file). curl missing from PATH: exit 1 with the install message before any prompt, stdin left unread, no program run. Local rejections before any Slack call (per key: empty, no prefix, the other key's prefix, the prefix alone, a dot, a space; both keys failing; no input at all): exit 1, each key's rule named, no curl call, no wrapped program, no directory. Slack validation, both checks always made (exit 1, reported per key, no file): `ok:false` from either or both, `ok:false` with a nested `"ok":true`, a body whose leading field is not `"ok":true`, curl unable to connect (exit 6), an empty body, a timeout after part of an ok body, `ok:false` with no error code, and an error field echoing the token (reported as `unexpected response`); a reply passes only when its leading field is `"ok":true`. Existing file: `no`, `YES` or no answer leaves it byte for byte at mode 0644 with no token prompt; `yes` replaces it (0600, new values); `yes` with a refused token leaves it. Writing: `mkdir`, `mktemp`, `chmod` or `mv` failing, or the written file empty after the rename, exits 1 with the cannot-create or cannot-write line, the target unchanged and no temp file left; the target changing before the rename (a directory or file appearing, the file vanishing or becoming a symlink or a directory) exits 1 with `changed; nothing written`, no `mv` and no temp file left; a directory appearing at the target as `mv` runs (where there was nothing, or in place of the existing file) exits 1 with `changed; nothing written`, the directory empty and no temp file left. Bad paths (relative, `~other/`, a trailing slash, an existing directory, `~` alone): exit 2 with one `credentials file:` line, no prompt, nothing done. Each session's output and typescript pass `assertNoLeak`. zsh rows cover success, success with `-a -x`, a local rejection, writing and bad paths; they skip when zsh is not installed. Every run's stdout, stderr, wrapped programs' argv and curl's argv and environment pass `assertNoLeak` (tokens from `fakeToken`, so they carry `LEAK_SENTINEL`); curl's stdin carries a token by design and is compared, not leak-checked. Controls pin these cases: moving the step after "Add a persona" fails the order case, cutting each phrase fails its case, a phrase placed only just outside its scope fails (at least one per scope helper), and the fungible example check fails with a declarative key added or `invited` replaced. `## Credentials command` says the persona is declared in `config.json` first and names `auth.test`, `apps.connections.open`, mode 0600 and the packaged `scripts/write-credentials.sh` (the line itself is credentials-command.test.ts's). A control table pins the check: a negation after the phrasing, in the list item before or in the wrapped line before is still flagged; the Constraints and credentials-command prohibitions are not. With the passwd home known: the passwd home, with a trailing `/`, spelled through `..`, a path under it (also through `..`), a passwd home given with a trailing `/`, and a home under a temp directory that lies inside the passwd home (the temp-directory rules do not apply); a symlink to the passwd home or a path under it, and a path under the target of a passwd home that is a symlink; a home holding `.config/claude-slack-channel-bots` or `.claude/channels/slack`; a `.claude` that is the passwd home or leads into its symlink target, and a `.config/agent-director` symlink into it (`launch-home-derived-path-in-real-home`); a home holding `.config/agent-director` or only the standard install path. Accepted: a clean home, a `.claude` leading elsewhere, a sibling sharing the passwd home's name as a prefix, and a clean home with a temp directory of `/` (the temp-directory rules apply only with no passwd home). With no passwd home (none, or a relative or empty one, taken as none) the guard fails closed: the would-be real home, a home outside the temp directory (even one holding Slack state), the temp directory itself (with a trailing `/` too), a sibling sharing its name as a prefix, a path leaving it through `..`, a home under it that is a symlink out of it, and a home under the lexical form of a temp directory that is a symlink are `launch-home-not-under-temp-dir`; a home under the resolved form, a clean home under it and a `.claude` leading elsewhere under it are accepted; Slack state, a `.claude` leading into the would-be real home or to the temp directory itself (`launch-home-derived-path-not-under-temp-dir`) and `.config/agent-director` are still refused. The `/home` limit: with no passwd home and the temp directory `/home`, the empty would-be real home is accepted, and refused only when it holds Slack state or `.config/agent-director`. Every path in `LAUNCH_HOME_DERIVED_PATHS` has its own row resolving into the passwd home and its own row resolving out of the temp directory with no passwd home. The order in which rules win: the real-home and temp-directory rules over Slack state and an install, Slack state over a derived-path link and an install, a derived-path link over an install. Each row asserts that nothing is probed for a home refused by path text alone, and that the probe is only ever asked about the launch home, the entries the rules name under it and the passwd home (with none, the temp directory). `LIVE_LAUNCH_HOME_PROBE` on fixtures beside a fake real home that holds both default state directories (`DEFAULT_STATE_DIR_PATHS`), passed as the passwd home: symlinked homes and a symlinked passwd home; `.claude` and `.claude/channels` symlinks into the fake real home's Slack state, and a dangling Slack-state symlink (Slack state); `.claude`, `.claude/channels` and `.claude/projects` symlinks into the fake real home, live, relative and dangling, a chain of dangling absolute and relative links, and a `.claude` outside it whose `projects` links back in (derived path in the real home); a dangling symlink into it at each derived path but the Slack state directory, `.config/agent-director` and the paths under it included (derived path, over the install rule); symlink loops (a `.claude` loop counts as Slack state, a `.claude/projects` loop is accepted, a `.config/agent-director` loop is an install); a `.config/agent-director` directory and dangling symlink; an empty home, a real `.claude` with settings and no Slack state, and a `.claude` leading elsewhere (accepted). Then with no passwd home, against the OS temp directory: an empty home under the case's root accepted; the temp directory itself and a dangling link out of it refused as not under it; Slack state; a `.claude` dangling outside it and a `.claude/projects` linked to the temp directory itself (derived path not under it); `.config/agent-director`. Each fixture tree is unchanged afterwards (`treeSnapshot`); an entry that cannot be looked at counts as Slack state (skipped as root). The guard in a `bun test` child, started from the repository root, `tests/` and `tests/integration/` (each `bunfig.toml`), each child's `env` a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call with `TMPDIR` under the case's root, a fake home under that `TMPDIR` (so the guard accepts it with or without a passwd entry) and fake tokens: under a fake home holding either default state directory it exits with `LAUNCH_HOME_REFUSED_EXIT_CODE` and writes only `launchHomeRefusalMessage`'s line, the one-file fixture never loads, the fake home is unchanged and no directory is made in `TMPDIR`; under an empty fake home it loads the fixture, which sees `homedir()` as the fake home and HOME as the preload's own directory under `TMPDIR`. This run: `launchTimeHomes()` holds `os.homedir()`, and the `homedir()`-derived defaults (`resolveServerStateDir` with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` unset, `resolveClaudeConfigDir()`, `resolveJsonlRoots` with no persona, `expandTilde('~')`, `resolveDefaultCronTablePath` with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` unset, `DEFAULT_STATE_DB_PATH`, `DEFAULT_STORE_PATH` expanded) resolve under `os.homedir()` and outside the passwd home, by path text and resolved (with no passwd entry, strictly under the OS temp directory). `scripts/preflight.sh`'s SR-2.3 block (its own describe, 'scripts/preflight.sh SR-2.3: bun test runs under a scratch HOME'; `preflight-ad-check.test.ts` covers SR-2.5 only), read from the script and run alone under bash with a stub `bun` that records each call, the Slack token variables (fakes), `CSCB_PERSONA` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set in its environment: a passing suite runs `bun test` once under a new HOME directory in `TMPDIR`, with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` at `<HOME>/state` and no Slack token variable, `CSCB_PERSONA` or `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, then the typecheck, and removes the directory; a failing suite exits 13 with the SR-2.3 test line, runs no typecheck and still removes it; a suite the guard refused to start (exit `LAUNCH_HOME_REFUSED_EXIT_CODE`) exits 13 with the SR-2.3 refusal line, which names the guard's `host-safety preload: refusing to start` line and the `TMPDIR` cause and not the test line, runs no typecheck and still removes it; a failing `mktemp -d` exits 13 with the SR-2.3 `mktemp` line and never runs `bun`. Its limit: the client decodes a binary's error envelope through an internal map built from its bundled error-name catalog, and the client exports neither, so a client whose catalog lacked a Phase-1-only name would deliver that error from a real binary as `ErrUnknownErrorName` and the check would still pass; only a real binary run proves that path. |

Every version is built from `PHASE1_FLOOR_VERSION`'s parts or imported from the versions helper, never typed. The floor constant: strict `major.minor.patch` with no pre-release suffix, and equal to the released client's own minimum (`CLIENT_MIN_VERSION`). `meetsPhase1Floor` passes the floor, its release candidate and a later patch, minor and major release and each one's candidate, and the floor's `rc.2` and `rc.10` (a `<floor>-rc.N` counts as the floor; a self-check shows `rc.10` sorts below `rc.2` as a string, above it in SemVer, and both below the floor in SemVer); refuses `OLD_AD_VERSION`, `DEV_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION`, the version just below the floor and that version's candidate; compares numerically, not as a string (each floor part replaced by 9 and by the next power of ten, keeping the versions whose string order is the opposite of their numeric order against the floor, the expected answer taken from `semver`, so both a refused and a passing version are generated when the floor allows; with a guard that the floor has at least one); and fails closed on a leading-`v` form, a `+build` form, a trailing newline, `DEV_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION` and the empty string. The helper: `PHASE1_RC_VERSION` is the floor's first release candidate (strict SemVer, the floor's major.minor.patch, pre-release `rc.1`, no build part), which guards the helper's shape only, since the helper builds it from the floor (`tests/ci-live-docker.test.ts` checks that the release the images pin, `AD_VERSION`, is the floor itself, a plain release `package.json` pins); `OLD_AD_VERSION` is below the released client minimum; a resolved client at that version is a defensive injection refused by the own floor; `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` equals the installed client's `version-floor.json` value; `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE_VERSION` is a strict SemVer release (no pre-release or build part); and a scan of every `tests/` TypeScript file (`node_modules` skipped), comments stripped (`stripComments`), finds its value in the code of the helper and of no other file (not vacuous: the scan reads the helper and finds it there), and the same scan of every `src/` TypeScript file finds it in none (not vacuous: it reads `ad-version-gate.ts` and finds `PHASE1_FLOOR_VERSION` there). The runtime re-check of the host binary (b.jg5 SRJ-204 to SRJ-207, SRJ-209; AC 22 and AC 23's first half), every label, interval, time limit, runbook title, log-line prefix and the runtime phrase imported from `src/`. Each case builds its own re-check with the file's local factory `makeRecheckRig`: one `createFakeClock()` clock, a `makeStubResolveSystemBinary` stub given the case's `outcomes` (or a replacement resolve, for an answer the test settles by hand or a synchronous throw), capturing record, stop, log and tick hooks that write one ordered event list, and a capturing version-changed listener that keeps each signal as a `[previous, new]` pair; a case may add its own tick and version-changed listeners, which run after the rig's; there is no health check, so the re-check's own timer is the only one armed. The file's other local helpers: `deferredAnswer` (a resolve answer the test settles by hand), `trackAnswers` (each trigger waiter's answer as it settles, `undefined` until then, so a case can tell an answer given at once from one that waits on the call), `NOT_RUNNING` (the not-running answer, built from the exported kind), `WELL_PAST_INTERVAL_AND_LIMIT` (a clock advance well past both the interval and the time limit, from the exported constants), `expectNothingAfterTheCall` (the rig's only event is its one call: no record, stop, log line, tick or version change, and no timer), `expectNextRecheckArmed`, `expectStoppedOnce`, `expectCouldNotRunLineNaming` (the line is `buildAdVersionRecheckCouldNotRunLine` of the error's `errName` and never carries its message) and `installDeps` (deps for the module-level install over a recording stub resolve). `afterEach` disposes every rig, resets the module's installed re-check, its tick and version-changed listeners and the client singleton, and removes the temp directories. Schedule: `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS` is 120 s and `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_TIME_LIMIT_MS` is positive and shorter; building the re-check arms and calls nothing; `start` calls nothing at once and arms one timer at the interval; nothing is called just under the interval and one call at it; N intervals give N calls, one timer pending after each; a second `start` is a logged no-op. An injected resolved version that passes CSCB's numeric check (the floor, its release candidate, a later release; native admission rejects the floor's release candidate first): no record or stop, it becomes the last version seen, the next call one interval later. The admitted development placeholder, or defensive injected old/malformed resolved versions (`OLD_AD_VERSION`, `DEV_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION`): record then stop, each once, label `ad-below-phase1-floor`, `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_STOP_EXIT_CODE` (non-zero), no timer left; the message names the version found, the floor, the binary path, `RUNTIME_RECHECK_PHRASE`, `PHASE1_REQUIRED_PHRASE` and the debug skill (`DEBUG_SKILL_RUNTIME_STOP_POINTER`, `DEBUG_SKILL_PATH`), and holds neither `PHASE1_RUNBOOK_SECTION_TITLE` nor `PHASE1_SWITCH_OVER_INSTRUCTION`, no line of `renderInstallSkillInstructions()`, no "switch-over runbook", "install agent-director" or "upgrade agent-director" and none of the `UPGRADE_FORMS` (`expectRuntimeEntryPointsToDebugSkill`); the last version seen is unchanged. The client's too-old error: the same single stop with label `ad-system-install-too-old`, the message naming the error's two versions, the floor, the path and the phrase, equal to the builder's runtime form and ending with the debug-skill pointer, with no install-skill block, checked the same way; it is recognised by its `errName`, so a plain object carrying it is decided the same way. A re-check that cannot run (unreachable for a timed-out probe or an unparseable version, not found, Bun too old, a plain error, a synchronous throw): no record, no stop, the last version seen unchanged, one timer re-armed, and the next interval calls again; a rejection with a value the description cannot name (a thrown string carrying a fake token, `null`, a plain object with no name, an error whose `errName` is token-shaped) is could-not-run the same way: no record, no stop, one timer re-armed, and one line starting with `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_COULD_NOT_RUN_LOG_PREFIX` with every credential redacted (`assertNoLeak`). The stop: after a pass then a below-floor answer, later advances call nothing and record and stop run no second time, and no tick runs on the stopping re-check; a throwing record hook is logged and the server still stops once; the real `recordStartupError` into a temp log directory writes one entry with the floor label, both versions, the path and the phrase; after either stop, an installed stub client logs no verb. Time limit: a call that never settles becomes could-not-run at the limit, no second call overlaps it, and the next interval still calls; a below-floor answer arriving after the limit is ignored; an answer inside the limit clears the limit timer. Dispose: before the first tick and after one, no further call or tick runs after any advance, dispose is idempotent and a later `start` arms nothing; after a dispose before `start`, `start` is a logged no-op; during a timed or a triggered call in flight, any trigger waiter is answered not-running before the call settles and the later below-floor answer records, stops, logs, ticks and signals nothing, with no timer left; with a trigger joined to a timed re-check, or two triggers, waiting on a call that never settles, dispose answers every waiter not-running at once, with no clock advance, nothing is re-armed, fired, logged, ticked or signalled, even well past the interval and the limit, and a later trigger answers not-running without a call. Tick hook: a listener runs once per timed re-check after it finishes (on a pass and on could-not-run, the time limit included) with the next re-check already armed (the stop and dispose cases above show it runs on neither); a listener that throws or rejects is logged once per tick, the listeners after it still run and the next re-check is armed. Module level: `installAdVersionRecheck` starts the re-check, and a second install is a logged no-op returning the same handle and arming nothing; `disposeAdVersionRecheck` clears the timer, is idempotent and is a no-op with nothing installed, `triggerAdVersionRecheck` after it answers not-running and calls nothing, and an install after it is a logged no-op returning nothing; a listener registered with `onAdVersionRecheckTick`, before or after install, runs on each tick, and its unsubscribe removes only that listener. The could-not-run line (SRJ-206, AC 22), one per run of could-not-run results: three failures in a row log one line, at the first, naming the error's `errName` and never its message; a pass ends the run and writes none, and the next failure logs a second line naming its own error; a later below-floor answer then records once and stops non-zero with no further line; mixed failure kinds in one run (unreachable, a plain error, not found, the time limit) log one line, starting with `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_COULD_NOT_RUN_LOG_PREFIX` and naming the first error's `errName` and reason, with every credential redacted (`assertNoLeak`) and never the error's message; an unreachable error whose binary path holds `\r`, `\n`, U+2028 and U+2029 gives a description (from `decideAdVersionRecheckOutcome`) and a line holding none of them, each still naming every visible part of the path; passes alone write no line. Trigger (SRJ-204, AC 23's first half): `trigger()` calls at once with no clock advance, and a passing answer comes back as the pass outcome with no tick, record, stop, line or version change and the timed re-check's timer untouched; below the floor and the client's too-old error each give one record and one non-zero stop with no clock advance, the answer is the stop outcome carrying the runtime phrase, and a later trigger answers not-running and calls nothing; a triggered could-not-run joins the current run (no second line) and a triggered pass ends it, so the next failure logs again; the first failure after `start`, when triggered, logs the line; two triggers during a triggered call or a timed call make no second call and every waiter gets the one outcome, acted on once (one version change, and a tick only for the timed call); a timed re-check coming due during a triggered call makes no second call (only the limit timer is pending), then arms the next re-check and runs the tick listeners; before `start` and after dispose, a trigger answers not-running, calls nothing and leaves no timer (dispose during a triggered call is under Dispose).
