A version that cannot be read (SRJ-202's strict parse): `ErrSystemInstallUnreachable` for a timed-out probe and for an unparseable version fails version unreadable, carrying the path, the error's reason as the failure's `reason` and in its detail; an unreachable value (recognised by `errName`) with no reason, an empty, non-string, upper-case, line-broken or over-64-character reason, a token-bearing reason or one carrying a fake token and URL fails version unreadable with reason `UNREACHABLE_REASON_UNKNOWN`, carrying the path and no credential (`assertNoLeak`); `UNREACHABLE_REASON_UNKNOWN` is none of `UNREACHABLE_REASONS` (`tests/test-helpers/install-check-fixtures.ts`) and `UNREACHABLE_REASON_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION` is one of them; a resolved `DEV_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION`, a leading-`v` form of the floor and of `OLD_AD_VERSION`, a `+build` form of the floor and the empty string fail version unreadable with reason `UNREACHABLE_REASON_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION`, carrying the path (and the version, when not empty), never a pass; a resolved value with no version, a numeric version or a `null` version fails the same way, with the same reason. Any other thrown value fails other, never a pass: `ErrBunVersionTooOld`, naming its `errName`; a plain error, a thrown string, number, `null` and `undefined`, and a plain object with no name; and a value whose `errName` getter throws, read as no name without the decision throwing. The minimum is an input: against `MIN_ABOVE_OLD`, `OLD_AD_VERSION` fails below that minimum while `DEV_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION` still passes below the floor; against a later minor release, the floor's release candidate fails below it; a leading-`v` form of the client minimum or `DEV_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION` as the minimum fails client minimum unreadable, never a pass. Recognition by name: for each of `ErrSystemInstallNotFound`, `ErrSystemInstallTooOld`, `ErrSystemInstallUnreachable` and `ErrBunVersionTooOld`, a plain object built by the local `byNameOnly` (not an `Error`, carrying only the `errName` and the install fields) gives the same outcome as the class-built error; `errName` wins over `name` (a value whose `errName` names `ErrBunVersionTooOld` and whose `name` names not-found fails other). No leak: `assertNoLeak` finds no credential in the outcome for a pass and a below-floor pass with token-bearing paths, a token-bearing unparseable version, a token-bearing client minimum, `ErrSystemInstallTooOld` with a token-bearing path, `ErrSystemInstallUnreachable` with a token-bearing path and diagnostic, and an error whose `errName` is token-shaped. `settleHostVersionCall`: a resolved call settles `resolved` carrying the same value object; a rejection and a synchronous throw each settle `rejected` carrying the thrown value, and the settle never rejects; it makes exactly one resolver call (a recording `makeStubResolveSystemBinary`). `redactToOneLine`: a fake token and URL, a CRLF with a run of line feeds, U+2028 and U+2029, and a fake token on one line with a URL on the next each give the exact expected single line (redactions as `REDACTED_SENTINEL_TAIL` and `REDACTED_TOKEN_PLACEHOLDER`, each break run one space), holding no line break and no credential; plain one-line text is unchanged. `compareAdVersions`, following the client's own order: `CLIENT_DEV_SENTINEL_VERSION` is the client's `DEV_SENTINEL_VERSION`; the sentinel ranks above `OLD_AD_VERSION`, the client minimum, the floor, its release candidate and a later major release, and equals itself; a release ranks above its own pre-release; a version equals itself; ordered pairs (including `BELOW_CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` against the client minimum) give -1 one way and 1 the other; the `LEXICAL_TRAPS` versions compare against the floor numerically, as `semver` does; two pre-release tags compare as strings; a leading-`v` form, a `+build` form, `DEV_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION` or the empty string on either side answers `null`, against the sentinel too. `buildPhase1HostNote`, built by the local `noteFor` from a below-floor outcome: for the defensive old-version note-builder input and the admitted `DEV_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION` the note names the found version, the path, the floor, `PHASE1_HOST_NOTE_PHRASE` and `PHASE1_RUNBOOK_SECTION_TITLE`, and holds no upgrade instruction, no `package.json`, no `UPGRADE_FORMS` match and no link. A source audit of the import-cycle fix: `src/ad-version-gate.ts` names `./install-check.ts` in no import, re-export, dynamic import or require, and takes `AD_BELOW_PHASE1_FLOOR` and `AD_SYSTEM_INSTALL_TOO_OLD` from `./install-check-labels.ts` (`importSource`); `src/install-check-labels.ts` exports constants and imports nothing; it exports at least one label, and `src/install-check.ts` re-exports every one of them with the same value (one `test.each` case per label). The `ErrInvalidFlags` hold (b.jg5 SRJ-207, SRJ-1008, SRJ-1016, SRJ-204, SRJ-205; `src/invalid-flags-hold.ts`, over the harness's fake clock and `makeStubResolveSystemBinary`, every version from `tests/test-helpers/agent-director-versions.ts`): `INVALID_FLAGS_HOLD_ALERT_TEXT` is SRJ-1008's text byte for byte, carries no secret and names no session-ending command; the hold decision (`decideInvalidFlagsHold`): a pass, a could-not-run (a trigger whose resolve throws at once) and a not-running answer hold, under the pass's version or the last version seen, and a stop does not; `lastAdVersionSeen`: none with nothing installed, the baseline before any pass, a passing new version after it, unchanged by the same version and by a could-not-run, none after dispose and once a stop ended the re-check, and `isRunning` false before start, true from start, false after dispose or a stop; the set reaction: holding P stops its timer, then posts the alert once at P; a second set while held changes nothing; Q's hold posts its own; ending P and holding it again posts one new alert; after the episodes close a set posts nothing but still stops the timer; each failing step is isolated, a `test.each` of 9 rows over P's set, alert, version-change end and retry with Q beside it (the retry timer stop throwing, the alert step throwing, ending its hold episode on a version change throwing, its retry at once throwing and rejecting, a set observer throwing and rejecting, an end observer throwing and rejecting): exactly one `failed` line, naming that step and redacted (`assertNoLeak`), and every other step of P and all of Q's still run (both stopped, ended with their end lines, retried with their retry lines, Q's alert posted, P's unless the alert step failed, P's hold episode left open only when ending it failed), no timer left; a version change whose ending of the holds throws (`versionChanged` throwing) logs one redacted `ending the holds on a version change failed` line, ends nothing and retries nothing; a version that is not a short version string is logged as `unreadable`; the version-changed end: a timed re-check passing with the same version keeps every hold, a could-not-run keeps them, and a pass with a different version ends every hold, posts nothing and retries each applied persona at once, at the re-check's clock time; a triggered re-check with a different version ends them too; a hold begun right after the immediate re-check that reported a new version stays held; a hold that began under no version ends at the first version change; a persona no longer applied is not retried, with one line; the other ends: forgetting P ends P's hold silently (no post, no retry) and keeps Q's, and P held again posts one new alert; `forgetAll` ends every hold silently: only the forget lines, no end or retry line and no retry; below the floor (AC 23): a reuse's re-check answering stop writes one startup-errors entry of the exported class and asks one non-zero stop, with no hold, no alert, no timer stop and no further agent-director call. SRJ-1204's bound (SRJ-204, SRJ-209), with the settings installed on the re-check's tick as `main()` installs them (`installAdSettings`) and `health_check_interval` 0: over several `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS` intervals with no health check, one re-check, one settings read and one pending timer per interval; an `ErrInvalidFlags` step mid-interval adds exactly one re-check and no read, another error adds nothing, and the next scheduled re-check comes when it was due. `afterEach` resets the settings (`resetAdSettingsForTests`). Each runtime refusal case (below the floor, and `ErrSystemInstallTooOld`) also shows no re-check line and no notice through the session manager's notice sink (`noticesDuring`, b.jg5 SRJ-1002), and checks its record through the shared `expectRuntimeEntryPointsToDebugSkill` (`tests/test-helpers/ad-version-entries.ts`). |
| test-timer-overrides.ts | test-timer-overrides.test.ts | The test-only timer override block's start-time install (b.2nr; see the architecture doc, Configuration, Test-only timer overrides). Every expected default is imported from the exported constants (`LATCH_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS`, `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS`, `DIALOG_READY_TIMEOUT_MS`, `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_BASE_S`, `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_CEILING_S`, `DELIVERY_STUCK_AFTER_MS`), never typed. `effectiveTestTimer` and `effectiveTestTimers` give the override or the default for each supported key; `buildTestTimerOverridesLine` gives no line for an absent or empty block and otherwise one line naming every overridden key with its default, in key order whatever the object's order; `installTestTimerOverrides` with no override logs nothing and changes nothing, and with overrides logs the one line, moves the installed version re-check's interval (on `createFakeClock()`, so no real timer is armed) and B's floor only for their own keys, answers the latch and retry values for `main()` to hand on, and leaves the exported constants alone. It is the module's own file only: the block's validation is pinned in config.test.ts, its feeds in conflict-latch.test.ts, unavailable-retry.test.ts, ad-version-gate.test.ts and ad-settings.test.ts, `main()`'s one call and the feeds' wiring in server-startup-wiring.test.ts, and the reload preview's no-effective-change answer in reload-preview.test.ts |

`errConfigMalformed`'s `[tmux]` key, value and minimum; `errUnusableName` per fault; `errInternal`, `errUnknownErrorName` and `errSchemaMismatch` putting the name and description where the client does. Reported name and message: an `ErrUnknownErrorName` reports its unknown name and envelope description, an error of its own class its `errName` and `errDescription`; a token in the description comes out redacted in every reporting class and through the step, checked with `assertNoLeak` on the classifications and rendered lines, each error also carrying a bare sentinel where nothing is reported (a `note` property, and an extra envelope key on the `ErrUnknownErrorName` forms), and each rendered line is exactly `class=… name=<reported name> message=<redacted, JSON-quoted>`; a multi-line description comes out on one line, its lines joined by spaces; a long one is capped at `MAX_LOGGED_MESSAGE_LENGTH`; an unsafe `errName` (token-shaped, with a space, overlong) is never reported, and a token-shaped unknown name is UNAVAILABLE and reports nothing (both with `assertNoLeak`); `describeAdErrorClassification` renders `class=… name=… message=<JSON-quoted>`, or the class alone; the redaction placeholder stands in for a bare token. Never throws: an `ErrSpawnNotFound` whose `errName` getter throws stays STATE (the class decides), a base error whose `errName` getter throws is UNCLASSIFIED with its message, an envelope whose `err_description` getter throws, a proxy whose every trap throws (UNAVAILABLE). `isInvalidFlagsError`: true for the client's `ErrInvalidFlags`, also when its `errName` getter throws; false for a base error with its `errName`, a plain `Error` so named, an object with that `errName`, another agent-director error, `undefined` and a proxy whose every trap throws. `isAdErrorInstance(value, errorClass)`: true for `errSpawnNotFound` against `ErrSpawnNotFound` and against the base `AgentDirectorError`, each Phase-1-only builder against its binding, and an `ErrSpawnNotFound` whose `errName` getter throws; false, throwing nothing, for an `ErrUnknownErrorName` carrying `ErrTmuxKillFailed`, a builder against another class, an agent-director error whose `name` (not class) matches, `undefined`, a string of that name and a proxy whose every trap throws; `ERR_SPAWN_NOT_FOUND_NAME` equals the `errName` of the builder's `ErrSpawnNotFound`. `goneErrNameOf`: `AD_GONE_ERR_NAMES` labels `AD_GONE_ERROR_CLASSES` in order (each GONE builder's `errName`); each GONE builder answers its name, and a base error named `ErrTmuxSendKeys`, an `Error` named `ErrTmuxCaptureFailed`, an `ErrUnknownErrorName` carrying `ErrTmuxSendKeys`, `errSpawnNotFound`, `undefined` and a proxy whose every trap throws answer nothing, none throwing. Purity: classifying every row makes no agent-director call (the stub call log) and no re-check, changes no value, and answers the same twice. a description carrying a token comes out redacted in the `ErrInternal`, `ErrConfigMalformed` and own-class forms, each value also carrying a bare sentinel where nothing is reported, and a token-shaped `unknownName` falls back to `describeAgentDirectorFailure`, all checked with `assertNoLeak`. `isLaunchTimeoutError` and `launchTimeoutFormOf` (b.jg5 SRJ-407): the two forms equal `errCallTimeout().errName` and `ERR_TMUX_UNRESPONSIVE_NAME`, and the launch calls taken from `AD_VERBS` and `AD_LAUNCH_VERBS` are exactly `spawn` and `resume`; yes rows, each declared at `spawn` and at `resume` and still classed UNAVAILABLE: `errCallTimeout(verb)`, `errCallTimeout()` with the client's default verb (the declared call decides), `errTmuxUnresponsiveLaunchTimeout(verb)` on the default and another instance id, and an `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` whose description is exactly `LAUNCH_TIMEOUT_PHRASE`; an `ErrCallTimeout` whose `errName` getter throws or behind a proxy whose every read throws is still the `ErrCallTimeout` form at either launch call; no rows: every yes row declared at every non-launch call (`status`, `get`, both `kill` declarations included), plain `errTmuxUnresponsive`, still stopping, still starting, after "duplicate session", HO rev 26's two new-row-ended builders (`errTmuxUnresponsiveNewRowEnded` UNAVAILABLE and `errTmuxNotAvailableNewRowEnded` ENVIRONMENT in the class table, so no launch timeout at either launch call), every other SRJ-104 row (an `ErrUnknownErrorName` with each Phase-1-only name among them), a base error named `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` or `ErrCallTimeout` and an `ErrUnknownErrorName` carrying `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` with the phrase in its envelope, other classes carrying the phrase, and values that are not agent-director errors or are hostile (an `errDescription` getter that throws, a non-string description, proxies), none throwing; the answers pure (the same twice, no value changed, no stub call). `describeAdFailureForLog` (b.jg5 SRJ-104, SRJ-1014): a reported name gives `describeReportedAdFailure`'s description, an `ErrUnknownErrorName` carrying a store-open name shows that name and never the client's placeholder name or text, a value with none or an unsafe `errName` gives `describeAgentDirectorFailure`'s, and a proxy whose every trap throws never throws. |
| checked-kill.ts | checked-kill.test.ts | The kill's outcome, its SRJ-110 class, the next-step predicate, one checked kill and its log rendering (b.jg5 SRJ-110, SRJ-701, SRJ-703), each kill made through a stub client (`makeStubClient`) with every value an instance of its class, built by the stub's builders, and every expected outcome, class label and constant imported from `src/checked-kill.ts`, `src/ad-error-class.ts` and the stub; every captured line and outcome is leak-checked (`assertNoLeak`). Successes, each letting the next step run (`killLetsNextStepRun`): a result with `kill_sent` true, false or absent is `killed` carrying it as given, and a resolved result with no boolean `kill_sent` is `killed` with it absent; `ErrSpawnNotFound` is `row-gone`; GONE (`ErrTmuxSendKeys`, `ErrTmuxCaptureFailed`) is `session-gone` carrying its name (SRJ-104: for `kill`, gone is success); `row-finished` (read `ended`, `missing`, `no-row`, and `hold-ended`, an old-life wait's kill whose hold ended, b.jg5 SRJ-811). Non-successes: `KILL_FAILURE_CLASSES` is SRJ-110's six classes; one row per value (`NON_SUCCESS_ROWS`: `ErrTmuxKillFailed` with each of its four descriptions, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, each CONFLICT case, `ErrTmuxNotAvailable`, `ErrInternal`, `ErrUnknownErrorName`, `ErrCallTimeout`, each UNUSABLE NAME fault, `ErrConfigMalformed`, a plain `Error`, `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared`, and the unlisted `ErrTmuxSessionCreate`, `ErrSpawnNotResumable`, `ErrInstanceIdCollision`, `ErrInvalidFlags`, `ErrCwdNotFound` and `ErrCwdNotADirectory`) is `not-killed` with its expected class, keeps the thrown value and never lets the next step run, and the row's `unlistedClass` column (set only for the unlisted values) is the class it keeps: each such value's outcome is exactly UNCLASSIFIED with that `unlistedClass`, and every other value carries none; `KILL_UNLISTED_CLASSES` is the three classes SRJ-110 has no kill row for; `ErrTmuxKillFailed` is told apart (`killFailed`) carrying its own description, and any other UNAVAILABLE value is not told apart and carries none: an `ErrUnknownErrorName` carrying that name is UNAVAILABLE with `killFailed` false and no description, and its rendered line is any UNAVAILABLE value's, its envelope's token-bearing description never in it (`assertNoLeak`). One plain call: a failed kill is not retried (a queued later success is never reached), the recorded call carries the instance id alone and no other key, and a kill call that throws synchronously, throws a value whose every read throws, or rejects with `undefined` gives a `not-killed` outcome with no throw or rejection. `killOutcomeStopsServer` is true only for an `ErrInvalidFlags` non-success whose re-check answered `stop`, and false with no throw for every other value; `killLetsNextStepRun` is false for a value that is not an outcome, and `isKillOutcome` true only when the kind is one. Under `goneIsFailure` (b.jg5 SRJ-904; its own describe, through `killThroughStubWith` and `GONE_IS_FAILURE`): each GONE value is the GONE non-success (`not-killed`, class GONE, the thrown value and its name kept), never letting the next step run, after one plain call; with no options, empty options or `goneIsFailure: false` it is the `session-gone` success as before; every other answer (each `kill_sent`, `ErrSpawnNotFound`, each of `NON_SUCCESS_ROWS`) gives the same outcome with and without the option. `describeKillOutcome`: a pin of each form's one line (the GONE non-success's `outcome=not-killed class=GONE <name> message=…` included), each non-success row on one line with its class, a fake token in a session name or a message (with a ticket URL) redacted on one line (the GONE non-success of `errTmuxCaptureFailed` too, never in the `session-gone` form), and the fixed `outcome=unknown` for a value that is not an outcome, with no throw; `outcome=row-finished read=hold-ended`, and `read=unknown` for a read of no known kind. Import boundary: following its runtime imports through `src/` (`forbiddenServerLoads`, with `SERVER_ONLY_MODULES`, `tests/test-helpers/source-audit.ts`), the module loads no server-only module, no Slack module and no `@slack/` package, and the walk reaches `ad-error-class.ts`. The notices built from a kill's outcome (b.jg5 SRJ-1003, SRJ-110; its own describe): `KILL_REFUSAL_AT_KILL` and `KILL_REFUSAL_AT_READ` and both texts pinned, `teardownKillRefusalNoticeText` at a try and at a status read and `teardownKillNotSucceededNoticeText`, each the instance id with `describeKillOutcome`'s rendering; a negative, fractional or NaN count of kills reads `its tries`; a description carrying a token comes out redacted, on one line, in both. Each outcome kind that quotes agent-director (a kill failure naming no survivor, a survivor-naming one, CONFLICT, UNUSABLE NAME, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, an UNCLASSIFIED `ErrInternal`), its description carrying a fake token: `describeKillOutcome` gives the not-killed form with its class and the description through the shared describers, redacted, on one line (`assertNoLeak`; b.jg5 SRJ-1014). |
| kill-retry.ts | kill-retry.test.ts | The bounded retry of a live row's kill (b.jg5 SRJ-702; SRJ-110's survivor rule and CONFIG bullet, SRJ-703, SRJ-704's and SRJ-1007's alert decision, SRJ-1014's lines; AC 56, AC 63's half, AC 64, AC 84). Every case runs `runKillRetry` on `createFakeClock()` through the file's `run` (a `RunSpec`: the kill's answers through the stub client's kill queue, each try one `checkedKill`; the reads between tries as stub `status` answers or a caller's own read kind, latched or no row; the seed, a row read `waiting` by default; an optional keep-going check, pass budget, checked-kill options and `configReadEndsTries`), moved only to the retry's own pending wait (`driveToEnd`, which records each wait's requested delay), so the schedule is asserted on what the code asked for, nothing waits in real time and no timer is left pending; every count, spacing, end, alert kind and class comes from `src/`, every description from the stub's builders. The schedule: a pin of 3 tries 2 s apart; each UNAVAILABLE form (`ErrTmuxKillFailed`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, `ErrUnknownErrorName`, `ErrCallTimeout`, a wrapped `UnknownError`) at every try makes exactly the exported try count at the exported spacing with one read before each further try, ending `exhausted`; a second-try success ends the tries with two kills, one read and no alert; a sleep-function wait is asked for the spacing before each further try; a kill that throws is taken as its outcome and tried again, a read that throws is a failed read, and a throwing log sink changes nothing. Only UNAVAILABLE of a row last read live is tried again: CONFLICT (never retried as a kill), UNUSABLE NAME, CONFIG, ENVIRONMENT, UNCLASSIFIED and the two successes (`ErrSpawnNotFound`, GONE) at the first try give one kill, no read and no wait, and a base `AgentDirectorError` merely named `ErrSpawnNotFound` is no success (not killed, UNCLASSIFIED); a seed of `ended`, `missing` or not live gets one kill; `killRetrySeedIsLive` for each seed kind; HO rev 23: `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` then `ErrTmuxNotAvailable` ends the tries at once with the ENVIRONMENT outcome. An old-life wait's hold-end query (`holdEnded`, b.jg5 SRJ-702, SRJ-811, option A; its own describe): one that never answers ended is asked after each wait, after each read that did not find the row finished and once when the last UNAVAILABLE outcome would stand, the tries running as without it; a hold that ends during the first try (answering a survivor-naming `ErrTmuxKillFailed`, the survivor decision, or `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, none) ends the tries before try 2 with no read, during the read before try 2 with no further kill, or during the last try (`ErrTmuxUnresponsive` or `ErrTmuxKillFailed` after a survivor-naming first try: the survivor decision, never the ordinary; `ErrTmuxKillFailed` with no survivor: none), each as the `row-finished` success read `hold-ended`, end `hold-ended`, not stopped, with one `killRetryHoldEndedLine`; the hold's end wins over a keep-going check answering false and over a failed wait in the same window; a hold already ended when the first try answers a CONFLICT or a success is never asked and that outcome stands; a query that throws counts as not ended. The reads (one before each further try, as the schedule cases show): `ended`, `missing`, `ErrSpawnNotFound` and a no-row read end the tries as the `row-finished` success with no alert; a failed read (UNAVAILABLE, UNCLASSIFIED, an UNUSABLE NAME that latched nothing, a base `AgentDirectorError` merely named `ErrSpawnNotFound`, which is no missing row) lets the try go ahead; a CONFIG read ends the tries (`read-config`, not counted as stopped) on a row last read `pending` and lets the try go ahead on one read `waiting` with `configReadEndsTries` absent or false, the result keeping no `configRead`, the last state read following each live read; under `configReadEndsTries` (the CLI teardown's option) a CONFIG read on a row last read `waiting` or `pending` ends the tries (`read-config`) after one kill and one read, the last try's outcome standing, no alert, the read's error kept as `configRead`, not counted as stopped, its read line saying why (`the row was last read waiting, and this caller ends the tries on any CONFIG read: no further kill`, or the `pending` wording), then the end line and no stop line; after a live read a CONFIG read at the next read ends them after 2 kills and 2 reads, the 2nd try's outcome standing; and a failed read that is no CONFIG (UNAVAILABLE, UNUSABLE NAME) still lets the try go ahead, keeping no `configRead`; a latching read ends them (`read-latched`, stopped); the keep-going check answering false after the wait, false after the read, or throwing ends them (`stopped`). The survivor rule and the alert decision: a survivor-naming first try (`STUB_SURVIVOR_PIDS`) then a read of `ended`, `missing` or `ErrSpawnNotFound`, or a second try's success (`kill_sent` false or true, after a read that found the row live or failed) or `ErrSpawnNotFound`, gives the survivor version quoting that description, both tries logged (the first try's line carries its outcome as `describeKillOutcome` renders it, redacted and capped, and the second try's line its own); then `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` twice gives the ordinary version quoting it alone; then CONFLICT, CONFIG or `ErrTmuxNotAvailable` gives no third try and the ordinary version; a stop by the keep-going check or a latching read gives the ordinary version quoting the standing survivor-naming failure; tries ending in an `ErrTmuxKillFailed` naming no survivor quote both; two survivor-naming failures quote the latest; the worker's own pid names no survivor: an `'outlived-exit-wait'` first try (naming only `STUB_WORKER_PID`) then a read of `ended` gives none, and a survivor description with the worker's pid beside its survivor clause then a read of `ended` gives the survivor version quoting it; with no survivor-naming failure an `ErrTmuxKillFailed` standing gives the ordinary version quoting its own description, one followed by `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` to the end gives none, and a `kill_sent: false` success (SRJ-703) gives none. A caller whose checked kill counts GONE as no success (`killOptions` in the `RunSpec`, `goneIsFailure`; b.jg5 SRJ-904): GONE (`ErrTmuxSendKeys`, `ErrTmuxCaptureFailed`) at the first try makes one kill, no read and no wait, the GONE non-success standing with no alert, its try line `not tried again` and no end line; a survivor-naming first try then GONE at the 2nd try makes no third try, and with the option ends with the ordinary version quoting the survivor-naming description, while with no options (the server default, unchanged) it ends in the `session-gone` success with the survivor version, each with its try and end lines. The pass budget: tries used on UNAVAILABLE spend it and a later kill makes one try with no read or wait; a spent budget still lets a later kill succeed; a retry success, CONFLICT, `ErrTmuxNotAvailable` after an UNAVAILABLE try, a finished read and a row not last read live leave it unspent; a fresh budget is independent. The lines: one per try and per read, each with the prefix and the instance id; a fake token in a try's description and a read's failure comes out redacted (`assertNoLeak` on every case's lines; the alert decision carries descriptions raw by design and is not leak-checked). Import boundary: through `forbiddenServerLoads` (`SERVER_ONLY_MODULES`), no server-only module, no Slack module and no `@slack/` package, the walk reaching `checked-kill.ts` and `ad-description-phrases.ts`. The read, stop and try lines exactly through `killRetryReadLine` (with `KILL_RETRY_VERDICT_*`), `killRetryStopLine` (`KILL_RETRY_STOP_WAIT_FAILED`, `KILL_RETRY_STOP_KEEP_GOING_FALSE`) and `killRetryTryLine` with the `KILL_RETRY_NEXT_*` tails: a CONFIG read on a row last read pending and a read that latched the persona each end the tries with no further kill, the last outcome standing (stopped for the latch); a failed wait between tries ends them before the next with no read, its stop line naming the wait; a retried UNAVAILABLE try, the last try with none left and a try after the pass budget was spent each log the builder's line for what follows. |

Every thrown value, at the `read-pane` and at the `get`: its verdict kind, the class it is reported under and its redacted one-line description (the reported name and agent-director's description when the classifier gives them, else `describeAgentDirectorFailure`'s; a CONFIG description naming the config file): at the `read-pane` GONE and `ErrSpawnNotFound` pass, at the `get` `ErrSpawnNotFound` skips and GONE fails as UNCLASSIFIED; at both, each of `UNAVAILABLE_FORMS` retries, `ErrConfigMalformed` fails at once, each of `CONFLICT_CASES`, each of `UNUSABLE_NAME_FAULTS` and the three `ErrTmuxNotAvailable` forms fail under their own class, and a plain `ErrInternal` (one carrying fake tokens included), `ErrSchemaMismatch`, `ErrSchemaMigrationRequired` and `ErrStoreOpen` (as unknown names), `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared`, `ErrSpawnNotInteractive`, `ErrTmuxSessionCreate`, `ErrCwdNotFound` and `ErrRelayModeOff` fail as UNCLASSIFIED; per call, the rows reach every verdict kind it has. The teardown's verdicts (helper `expectTeardownReport`: the classifier's class, unchanged, and `describeReportedAdFailure`'s redacted one-line description, a CONFIG one naming `AD_CONFIG_FILE_DISPLAY_NAME` and ending with the reported description): `pauseVerdictOf` over a successful pause is done, and over each row of SRJ-903's table (`PAUSE_ERROR_ROWS`) gives its verdict kind, class and description: GONE (`ErrTmuxSendKeys`) escalates; each of `UNAVAILABLE_FORMS` retries; each of `CONFLICT_CASES`, the three `ErrTmuxNotAvailable` forms (ENVIRONMENT), each of `UNUSABLE_NAME_FAULTS` and a plain `ErrInternal` (one carrying fake tokens included) fail; `ErrConfigMalformed` fails naming the config file; `ErrSpawnNotPausable` (a `pending` row) and `ErrSpawnNotFound` (STATE), `ErrPauseTimeout`, `ErrSchemaMismatch` and the other `STORE_OPEN_ERR_NAMES` (as unknown names), `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared`, `ErrRelayModeOff`, `ErrSpawnNotInteractive`, `ErrTmuxSessionCreate` (LAUNCH_FAILURE) and `ErrCwdNotFound` (DIRECTORY) escalate; the rows reach every verdict kind; `pauseVerdictAfterLastTry` turns each row's retry into an escalation with the same class and description and leaves every other verdict, a done one included, as it is. `stateReadVerdictOf`: a null row is absent, each of `AGENT_DIRECTOR_DEAD_STATES` finished, each live state, `pending` and a state CSCB does not know live; over each thrown value (`STATE_READ_ERROR_ROWS`) `ErrSpawnNotFound` is absent and every other fails at once under the classifier's class, GONE, STATE, LAUNCH_FAILURE and DIRECTORY kept (never relabelled UNCLASSIFIED as the precheck's `get` relabels). The outcome: `teardownStopped` carries each `TEARDOWN_STOPPED_*` reason and `teardownFailed` each `TEARDOWN_STEP_*` step with the report's fields and no other; a CONFLICT, an UNCLASSIFIED and a plain `ErrInternal` description carrying fake tokens come out redacted on one line from `pauseVerdictOf`, `stateReadVerdictOf` and `teardownErrorReportOf`. The kill's mapping (b.jg5 SRJ-904, SRJ-702, SRJ-907, SRJ-1007; over retry results built by hand or run through `cliKillRetry`, the real `runKillRetry` under `TEARDOWN_KILL_RETRY_OPTIONS` over `checkedKill` with `TEARDOWN_KILL_OPTIONS` on a fake clock with no timer left pending): `teardownKillOutcomeOf` stops the persona (`killed`) for each success (`SUCCESS_RESULTS`: `kill_sent` true, false, absent; `ErrSpawnNotFound` by the stub's class; a read finding the row `ended`, `missing` or no row), carrying the retry's result and no alert, or, given the survivor decision after a survivor-naming try, that decision as it is; it fails the persona at step `kill` with the classifier's class and the reported, redacted description for every other answer (`NOT_KILLED_ROWS`: each of `UNAVAILABLE_FORMS` after its tries, each CONFLICT case, the three ENVIRONMENT forms, each UNUSABLE NAME fault, a plain `ErrInternal`, a base `AgentDirectorError` merely named `ErrSpawnNotFound` at a try (UNCLASSIFIED, no success), the three store names, `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared`, CONFIG at a try, descriptions with fake tokens redacted), each unlisted one under its own class (`UNLISTED_ROWS`: STATE, DIRECTORY, LAUNCH FAILURE), GONE at the first try of `cliKillRetry` as GONE after one kill with no alert (`GONE_ROWS`), a `session-gone` outcome as GONE described by its name, never stopped, and an outcome of a kind the mapping does not list as UNCLASSIFIED, never stopped. The alert decision: a survivor-naming first try then `ErrSpawnNotFound`, or a read finding the row `ended`, in `cliKillRetry` stops the persona carrying the retry's own survivor decision; a survivor-naming first try then GONE at the 2nd try of `cliKillRetry` fails as GONE after 2 kills and 1 read with the retry's own ordinary decision; every unlisted row and every row of `NOT_KILLED_ROWS` but UNAVAILABLE, after a survivor-naming try, fails keeping the retry's ordinary decision quoting it; an `ErrTmuxKillFailed` naming no survivor after a survivor-naming one keeps the ordinary decision quoting both. CONFIG at a read between tries (`CONFIG_READ_ROWS`, each run through `cliKillRetry`: `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` on a row last read `waiting` and on one last read `pending`; the survivor-naming `ErrTmuxKillFailed` on a row last read `waiting`; it, a read of `waiting`, then `ErrCallTimeout`; `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, a read of a `pending` row with no launch start, then `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`): the CONFIG read ends the tries (`read-config`) with no further kill, the last try's outcome standing and the read's error kept as `configRead`, and the persona fails at the kill as CONFIG naming the config file, whatever the last try, keeping the retry's decision. `teardownKillReadOf`: no row is no row, each dead, live, `pending` and unknown state its state, a `pending` row with no launch start a live read and never a latching one, and every thrown value (UNUSABLE NAME, CONFLICT, each UNAVAILABLE form, `ErrSpawnNotFound`, a plain `ErrInternal`, CONFIG) a failed read carrying it. every expected alert line built from `src/kill-failure-alert.ts`'s exports with `killFailureCliTeardownEntryContext(command)`), for both commands: SRJ-907's forms pinned once as literals, with no `[slack] ` prefix, `teardownFailureLine` and `teardownNotStoppedLine` for counts 1 and 2; for a CONFLICT, a CONFIG and each store name (`LINE_ROWS`), the line holds, in order, the command, the persona, its session, the class and the redacted description, on one line, a CONFIG one naming the config file and a store one its name; a CONFLICT and an UNCLASSIFIED description carrying fake tokens come out redacted on one line in the line. `teardownRejectedOutcomeOf` over a plain `Error` carrying fake tokens, an agent-director error, a string and `undefined` is the failed outcome at `TEARDOWN_STEP_UNEXPECTED` with `teardownErrorReportOf`'s report, on one line, with no leak. `personaTeardownReportOf`: each stopped reason and each kill success with no alert (`SUCCESS_RESULTS`: `kill_sent` true, false and absent, `ErrSpawnNotFound` by class, a read finding the row ended, missing or gone) reports nothing; each kill success after a survivor-naming try reports no failure line and is not failed, the survivor alert line with its CLI closing sentence printed and logged as one line, naming the stub's pids and the command, and one `persona-kill-survivor` entry holding it; each failure whose decision is the ordinary version (`ORDINARY_ROWS`: each `ErrTmuxKillFailed` description standing after the tries, one naming no survivor after a survivor-naming try with both quoted, and `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, CONFLICT, GONE and a plain `ErrInternal` after a survivor-naming try) reports the failure line then the ordinary alert line, printed and logged as two lines, and one `persona-kill-failed` entry holding both, flattened; each failure with no alert (`NO_ALERT_ROWS`: a pause CONFLICT, a pause CONFIG, a state read UNAVAILABLE, a kill `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` or CONFLICT with no survivor-naming try, a kill UNUSABLE NAME, a teardown that rejected) reports the line alone and one `cli-teardown-failed` entry; a version that does not fit the outcome is never reported (a stopped persona given the ordinary decision reports nothing, a failed one given the survivor decision only its line under `cli-teardown-failed`); descriptions carrying fake tokens come out redacted in every printed line, logged line and entry (the survivor description's token leads it, so the log cap never cuts the token off). The bounds (b.jg5 SRJ-908): `exitTimeoutMsOf` in ms, 0 for 0, negative and non-finite values; `precheckBoundMs` for call timeouts 0, the minimum, the default and the maximum is its two calls' tries; `teardownBoundMs` for `exit_timeout` 0, 1 and 5 s is the state read, the pause's tries, the poll and the kill's tries with their reads; both grow with each input, and a negative or non-finite call timeout counts as 0. The answer check's line, the restart lines and the not-restarted alert (b.jg5 SRJ-906, SRJ-1013; describe 'the not-restarted alert and its class, the answer check's line and the restart lines'), 9 cases: `answerCheckFailedTryLine`, `cleanRestartStartingAfterFailedTeardownLine` and `cleanRestartStartFailedLine` (for a status and for null) pinned exactly once; for each try number up to `PRECHECK_TRIES`, the answer check line of a CONFIG failure names the config file and an UNCLASSIFIED description carrying fake tokens comes out redacted, each on one line; `CLEAN_RESTART_NOT_RESTARTED_LABEL`, `CLEAN_RESTART_NOT_STARTED_SENTENCE` and one persona's `cleanRestartNotRestartedAlert` line pinned exactly once; for one failed persona, two, and two given in the other order, one line with no newline that starts with the command, names each persona with its session and class in the order given and ends with the closing sentence, with no leak; and failures whose descriptions carry fake tokens give an alert holding each class but neither the redacted description nor the token. Purity (static, b.jg5 SRJ-114, SRJ-115, SRJ-801, SRJ-908): its import walk (`forbiddenServerLoads`) reaches no server-only or Slack module, never `src/cli.ts`, `src/retired-keys.ts` or the settings reader `src/ad-settings.ts` (the walk reaches `src/ad-config-file.ts`), and only relative or `node:` specifiers; its text has no `Date.now`, `setTimeout`, `setInterval` or `instanceof`. Passes: `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` succeeds with the path, version and client minimum as the floor and no note after one resolver call; only `DEV_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION` succeeds below CSCB's floor with a note equal to `buildInstallCheckPhase1Note(version)`, naming the version, `INSTALL_CHECK_PHASE1_NOTE_PHRASE` and `PHASE1_RUNBOOK_SECTION_TITLE`. Text copied from a thrown error is one token-free line: an unreachable error's diagnostic (fake token, URL, CRLF and a run of line feeds) and signal (U+2028) are redacted to one line and its numeric exit code kept, compared whole with the canned failure; a diagnostic or signal that is not a string, or an exit code that is not a number, is `null`; a not-found error's checked locations keep each `kind` and redact each `detail` to one line; checked locations that are a string, an object or missing give an empty list with no credential. Recognition by name: for not found, too old and unreachable, a plain object carrying only the error's `errName` (no `name`, not an `Error`) gives the same result as the class-built error. The client minimum through `runInstallCheck`: a non-SemVer or leading-`v` minimum fails `AD_VERSION_FLOOR_UNREADABLE` with the minimum in `detail`; a seeded malformed-file or missing-field floor failure is returned unchanged and the resolver is never called. `readClientMinVersion` over its injected `resolveFloorUrl` and `readFile` (a temp-dir `file:` URL; no module mock): a readable floor file gives its `min_binary_version`, read once from the resolved path, and a string minimum that is not a version is returned as is; a reader throwing an `Error` (fake token, URL, line breaks) gives the canned read failure with `underlying` redacted to one line, a thrown string `underlying: null`; the default resolver with a throwing reader passes the reader the installed client's floor file (`FLOOR_SUBPATH` from the versions helper) and fails as a read; a resolved URL that is not `file:` gives the canned read failure without calling the reader; a throwing resolver gives the resolve failure (`underlying` one line, or `null` for a thrown string) without calling the reader, its message not the read failure's; malformed JSON gives the parse failure with a one-line `underlying` and no credential; a missing field, an empty string, a number, `null`, a JSON `null` document and a JSON array document give the missing-field failure with the parsed document as `detail`; the four modes carry four distinct messages. The cache bypass: a call with an injected reader, or only an injected resolver, neither returns nor replaces a seeded value, and a seeded failure is not returned to it; after a reset, neither a failed nor a successful injected read is cached, so the next no-arg call reads the installed floor file; an empty deps object uses the cache as a no-arg call does.
