New features that add significant logic should get their own test file (e.g., `session-manager.test.ts`).

## Isolation (SR-13.2)

Tests never touch the real home or a real install.

- **Temp directories only.** Every file a test reads or writes (config, credentials, state directory, crontable, logs, working directories, agent-director store) sits under the test's own `mkdtempSync` directory, passed explicitly, and removed in `afterEach`.
- **agent-director's settings file.** A test writes `config.toml` only through `writeAgentDirectorConfig` (`tests/test-helpers/ad-settings.ts`) under a temp HOME, never as a hand-written TOML literal and never under the real `~/.config/agent-director`, and reads it through a reader whose home getter answers that HOME. It takes every settings value (defaults, minimums, key names, the path, refused forms) from `src/ad-settings.ts` or the helper, never typed; the SRD's numbers appear only in the pin table of `tests/ad-settings.test.ts`.
- **Never the real home.** No test reads or writes `~/.claude`, `~/.claude/channels/slack`, `~/.config/claude-slack-channel-bots`, `~/.config/agent-director` or `~/.npm`, and no path in a fixture resolves to them. Code that falls back to `os.homedir()` is tested through `runInFakeHome` (see Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks); code that takes a home or `SLACK_STATE_DIR` gets a temp one. `runPostinstall` falls back to the OS home (and so the real `~/.claude/skills`, which a case with `linkSkills: true` or an installed-copy `packageDir` writes) when `homeDir` is unset, so `tests/postinstall.test.ts` calls it only through its `postinstall()` wrapper, which refuses options without `homeDir`, with paths from `makeSandbox()` (home, state dir and MCP path under one `mkdtempSync` root).
- **The retired-key record.** A test writes `retired-keys.json` only through `writeRetiredKeysRecord` (`tests/test-helpers/retired-keys.ts`), in its own `mkdtempSync` state directory passed explicitly, never in the real state directory (`~/.config/claude-slack-channel-bots`); a store under test is loaded over that directory, and a test that reaches `recordStartupError` points its `logDir` (or `SLACK_STATE_DIR`) at the test's own directory.
- **The stored-choice file.** A test writes a valid `channel-delivery.json` only through the stored-choice helper (`writeChannelDeliveryRecord`, `tests/test-helpers/channel-delivery.ts`), in its own `mkdtempSync` state directory passed explicitly, never in the real state directory (`~/.config/claude-slack-channel-bots`) (b.deo SRI-1204); the reload harness's `h.writeChannelDeliveryBytes` puts bytes the parser must refuse in the harness's own state directory (see the stored-choice helper below for how they are made). A store under test is loaded over that directory.
- **No real server or CLI.** No test calls `main()`, runs `src/server.ts` or `src/cli.ts` as an entry point, or starts a real daemon in-process. The one exception is the usage-text tests in `tests/cli.test.ts` (`unknown subcommand`, and the two `credentials` cases that stop before the script: a usage error and an unknown persona), which run `src/cli.ts` in a child process through the file's `runCli` helper. `runCli` starts Bun by absolute path (`process.execPath`) with its `env` a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call (see `host-safe-env.ts` below): the temp HOME, no tool, so `PATH` is `EMPTY_CHILD_PATH` and names no directory, its own `TMUX_TMPDIR`, and as extras `SLACK_STATE_DIR` and the fixed `BUN_RUNTIME_TRANSPILER_CACHE_PATH=0`, so Bun writes no transpiler cache under the temp HOME for a tree snapshot to see. Nothing else comes from `process.env`. It has a 15 s time limit and checks that the child spawned and was not killed by it. These cases never reach a subcommand that acts. A new test that spawns the CLI uses `runCli`. The CLI is otherwise tested through `createCli` with injected `CliDeps`; `server.ts` through its importable modules (see Importing server.ts).
- **Inject the `claude_config_dir` check.** The bring-up controller's default `checkConfigDir` resolves an unset `claude_config_dir` against the real OS home's `.claude`, so a dangling real `~/.claude` would hold every persona. Every controller a test builds injects `checkConfigDir`: `checkPersonaConfigDir(persona, { home })` over a scratch home holding a real `.claude` (the `configDirCheckUnder(base)` helpers in `tests/persona-lifecycle.test.ts`, `tests/registry.test.ts`, `tests/health-check.test.ts` and `tests/persona-relaunch-gate.test.ts`; `tests/persona-connections.test.ts`; the reload harness without a real launch), or `checkLaunchConfigDir` where the suite already set the session manager's spawn home (`tests/session-manager.test.ts`, `tests/restart.test.ts`, `tests/persona-bringup.test.ts`, the reload harness with a real launch). `tests/session-manager.test.ts` sets a scratch default spawn home (`_setSpawnHomeDir` over a `default-home` fixture holding a real `.claude`) in `beforeEach`, so no launch resolves against the process home; a test that needs its own replaces it with `useSpawnHome`.
- **CLI log seams.** `openLogAppend`, `closeFd` and `initLogging` are `CliDeps`, so CLI tests inject fakes: no real descriptor is opened and `console` is never taken over. To see which lines reach the log and which the terminal, inject an `initLogging` that swaps `console.error` for a recorder and restore it afterwards. The agent-director calls are built by the exported `createDirectorOps(getClient)`; test it by passing a stub client, not by installing a Client singleton.
- **Fake tokens only.** See the rules under Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks.

### Host safety (b.jg5 SRJ-1301)

Host `bun test` never executes an agent-director binary. The client's discovery (`Client.create()`, `resolveSystemBinary()`) finds the host's real install on `PATH` or at the standard install path under `~/.config/agent-director`, and a real client opens its store. Redirecting HOME alone moves neither: `PATH` still names the install, and `src/` code that calls `os.homedir()` still sees the launch-time home. So the preload guard first refuses to start under a launch-time home that could reach the operator's real Claude, Slack or agent-director state, and only then redirects the environment.

- **No unit test executes an agent-director binary.** Every unit test gets its client from the stub (next item), and the preload guard (below) makes a test that forgets it fail as not found instead of reaching the host's install.
- **Everything that spawns runs only in the images.** A test that runs an agent-director binary (the release's, its agent-director-admin, 0.10.0's or any other), starts a tmux server or session, launches a worker (a real `spawn`, reuse or `resume`) or edits an agent-director store with `sqlite3` is a `/ci` scenario (`tests/integration/test-*.sh`, see Integration scripts run only in docker CI), a contained real-agent heavy scenario (`tests/real-agent/`) or a `/ci-live` check (`ci-live/checks/`), never a `bun test` file, and refuses to run outside its image (`/etc/cscb-ci-image`, see `tests/README.md`, Image marker).
- **The one exception: `/ci-live`'s HOST check.** The `/ci-live` runner's host snapshot (`snapshotHost`, `ci-live/lib/host-state.ts`), taken on the host before and after each run, makes two read-only probes there: the host's installed agent-director's `list` of its `service=cscb` rows (`agent-director --store-path <HOME>/.config/agent-director/state.db list --label service=cscb`) and `tmux ls -F '#{session_name}'`. It never runs a binary the images install. Nothing else on the host, in any test, runs an agent-director binary or touches the host's tmux server or `state.db`; `tests/ci-live-docker.test.ts` and `tests/ci-live-checks.test.ts` pin those two argv as the only host-side ones.
- **The images' agent-director binaries stay in the images.** The release's binary, its agent-director-admin and its `install.sh` are installed and run only inside the `/ci` and `/ci-live` images (`docker/README.md`). None of them enters a development tree, is installed globally or in a production CSCB install, or runs on the host; a development tree holds only the npm client at `package.json`'s exact pin.
- **Dependencies are installed with scripts disabled.** Install a development tree's dependencies with `bun install --ignore-scripts`, so CSCB's own `postinstall` never runs against the real HOME (`tests/README.md`, Running the unit suite).
- **Every client is a stub.** A test that needs an agent-director client gets it from `tests/test-helpers/agent-director-stub.ts`: a `makeStubClient()` installed with `setClientForTests`, or a seam filled with `makeStubCreateClient(...)` (the start gate's `createClient`) or `makeStubResolveSystemBinary(...)`. `makeCloseCountingStubClient(opts?)` gives a `makeStubClient(opts)` stub whose `close()` only counts its calls (`{ client, closes() }`), for a case that checks which client was closed and how often; `makePassingGateDeps(overrides?)` gives the start gate's seams for a run that passes every step (a default `makeStubCreateClient()`, the three API-surface probes passing, an ENOENT state.db stat, UID 1000, a swallowing startup-error recorder and an `exit` that throws), with `overrides` winning over every default. `makeStubClient`'s default `binaryVersion` is `PHASE1_RC_VERSION` (from `tests/test-helpers/agent-director-versions.ts`), which passes CSCB's Phase 1 floor; a test that needs an older or development binary sets `binaryVersion` explicitly, taking the value from that helper. `tests/install-check.test.ts` passes `runInstallCheck` a `makeStubResolveSystemBinary(...)` resolver, and `tests/postinstall.test.ts` passes the probe an injected install check answering canned results; neither mocks `agent-director`.
- **No value import of `Client` or `resolveSystemBinary`.** No test file or test helper holds either as a value from `agent-director`: no named import or re-export (aliased or not), no `export *`, and no read through the module namespace (a property or element read, destructuring, a spread or rest copy, a default or `require` binding, a dynamic `import()`). There is no exception, not even inside `expect(...)`: an identity assertion on `ad.resolveSystemBinary` is a read. Type-only imports (`import type`, an inline `type`) are allowed, and so is spreading the namespace, or a copy of it, into a `mock.module` factory (`{ ...REAL, resolveSystemBinary: stub }`) or comparing a copy with the live namespace entry by entry (see Module Mocks).
- **Every child's `env` is a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call.** Every child-process call in a test file or test helper (the `child_process` functions, `fork` included, however imported; `Bun.spawn`, `Bun.spawnSync` and `$`) passes an options object literal with exactly one `env`, and that `env` is a direct call of `hostSafeChildEnv` imported from `host-safe-env.ts` (see the helper list under Factory Functions). A `Bun.$` template ends in `.env(hostSafeChildEnv(...))`. A child-process function referenced without being called (put in a deps object, re-exported) is refused: the audit can't follow it.
- **The preload guard.** The `[test]` preload of the repository root's `bunfig.toml` and of the `bunfig.toml` in every directory holding a test file (`tests/bunfig.toml`, `tests/integration/bunfig.toml`) loads `tests/test-helpers/host-safety-preload.ts` once, before any test file, when `bun test` starts in one of those directories. Bun reads only the `bunfig.toml` in the directory the run starts in (no upward search) and resolves a relative preload path against that directory, so each file names the guard relative to its own directory, and every entry starts with `./`, `../` or `/` (a bare `a/b.ts` is looked up as a package). The guard is the first `[test]` `preload` entry: Bun loads the entries in order, so an entry before it would run before the refusal. Entries after it are allowed. A new directory of test files gets its own `bunfig.toml` naming the guard the same way. A run started in a directory with no `bunfig.toml` (outside the repository, or one holding no test file, such as `src/`) has no guard. Its first action is the launch-home refusal (next item); a run it accepts goes on. The guard then computes its variables with `preloadRedirectedEnv(process.env, home, tmuxTmpDir)` from `host-safe-env.ts`, which is pure, and applies them to `process.env`: each of `PRELOAD_ENV_NAMES` is set or deleted, nothing else is touched. Its only writes to disk are the new HOME directory and, when the run inherited no valid one, the `TMUX_TMPDIR` directory, both directly under the OS temp directory (`osTempDir()`, the normalized `os.tmpdir()`, so a `TMPDIR` such as `/tmp//` names the same directory as `/tmp`); a refused run makes neither:
  - `HOME`: a new, empty directory directly under the OS temp directory, named with `PRELOAD_HOME_PREFIX`. Every test file shares it; it is never removed, so each run leaves one.
  - `PATH`: the inherited absolute entries, in order, duplicates dropped, without every directory that holds an `agent-director` entry; `EMPTY_CHILD_PATH` when none is left.
  - `TMUX`, `TMUX_PANE` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`: unset. `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` names a Claude configuration directory (a run started from a bot's session inherits its persona's), which Claude Code, a hook or any code honouring it would read and write. `TMUX_TMPDIR`: the process's one fenced directory from `childTmuxTmpDir()` (the inherited one when valid, else a new one), the same one `hostSafeChildEnv` hands every child, so a run makes at most one. It is never removed.
  - `SLACK_STATE_DIR`: `<new HOME>/.config/claude-slack-channel-bots` (`PRELOAD_STATE_DIR_PATH`, the server's default state directory since bug b.fc4; not created). It is set rather than unset because the state-directory resolvers fall back to `os.homedir()`.
  - `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`: `<new HOME>/.config` (`XDG_CONFIG_DIR`, not created). The server's default state directory, and the crontable in it, with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` unset is `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/claude-slack-channel-bots` (bugs b.fc4, b.avm): an inherited value (the operator's own `~/.config`, say) would put it in the real home, and unset, empty or relative, `resolveXdgConfigHome` falls back to `os.homedir()`.

  It then runs `preloadCheckFailures(process.env)`, the one check shared with `tests/host-safety.test.ts`, and throws, failing the run before any test file loads, when it reports anything. The message names the failed checks by their `PRELOAD_CHECK` labels (a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` still set, empty included, is `claude-config-dir-set`; an `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` unset or empty is `xdg-config-home-unset`, and one that is relative or not strictly under HOME is `xdg-config-home-not-under-home`) and HOME, never the environment. No file imports the guard. With the guard in place, a test that forgot its stub fails as not found (`ErrSystemInstallNotFound`, the class label `AD_SYSTEM_INSTALL_NOT_FOUND` exported from `src/install-check.ts`) instead of reaching the host's install.
- **The launch-home refusal.** Bun fixes `os.homedir()` and `os.userInfo().homedir` at start-up from the HOME the run was launched with, and the guard cannot move them. So, as its first action, before it makes a directory or changes the environment, the guard checks each launch-time home (`launchTimeHomes()`: both values, duplicates dropped) with `launchHomeRefusal(launchHome, passwdHome(), LIVE_LAUNCH_HOME_PROBE)` from `host-safe-env.ts`. Nothing runs before that check: every module a file loads runs its top-level code before the file's first statement, so the guard loads only `node:` builtins and `./host-safe-env.ts`, and `host-safe-env.ts` only `node:` builtins (no `src/` module, package or other test helper, not even for a type). The decision is pure: its only reads are the probe's (a `LaunchHomeProbe`): `exists` (an `lstat`; a dangling symlink, or an entry that cannot be looked at, counts as present), `canonical` (the path made absolute with every symlink on it followed as far as it exists: the real path of its deepest existing part, a dangling symlink followed to its target, the rest appended, so it names the entry a write to the path would reach) and `tempDir` (`osTempDir()`). Below, a path "resolves" to its `canonical` form. The first rule that applies wins, each with its `LAUNCH_HOME_REFUSAL` label:
  - `launch-home-not-absolute`: the home is empty or a relative path.
  - `launch-home-in-real-home` (passwd home known): the home is the account's passwd home or lies under it, compared by path text first (nothing is probed for a home that matches) and then with both resolved.
  - `launch-home-temp-dir-is-root` (no passwd home): the OS temp directory resolves to the file system root (`/`). Every absolute path lies under the root, so the temp-directory rules would accept any home. Nothing about the home is probed.
  - `launch-home-not-under-temp-dir` (no passwd home): the home does not resolve strictly under the OS temp directory (resolved too); the temp directory itself is refused.
  - `launch-home-holds-slack-state`: anything exists at `<home>/.config/claude-slack-channel-bots` (`PRELOAD_STATE_DIR_PATH`, the server's default state directory), at `<home>/.config/cscb` (`LEGACY_XDG_STATE_DIR_PATH`, its default in 0.14.0 and 0.15.0) or at `<home>/.claude/channels/slack` (`LEGACY_STATE_DIR_PATH`, its default before bug b.fc4); both are in `DEFAULT_STATE_DIR_PATHS`.
  - `launch-home-derived-path-in-real-home` (passwd home known): a path in `LAUNCH_HOME_DERIVED_PATHS` under the home resolves to the passwd home (as given or resolved) or a path under it. These are the paths `src/` and the agent-director client derive from `homedir()`: `.claude`, `.claude/channels/slack`, `.claude/projects`, `.claude/skills`, `.claude/slack-mcp.json`, `.config/agent-director`, `.config/agent-director/state.db`, `.config/agent-director/config.toml`, `.config/agent-director/bin/agent-director`, and `.config`, `.config/claude-slack-channel-bots` and `.config/claude-slack-channel-bots/crontab` (the server's default state directory with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` unset, and the crontable in it, which the server's start creates and into which it moves the old default's files; bugs b.fc4, b.avm). `.claude/channels/slack` (`LEGACY_STATE_DIR_PATH`) stays in the list because the CLI still reads it until the server has moved it.
  - `launch-home-derived-path-not-under-temp-dir` (no passwd home): a path in `LAUNCH_HOME_DERIVED_PATHS` under the home does not resolve strictly under the OS temp directory.
  - `launch-home-holds-agent-director`: the home holds `.config/agent-director` or an entry at the standard install path.

  `passwdHome()` reads `/etc/passwd` with `passwdHomeFrom`: the first line whose uid field equals the user's uid exactly decides, and a later line for the same uid is never used. There is no passwd home when the file cannot be read, when no line has the uid (as for an account served by LDAP), or when that first line is malformed (fewer than seven fields, or an empty or relative home); `launchHomeRefusal` also takes a relative or empty passwd home as none. With no passwd home the real home is unknown, so the guard fails closed: the temp directory must not resolve to the root, the two temp-directory rules take the real-home rules' places, and the home and every derived path must resolve strictly under the OS temp directory. The root is the only ancestor of a home refused as the temp directory: with no passwd home and the temp directory set to another one (`TMPDIR=/home`, say), a home under it, the real one included, passes the temp-directory rules unless a derived path resolves out of that directory, and is then refused only by `launch-home-holds-slack-state` or `launch-home-holds-agent-director`. On a refusal the guard writes one line to stderr, `launchHomeRefusalMessage(reason, launchHome)`: `host-safety preload: refusing to start: <label> (launch-time home "<path>")` and a pointer to `tests/README.md`, never the environment. It then exits with `LAUNCH_HOME_REFUSED_EXIT_CODE` (78, sysexits' `EX_CONFIG`), so a refusal is told apart from a failing test (exit 1). No test file loads and no directory is made. For a home the guard accepts, no path in `LAUNCH_HOME_DERIVED_PATHS` resolves into the real home (with no passwd home, each resolves strictly under the OS temp directory, which is not the root), and nothing exists at its Slack state directory or agent-director install paths. When `src/` derives another path from `homedir()`, add it to `LAUNCH_HOME_DERIVED_PATHS` and to `LINKABLE_DERIVED_PATHS` in `tests/launch-home-guard.test.ts`, which writes the list out so that a path dropped from the helper fails its row.
- **HOME under the preload.** The preload moves only what reads the environment (the client's discovery, the `SLACK_STATE_DIR` resolvers, the crontable's default through `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`). `src/` code that calls `homedir()` directly still answers the launch-time home that the refusal accepted: the start gate's `state.db` path, the state-directory resolvers' fallback with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` unset, the crontable's default with `SLACK_STATE_DIR` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` unset, the Claude config-directory default, the default transcript root and `~` expansion. So every run starts with its own scratch HOME outside the real home (`tests/README.md`; `/publish`'s preflight starts its SR-2.3 run the same way), and code that falls back to `os.homedir()` is tested through `runInFakeHome`. Don't patch `os.homedir` in the preload instead: a patched property misses a named import, and a process-wide `mock.module('node:os')` is the top-level mock that Module Mocks forbids.
- **What a test may change.** A test never widens what the guard set: it never sets HOME, `TMUX` or `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` process-wide and never puts back a `PATH` directory the guard removed. It leaves `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` as the guard set it: a test that needs another value, or needs `SLACK_STATE_DIR` unset for the crontable's default, passes `resolveDefaultCronTablePath`, `resolveXdgConfigHome` or the loader (`ResolvePersonaConfigOptions.env`) an explicit environment object and its own temp home instead, never a copy of `process.env`, as `tests/config-cron.test.ts` does. It may narrow `PATH` (a subset of the guard's entries, or a scratch directory of the test's own that holds no `agent-director`) and set `TMUX_TMPDIR` and `SLACK_STATE_DIR`, and restores each to the guard's value in `afterEach`, as `tests/legacy-reply-hook-cleanup.test.ts` (`PATH`, `SLACK_STATE_DIR`) and `tests/host-safety.test.ts` (`PATH`, `TMUX_TMPDIR`) do (see also Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks). One exception: a host-safety refusal test may point `PATH` at a fixture directory of its own that holds a plain, non-executable `agent-director` file, to prove the refusal, and restores `PATH` in `afterEach`. `tests/host-safety.test.ts`'s "a tool's own PATH directory holds an agent-director file" row is the one such case.
- **Enforcement.** `tests/host-safety.test.ts` audits the tree with the TypeScript parser, so text in strings, comments, templates and regex literals never counts: the value-import rule and the child-environment rule over every file `bun test` runs and every test helper; that no file in the repository imports the preload, and that the repository root and every directory holding a test file have a `bunfig.toml` whose first `[test]` preload entry names it with a path entry that resolves to it from that file's directory (a directory with none fails, naming it; an entry before the guard fails, entries after it are allowed); that the preload's first statement after its imports is the launch-home refusal loop over `launchTimeHomes()`, calling `launchHomeRefusal(<home>, passwdHome(), LIVE_LAUNCH_HOME_PROBE)` and, when it gives a reason, ending in `process.exit(LAUNCH_HOME_REFUSED_EXIT_CODE)`, so nothing in the guard runs before the refusal; that the preload loads only `node:` builtins and `./host-safe-env.ts` (`PRELOAD_LOADS`) and `host-safe-env.ts` only `node:` builtins (`HELPER_LOADS`), checked by `guardLoadFindings`, which flags any other static import (side-effect-only and type-only included, so the rule does not depend on what the transpiler drops), `export … from`, `import x = require(…)`, dynamic `import()` or `require()`, and any load whose specifier is not one string literal, because a loaded module's top-level code would run before the refusal; that only the guard's own files (`REAL_HOME_FILES`: `host-safe-env.ts`, the preload, `tests/host-safety.test.ts` and `tests/launch-home-guard.test.ts`) reference `realHome` or `passwdHome`, the helpers that answer the real home's path; over `src/` and `scripts/`, the SRJ-121 call sites (`Client.create` only in the startup gate's module, `resolveSystemBinary` as a value only in the server, the install check and `/publish`'s check). The value-read matcher these audits share with `tests/fmk-source-audit.test.ts` lives in `tests/test-helpers/ad-value-reads.ts` (`agentDirectorValueReads`, `agentDirectorNamespaceArguments`). It then runs the shared `preloadCheckFailures` on the live environment (every name in `PRELOAD_ENV_NAMES`) and, only when that reports nothing, calls `runStartupGate()` and `runInstallCheck()` un-injected: both must fail as not found. `tests/launch-home-guard.test.ts` covers the launch-home refusal: `passwdHomeFrom` over passwd texts; every rule and their order over a recording fake probe, with the passwd home known and unknown, a temp directory resolving to the root, the `/home` temp directory that lets the would-be real home through, and one row per derived path; the live probe over fixtures beside a fake real home, and against the OS temp directory with no passwd home; the guard in a `bun test` child started from each directory with a `bunfig.toml` (the repository root, `tests/` and `tests/integration/`), refused under a fake home holding either default state directory (`.config/claude-slack-channel-bots` or `.claude/channels/slack`) and accepted under an empty one; that this run's `homedir()`-derived defaults resolve outside the passwd home (with no passwd entry, strictly under the OS temp directory); and preflight's SR-2.3 scratch HOME. A refusal test launches its child only with a fake home under its own `mkdtempSync` root, never with the real home.
- **Fixing a finding.** Inject the stub, or pass `env: hostSafeChildEnv(...)` directly in the call. When the preload check fails, run `bun test` from a directory whose `bunfig.toml` loads the guard (the repository root, `tests/` or `tests/integration/`), so the guard loads and sets HOME and `PATH` itself; still start with a scratch HOME (`tests/README.md`). When the bunfig audit names a directory, give it a `bunfig.toml` whose first `[test]` preload entry names the guard relative to it. When the load audit flags the guard or `host-safe-env.ts`, write what the guard needs in `host-safe-env.ts` from `node:` builtins instead of importing it. When the guard refuses to start (exit 78, `host-safety preload: refusing to start: <label>`), relaunch with a new, empty scratch HOME outside the real home, made under a `TMPDIR` outside it too (and not `/`), as `tests/README.md` shows. Never loosen an audit or a refusal rule, add an exemption or widen what the preload set to make room.

### Integration scripts run only in docker CI

The integration scripts and real-agent fixtures run inside the Docker CI harness, importing packed production state/receipt adapters. They never classify native terminal text or retain it in diagnostics.

- Never run them on a dev box or on any host without container support, and never against the real home. If docker is not available, report the integration run as not done; don't run the scripts directly.
- They refuse outside the image. `/etc/cscb-ci-image` exists only in the `cscb-ci` images. `tests/runner.sh` (before it reads its arguments, writes an in-shard file or runs a script), `scenario.sh` (at source time, so every script that sources it), each `test-*.sh` that does not source it (Tests 1 to 4), `fmk-driver.ts`, `stub-mcp-session.ts`, `switch-over.ts` and `fmk-texts.ts` check for it before their first other step and, without it, refuse and say why; `ad-version-stand-in.sh` checks for it before it reads its settings or runs agent-director, and refuses (exit 70) without it or without its settings file. So does every `scenario.sh` step that installs, moves or swaps an agent-director binary or the shim, every harness agent-director call (`ad`, `ad_capture`, `ad_admin`, `ad_admin_capture`) and every harness `sqlite3` read, edit or copy (`ad_store_edit`, `ad_store_id`, `ad_store_backup`, `ad_store_pending_no_launch`) and every stub-worker helper (`stub_mode`, `stub_dialog_delay`, `stub_release`, `stub_press_enter`, `stub_type_exit`, `write_mcp_config`), the store-row read (`ad_store_row`), the Slack stub and stub-session helpers (`slack_stub_push`, `stub_session_deliveries`, `stub_session_call`; `IMAGE_GUARDED_STUB_HELPERS`, each running the check as its first step) and the agent-director settings writer (`write_ad_settings`), through `require_ci_image`. A script that sources `scenario.sh` runs nothing but `set` options and literal assignments before its source line; one that does not starts with its own marker check. `fmk-driver.ts`, `stub-mcp-session.ts`, `switch-over.ts` and `fmk-texts.ts` statically import only `node:` built-ins and load the package under test (the session client the package's MCP SDK, the printer for an entry that needs it also its agent-director client; `switch-over.ts` also the step reader `tests/test-helpers/runbooks.ts` and `markdown.ts`) only after the check. The Slack stub server has no marker check and statically imports only `node:` built-ins.
- The `SCENARIO_ROOT` rule. Every `scenario.sh` function with a step (`sqlite3`, a copy, move or install, an `install.sh` run, a run of agent-director, or a call of a function with one) calls `require_scenario_home` before its first step, except the functions `tests/host-safety.test.ts` lists with why: `HOME_CHECK_EXEMPT` (primitives, setup and shared-mode helpers) and `HOME_CHECKED_BY_CALLERS` (private helpers run only after an audited helper's check, and from no script). A new helper with a step needs the check, or a commented entry in one of those lists. `slack_stub_push`, `stub_session_deliveries` and `stub_session_call` make no step in this sense, so they need neither, and none of them runs `require_scenario_home`, directly or through a function it calls (pinned in `tests/host-safety.test.ts`), so they work in shared mode too. The check refuses unless `SCENARIO_ROOT` is a directory and HOME is under it, as written and by real path. No harness step touches the invoking user's own `~/.config/agent-director`. An fmk script (a `TEST_NAME` carrying `-fmk-`) gets its own HOME, agent-director install and store, and tmux server under `SCENARIO_ROOT`, with the shim in front of the binary; Tests 5 to 12 and Test 29 keep the container's shared HOME and store, with no shim (`tests/README.md`, Scenario helper).
- Their config and credentials files are written inside the container; the scripts never read a config from the host.
- `bun test` does not run them. `tests/integration/session-leader.test.ts` is the one `bun test` file under `tests/integration/`: it spawns only a throwaway child and touches no CSCB state. The Slack stub server has no `bun test` suite of its own: Test 10 exercises it end to end, and Test 29 its push control, with the stub session's delivery record and tool calls.
- `tests/README.md` has the suite's layout, the run order (canonical order: test-1; then test-2, test-3 and test-4, each when present; then every other script by ascending number, so test-0 follows test-4; in each shard, test-1 first, then the shard's assigned scripts in canonical order; see its Script names, prerequisites and canonical order, and Sharding), the helper's contract and how to add a scenario. A new scenario sources the helper, which gives it its own scratch state dir, port and persona-name tag, bounded polling waits and a cleanup trap, and, for an fmk scenario, its own HOME, agent-director install and store, tmux server and shim. `tests/README.md` (Scenario helper, and Rules for every fmk scenario) describes both shims and required closing assertions: the three server-backed assertions for ordinary fmk scripts, or `assert_codex_d1_safety` for the exact six Codex D1 entrypoints.
- `CSCB_SLACK_API_URL` (the Slack API base URL override, honoured only for an `http://127.0.0.1…` or `http://[::1]…` URL) exists for the scenarios alone. The fmk scenarios (Test 0 and Tests 13 to 28) and Tests 10, 12 and 29 set it to point a live start at the Slack stub. A unit test passes an env object to `resolveSlackApiUrlOverride`, or a `slackApiUrl` to the connection manager or the option builders. The one case that sets it in `process.env`, to prove it is not read from there, restores it in `finally`.
- The test-only timer overrides (b.2nr). A scenario that would otherwise wait out a production timer shortens it with the `test_timer_overrides` block of the CSCB `config.json` it writes (`src/test-timer-overrides.ts`; see the architecture doc, Configuration). The block is never written into a user's configuration, and no user doc names it.
  - Scenarios 20 (`test-23-fmk-latch-recheck.sh`) and 24 (`test-26-fmk-timing-settings.sh`) use the keys below. Delivery and real-agent scenarios also use narrowly scoped overrides, including the existing E5 `delivery_stuck_after_ms`; do not shorten a timer whose actual duration the case asserts.
  - Scenario 20 sets `latch_recheck_interval_ms`. Scenario 24 sets `ad_version_recheck_interval_ms` and `dialog_ready_timeout_ms` in every leg, plus the two retry keys in the refused-stopping, refused-grace and call-timeout legs. The two retry keys are always set together.
  - Write the block in every start's `config.json`. The server reads it once, from the start-time configuration, so a leg that changes the set removes `config.json.last-applied` and restarts.
  - Require the one `TEST TIMER OVERRIDES ACTIVE` line after every start, equal to the printer's line for that set.
  - Never retype a value: print it through `fixtures/fmk-texts.ts`, which gives the effective value for the set the scenario passes. The entries are `testTimerOverridesBlock`, `buildTestTimerOverridesLine`, `effectiveTestTimer`, `adLaunchBoundMs` (B over a written table and, as one more argument, a floor override) and `unavailableRetryDueS` (a retry's due time, over the retry base and ceiling given together).
  - A leg whose assertion depends on a production timer keeps that timer at its default: test-26's stopping leg, where S's first retry must fall due after the stopping window.

### Asynchronous AD recovery assertions

- A harness poll and CSCB's retry timer are independent observers. For a launch-timeout recovery assertion, identify CSCB's first own `waiting` observation, its matching persona retry and that retry's first owned row read. Require the condition to end within the existing bound after that read, whether the harness sees `waiting` earlier or later; a later read cannot hide a delayed condition end. Test 17's executable shell controls cover both observer orders and the recorded CI timing shapes.
- An AD event's `caller_pid` names the AD CLI child. Match it to the shim's exact invocation PID and require the recorded CSCB parent, instance, operation, time window, event order and expected outcome. Use `ad_cscb_calls … with-pid` when that correlation is needed; its default projection stays six columns. Preserve the originating uncertain text and all refused Enters through the first successful Enter. Test 18's controls reject a server-PID match and unrelated children, including another child of the same server.
- For timed-out launch recovery, calculate B from AD's `launch_started_at`, then audit actual retry starts, re-arms and the persona's owned AD calls. Bound each timer phase from the preceding re-arm and require a checked kill in the first eligible retry; fail immediately if that retry ends without one. A retry can cross B during an AD call; accepting its deferral requires the production rule's own before-B answer. A nominal schedule or broad final deadline cannot prove these properties. Test 26's executable controls cover a missed retry, launch-start offsets, calls crossing B and bounded jitter.
- After proving recovery starts in that retry, allow separate bounded checked-kill confirmation, fresh-launch and applied-`SessionStart` phases, plus timer stop where the leg requires it. Keep genuine create errors, no kill before the AD-based B, successful checked-kill evidence, fresh AD terminal evidence and an owned row read before relaunch, and the recovered session's exact hook identity. Production-path tests with modeled AD outcomes do not establish native hook behavior; test 26's Docker scenario owns that proof.

### Safe Docker inspection

Agents MUST use the private inspection wrapper for Docker diagnostics, as required by root `AGENTS.md`. Supply credentials only through the environment; never print them or dump environment values. Use one of:

```sh
bun scripts/safe-docker-inspect.ts docker inspect NAME
bun scripts/safe-docker-inspect.ts docker container inspect NAME
bun scripts/safe-docker-inspect.ts docker image inspect NAME
```

The wrapper emits complete sanitized JSON with all recursive `Env` fields removed and copied credential/routing values masked. Only `--format '{{.Id}}'` and `--format '{{json .Id}}'` (or `-f` equivalents) are supported; these project locally after sanitization. Other formats, global Docker options and unsupported commands are refused before spawning Docker. Never bypass it with raw inspection through a tool, pipe or log, even with `--format`; on failure, stop inspection rather than fall back.

The CLI captures both streams privately with a 4 MiB combined input budget and a 30 s deadline, and bounds sanitized output too; failures emit neither stdout nor stderr. The API accepts a byte budget up to 16 MiB, a deadline up to 30 s and an abort signal, returning wrapper `code`, inspection `text`, `actualExitCode` (null when unobserved), sanitized `stderr`, `timedOut` and `outputTruncated`; every failure has empty `text`.

Incomplete or uncertifiable capture, including a byte cap, deadline, abort, read failure or cancellation, withholds child stderr behind a fixed nonempty reason while retaining available exit metadata; no secret prefix may escape. Snapshot monitors must retain the redacted command, wrapper and actual exit codes, and sanitized stderr or the withholding reason for every inspection attempt.

Wrapper exit codes are 1 (child/capture failure), 64 (refused request/options), 65 (invalid or unsafe data), 75 (byte limit), 124 (deadline) and 130 (cancellation). This protects wrapper callers, not unrelated shell commands, and leaves existing CI's narrow inspection contracts intact.

Only test 41 and the five named Codex heavy entrypoints select `assert_codex_d1_safety` as their closing assertion. Require no CSCB server or process record, positive harness spawn/send and agent-director tmux calls, no tmux outside the harness-to-agent-director chain, and no finished-row kills or deletes; empty or malformed logs fail. The assertion must pass in the script's own shell, and the EXIT trap rechecks the original logs after cleanup. Ordinary fmk scenarios keep all three server-backed closers.

Use `bun scripts/real-agent-matrix.ts --codex-only --output <fresh-private-directory>` for the explicit 15-trial local Codex selection. It skips Claude jobs, runtime-latest fetching and Claude credential admission, while retaining all Codex admission, result, token, interruption and retirement safeguards. Plan/results record `selection: codex-only`; Claude models and scheduler are null and Claude jobs are empty. Its verdict is `REAL-AGENT-CODEX-VERDICT PASS|FAIL <run-id>`, and `--plan` ends with `REAL-AGENT-CODEX-PLAN <run-id>` without accessing Codex auth/config. The default full selection stays at 73 invocations with `REAL-AGENT-VERDICT`; a focused PASS proves only the selected Codex trials.

Real-agent actions wait on AD `waiting` and typed refusals. Explicit `/compact` trials verify later exact receipts and durable queue progress; no pane or native compaction marker establishes lifecycle state. Diagnostics omit pane text.

AC15 waits for each submitted part's actual completed native acknowledgement before releasing its successor. The gate's crash audit combines the actual completed pre-compaction journal prefix with the surviving suffix; it never restores historical records into the production queue. Keep receipt identity, send-window, FIFO and completion checks across that boundary.

The child crash barrier publishes private mode-0600 checkpoints and an atomic marker through temporary-file rename. Its exact schema allows only bounded stages, booleans, PID/instance, durable first/successor IDs, counts and fixed error classes; no streams, arguments, bodies or arbitrary errors. Reads refuse symlinks, non-files, malformed/foreign data and records over 2,048 bytes. Read the marker before the checkpoint so publication cannot pair a new marker with an old checkpoint.

Within the original shared 180 s deadline, require both actual durable acceptances, successful text-call return and visible input. Record bounded child status/checkpoint/call-count diagnostics before SIGKILL or failure cleanup. The actual kill boundary stays after successful text and before Enter, with the native agent alive; verify SIGKILL and Enter-only durable recovery rather than simulating the crash.

Synthetic controls in `codex-delivery-controls.test.ts` cover delayed startup/successor acceptance, failed transport, early exit/signal, malformed/foreign/oversize/symlink checkpoints, atomic publication, marker-first snapshots, deadlines and diagnostics-before-kill. `codex-ai-controls.test.ts` holds successor parts until real completion and refuses failed acknowledgement; `codex-gate-controls.test.ts` checks the preserved prefix/suffix and rejects missing receipts, sends and FIFO violations. Host-safety audits pin the added helper and executable surfaces; controls supply no native acceptance.

On a failed native delivery wait, capture structured AD row and exact-receipt/journal evidence before cleanup. `transcript-receipts.ts` projects counts and send/receipt state with fixed reason classes; no composer observation, pane text, arbitrary parser errors, message bodies or credentials enter diagnostics. Preserve the original wait, FAIL and cleanup order; late readiness cannot pass.

Real-scenario expected counts and latency samples cover original Slack, cron and interject inputs. Reply reminders are counted separately but still require full receipt and FIFO evidence, one link to a previously delivered Slack input, and no recursive link. `transcript-receipts.test.ts` checks these rules with the real queue in scratch directories, including compacted history and deliberately damaged reminder evidence.
Scenario workload ordinals resolve through `workloadItem` over the cumulative receipt journal: open-head, key-retry, restart-spacing, reconnect and fidelity-size assertions use the original input's durable sequence even when reminders have been inserted. Selection does not remove reminders from receipt, FIFO or no-retype audits.

Test 26 polls for the exact existing recovery-get result within its existing `LOG_WAIT_S=30` before `get_line`. Keep the same exact count/outcome assertions and every timing bound. `fmk-launch-pending-dialog-wait.test.ts` covers delayed call/result publication, immediate completion, missing/wrong outcomes and duplicates.

Real scripts may retain independent transcript input/output assertions; no script consumes or retains native pane text. Their assertion evidence stays in the bot's transcript, CSCB server log, shim log and Slack stub record; observations and measurements print as `recorded:` lines. Do not write an artifact under `/test-results/`, loosen the exact matcher, change the original 15 wrapper cases, or automatically rerun a scenario. Heavy AI criteria require three independent successes per criterion; use the explicit selected model with native fallback disabled and the classifier enabled. Decision 19 requires real completed exact read-back for every AC11 case, including case_03, with no spoof obedience; every classifier-stop pass is withdrawn. Gate cases do not assert model prose.

Previous failed runs stay failed, and a historical case_03 classifier-stop success under the withdrawn rule cannot satisfy current acceptance.

Decision 19's completed diagnostics are recorded in [tests/README.md](../tests/README.md#heavy-inventory-and-invocation): fresh-conversation Opus trial 1 had 13 exact read-backs pass and two fail (case_03 classifier, case_12 double escaping). After listing 255 offered IDs, `azure/openai/gpt-6-astra` passed native compatibility and one all-15 diagnostic with strict case_03, exact entities, long-case and all 12 fresh-session proofs, zero classifier stops and zero duplicates. This is diagnostic evidence only; it establishes neither three independent successes nor release AC11 acceptance. Preserve the supplemental long-proof setup error and its unpaid saved-evidence replay as separate records.

The original other-55 run remains paused after the first 2.1.280 order-case FAIL: typing p95 2269.602 ms exceeds 2000 ms despite 53 FIFO receipts and zero duplicates; delivery p95 3869 ms meets its 10000 ms limit. Duplicate upgrade reads exhausted the unchanged 40-call/10-second budget. A deterministic sustained-load control with production upgrade wiring fails before the own-marker fast path (typing p95 2880 ms) and passes with it (1567 ms); the recorded-gap control also passes before the fix and is not a regression discriminator.

Native 2.1.280 latency remains unproved after this fix. Its remaining 54 invocations (36 non-AI, 18 AI; resolved latest 2.1.302) remain pending.

The previous focused AI results were 18 PASS and three FAIL: AC11/AC12/AC20/AC23/AC91/AC106 each passed 3 of 3 on actual `azure/openai/gpt-6-astra` and native 2.1.296 with fallback disabled and no classifier stops; all-`x` AC15 failed all three 180 s runs. The observed AC15 sequence after part one was an empty assistant turn, a native visible-response follow-up, then a permission-held `fetch_messages` request, leaving parts two/three unsent; no production defect is established and the roughly 15-second delay before the permission prompt remains unexplained.

The three subsequent all-`x` AC15 trials also failed (0 PASS, 3 FAIL) on actual `azure/openai/gpt-6-astra` and native 2.1.296, with all three parts delivered exactly in each trial, zero classifier stops and zero duplicates. Trial 1 returned the correct case/message count but a 5,338-character body instead of 5,000; trial 2 produced no valid required JSON; trial 3 returned short bodies of 1,869 and 1,631 characters. Preserve these failures separately from the earlier permission-held runs and retain the other six criteria's historical 3-of-3 results.

Unpaid actual-snapshot replay reaches trial 1's exact-body mismatch, not a pass. Decision 20 replaces only the repeated-`x` body with deterministic 5,000-byte ASCII words and punctuation (LCG32, seed `20261017`), no classifier bait and the original 1700/1700/1600-byte parts; generate one body for sending and private expected JSON, with the recipe, seed and body/part hashes recorded in `tests/real-agent/fixtures/input-audit.json` outside the model prompt.

Run three fresh independent AC15 trials on actual `azure/openai/gpt-6-astra` and native 2.1.296 with fallback disabled, preserving prompts, JSON schema, exact receipts/read-back, `messages:1`, response/tool discipline, bounds and all assertions. Start the full 58-invocation heavy run only after AC15 passes 3 of 3; if any AC15 trial fails, stop and report a one-line blocker with actual versus expected. No new paid acceptance has been established.

The full 58-invocation heavy run has not started and is not waived; the full `/ci` PASS on `fc763a36` predates these AC15 test changes. If no model offered by the CI key can run the cases, report that and stop without weakening assertions.

Real-agent actions wait on AD `waiting` and typed refusals. Explicit `/compact` trials verify later exact receipts and durable queue progress; no pane or native compaction marker establishes lifecycle state. Diagnostics omit pane text.

Test 32's down interval must block real spawn/resume before pausing, using counted `unknown-untyped` faults. Prove all 13 mixed-source acceptances with the real row ended and unchanged, every attempted launch refused before execution, and zero sends/deliveries; only then release the faults under lock with the server stopped and restart. Retain cumulative 13/33/53 delivery checks.

Test `test_mcp_drop_endpoint` only through internal fixtures: absent/false gives no route; true is captured at start, validates the actual peer and uses the SDK DELETE lifecycle. Never put it in shipped configuration or settings examples. Keep `delivery_stuck_after_ms` documented as the existing E5 override. Preserve the 3 s Slack ACK test separately from real delivery p95 limits of 2 s typing and 10 s receipt confirmation.

The gate’s downgrade oracle is PRD R55/review decision 15; `docs/migration.md` documents its four safety phases, including the native selection described in the shipped-description audit below. The Codex login and D1 harness contracts are described above; native acceptance remains separate from controls. A static/unit check or `--plan` result proves neither paid acceptance nor measured durations; record actual gate/heavy verdicts, duplicate/token reports and durations only after those runs.


#### E7 final acceptance evidence

The final settled integration tree passed the following checks on native Codex 0.159.2 and admitted host model `gpt-6-astra`. These results do not claim acceptance of the separate default Claude heavy matrix.

| Check | Actual final evidence |
|---|---|

The bounded native config checks use amended decision 22 above.

### The /ci-live runner

The `/ci-live` runner (`ci-live/`, see `docker/README.md`) has its own dependency, `playwright-core`, installed only in `ci-live/node_modules`. Its unit tests live in `tests/` like every other suite.

- Import only from `ci-live/lib/`, `ci-live/provision/`, `ci-live/checks/` and `ci-live/dry-run/`. Never import `ci-live/run.ts`, `ci-live/main.ts`, `ci-live/maintenance.ts`, `ci-live/runtime/` or `ci-live/browser/` (`driver.ts` and the flows): they load `playwright-core`, directly or through `main.ts` or `ci-live/runtime/workspace.ts`, so `bun test` and `bun run typecheck` would fail on a clone where the runner's dependency is not installed. Put logic a test must reach in `ci-live/lib/` or `ci-live/provision/`, as the memory watchdog (`lib/memory-watchdog.ts`), the copy of the container's own logs (`lib/container-logs.ts`), the stray-app rules (`provision/app-listing.ts`) and the prompt guard (`checks/prompt-guard.ts`, driven through `PromptGuardDeps` with a fake trail and the shared fake clock) are.
- Put no `*.test.ts` or `*.spec.ts` file under `ci-live/`: `bun test` would pick it up.
- A runner file loads nothing from outside `ci-live/` except `src/`'s two import-free text modules, `src/reload-preview-clauses.ts` (the reload preview's retired clauses) and `src/startup-summary-ending.ts` (the start summary's ending builder), and no package but `node:*` builtins and `playwright-core`. The runner runs on the host and `ci-live/tsconfig.json` typechecks every file it reaches: any other `src/` module would load the Slack SDKs, the agent-director client or `bun:sqlite`. Neither module may import anything. The checks build the texts these modules cover from them, never a literal copy; other `src/` texts the checks expect stay copies, each pinned against `src/` in `tests/ci-live-checks.test.ts`. `tests/ci-live-docker.test.ts` enforces the load rule by a source audit.
- Drive I/O through the runner's seams (`SecureFs` for the secret store, `ScanFs` for the secrecy scan, `LockDeps` for the run lock's file access and PID liveness test and a collecting warning sink for its stale-lock `WARNING`, the spawn function for docker and the host probes, the injected fetch and clock; `ContainerLogsSink` for the copy of the container's logs (an in-memory sink, or `containerLogsSink` over a `mkdtempSync` results dir) with a fake `ContainerExec` for the container and the shared fake clock as the collector's `clock` (`CollectorClock`), which a caller's wait runs on; for the memory watchdog, its `WatchdogSources` and `WatchdogClock`, the file reader `readHostCgroup` takes and the `/proc` reader, `ProcReader`). A test never reads `~/.config/cscb-test/` or the host's cgroup files, takes the real run lock, starts a container or reaches Slack.
- The code no test may import (`ci-live/run.ts`, `ci-live/main.ts`, `ci-live/maintenance.ts`, `ci-live/runtime/`, `ci-live/browser/`) is pinned by the source audit in `tests/ci-live-docker.test.ts`: its environment reads, spawn sites, host commands (the HOST check's two read-only probes the only host-side agent-director and tmux argv), the staged agent-director binary (selected only through `selectAgentDirectorBinary`, copied into a fresh temp dir removed in a `finally`, never in a spawn or docker argv), the tmux and agent-director commands inside its `bash -c` scripts (collected by `shellScriptCommands`, with a script it cannot read on a closed list of one), docker subcommands (the exact `docker stats` and `docker stop` argv included), leftover removal, the run lock's acquires and their warning sinks, signal handling (`main.ts`'s `stopRun` and `maintenance.ts`'s stop), the memory watchdog's wiring, the cleanup order, the copy of the container's own logs awaited before every removal or stop of the container (so a failed check can still be traced once the container is gone) and sealed before the closing scan, `appsCommand`'s order, the prompt guard's wiring, the browser's bounds in `browser/driver.ts` (`ensureSignedIn`'s idle and the one-at-a-time flows included) and each account's sign-in-code wiring (its own address for the mailbox, `ensureSignedIn`'s password and emailed-code branches, the second address's `addressForms` registered with the redactor, `openSessions`' fixed skip reason). A change there that adds a host command, a `bash -c` tmux or agent-director command, a spawn site, a lock acquire, an environment read or a removal or stop of the container, or reorders a pinned sequence, fails the audit; widen or update the audit for that one item in the same change, and never loosen a pattern to get it green.
- Correlate delivery evidence through `ci-live/checks/delivery-evidence.ts` (b.omv SR-13.6): `deliveryLines` matches complete persona/source/channel/timestamp fields and `waitDeliveryLines` waits for the receipt diagnostic that can follow a Slack reply. Assert the expected sender where the check requires it. Never select a delivered item by body text in a server log; transcript-only `tagstext` remains available for edited messages.
- Use `ci-live/checks/agent-control.ts` for Check 12's terminal stop/lift and Check 18's permission-prompt input (b.ou8). It sends an optional Escape through agent-director, polls `status` for `waiting` within `REPLY_TIMEOUT_MS`, then attempts one `send-keys --text` submission; agent-director appends Enter. Keep its permission/dialog guards: no pending or pane-hash override, screen-based readiness check or automatic resend after refused or uncertain input. Check 7 injects its fault with agent-director `kill` and requires `kill_sent === true`; that confirms the fault was sent, while the existing reconnect, log and Slack-reply assertions still prove recovery.
- Test agent control through `fakeContainer`, `makeCtx` and `virtualClock` in `tests/ci-live-checks.test.ts`: literal text quoting, Escape before status polling, waiting after pending/working states, refused or malformed status, bounded waiting and no text resend. Retain the end-to-end delivery and permission assertions when changing the control mechanism.
- `queueEvidenceScript` projects metadata inside the container: full acceptances, compacted delivered keys, original sequences, sent attempts, receipts, confirmed items and outstanding counts. It confirms a full item only when every fixed part has a receipt, and recognizes a compacted Slack key by channel, timestamp and delivered time. Only the projection leaves the container. Check 24 retains the original timestamp immediately and records its accepted sequence, proves one outstanding item with no premature send/receipt/reaction/restart and the exact fixed E5 waiting notice, then blocks later checks until that same item has a transcript tag, matching delivered diagnostic and no outstanding parts after recovery. A new successful post cannot satisfy teardown.
- Drive those checks with `fakeContainer`, `makeCtx`, scripted human sessions and `virtualClock`; use `assertNoLeak` plus body sentinels. Exercise wrong persona/channel/timestamp, early receipt, incorrect notice body, duplicates and failed original-item recovery. Keep the helper free of production adapter imports on the host.
- The runner's tokens are fakes built at runtime, as everywhere under `tests/` (see Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks); `tests/secrecy-audit.test.ts` rejects a token literal.

#### Remaining agent-director dependencies (b.ou8)

The migration is partial. The following existing paths remain unresolved; do not extend their lower-layer access or weaken their assertions to complete the migration. The [two cardinal rules](engineering-guide.md#the-two-cardinal-rules) govern tests and fixtures too.

- **Native idle compaction (B10):** `tests/integration/fixtures/native-idle-compaction.ts` still interprets native binary fragments. agent-director must own compatibility detection and expose structured support/disabled/unknown reasons and current-launch observations for native compaction before this fixture can migrate. Preserve the real compaction boundary, launch/session provenance, timing bounds and queued-delivery assertions; a version string or generic idle state cannot establish them.
- **Process continuity (Check 13):** its existing pane-PID comparisons require an agent-director observation that verifies the same live launch/process before and after both in-place reloads, with PID-reuse protection and an explicit unknown/unavailable outcome. An unchanged row ID, session ID or launch timestamp alone does not prove that process survived. Preserve the one-row, server-PID, routing, receipt and remembered-word assertions as well.
- **Diagnostic pane capture (B11/B12):** `ci-live/lib/container-logs.ts` still captures panes through tmux. agent-director's [v0.14.0 caller contract](https://github.com/gabemahoney/agent-director/blob/v0.14.0/docs/caller-contract.md) permits `read-pane` only while `pending`, at most once a second; replacing arbitrary working/permission-state captures with that verb would still violate the contract. Request an agent-director-owned diagnostic surface with supported states, ownership checks, bounds and explicit refusal/unknown results. Treat its output only as diagnostics, never as readiness or delivery evidence.

### Slack intake proof in containers (b.omv SR-13.6)

Run `tests/integration/test-31-fmk-ack-burst.sh` only through `/ci`. It submits
1,000 distinct events concurrently to one silent stub persona, correlates
every WebSocket send with its actual ACK by envelope ID, generated socket
UUID and label, and requires every monotonic pre-send-to-ACK interval to be
at most 3,000 ms. Its pass state intentionally keeps all 1,000 items
outstanding, with no typing, receipts, receipt reactions or model calls.
Before asserting the deadline, it prints all 1,000 numeric elapsed intervals
in push order, their maximum and p95, and over-limit and negative counts,
so a deadline failure retains the complete timing evidence.
This is separate from the queue store's real-file accept p99 and the
real-agent framework's transcript latency. Record actual script duration
from `/ci`, not from the host's static checks.

Test 29 compares the exact `message-received` Counter over all stopped
writers' log rotations with the complete accepted queue set, preserving
pre-compaction evidence across restart. Cover paired-event dedupe, both
shared recipients, direct DM and every refused input/nonrecipient. Keep its
E5 metadata and transcript export. See the Slack stub and acknowledgement
burst sections in `tests/README.md` for timing fields and proof boundaries.

## Fixture Patterns

### Factory Functions

Every test file defines factory functions that create fixtures with sensible defaults and optional overrides:

```
makePersonaConfig(overrides?, baseDir?) — a resolved PersonaConfig with one default persona
makeMultiPersonaConfig(specs, baseDir, overrides?) — a resolved PersonaConfig with one persona per spec
makeTransport() — minimal transport stub
makeServer() — minimal MCP server stub
```

Always use factory functions instead of hardcoding fixture values in individual tests. When a new field is added to a type, update the factory function — all tests automatically pick up the default.

Reload-controller tests build their configuration directory, personas and runs with `makeReloadHarness` (see Reload Tests), not by hand.

Persona-loader tests, and tests of persona-keyed code such as `spawnForPersona`, `startupSessionManager` and `launchSession` in `src/session-manager.ts`, use the shared persona-config helper, `tests/test-helpers/persona-config.ts`. It is the only config builder; the one config fixture set is the upgrade suite's 0.11.1 configs under `tests/fixtures/upgrade-0.11.1/` (`readme-example.json`, `two-personas.json`; see the upgrade-0-11-1.test.ts row), and a route-keyed config appears only as raw JSON in a pre-persona rejection case. The CLI's `loadConfig` dependency returns a resolved `PersonaConfig`, so CLI tests build it with these helpers too. The persona-config helper provides:

```
makePersona(overrides?, baseDir?) — one PersonaInput entry in file form; default paths sit under baseDir
makeFungiblePersona(overrides?, baseDir?) — one PersonaInput entry in file form for fungible mode; paths as makePersona's
makePersonaConfigInput(overrides?, baseDir?) — a PersonaConfigInput file-form config with one default persona
makePersonaConfig(overrides?, baseDir?) — a resolved PersonaConfig, as loadPersonaConfig returns it, except mcp_config_path (see below)
makeMultiPersonaConfig(specs, baseDir, overrides?) — a resolved PersonaConfig with one persona per PersonaSpec, in order
makeStandInPersonaConfig(personas, baseDir, overrides?) — makeMultiPersonaConfig for stand-in personas keyed and named by one channel ID each
writeConfigFile(dir, input) — writes input as JSON to <dir>/config.json and returns the path
```

The config builders (`makePersonaConfigInput`, `makePersonaConfig`, `makeMultiPersonaConfig`, `makeStandInPersonaConfig`) take the switch (`allow_invited_channels`) through their server-wide overrides; the `invited` section goes through the persona overrides of `makePersona` and `makeFungiblePersona` or a `PersonaSpec` of `makeMultiPersonaConfig` and `makeStandInPersonaConfig` (b.deo SRI-1203). `makePersonaConfigInput` and `makePersonaConfig` take no persona overrides: their overrides are config-level, and they build their one default persona with none. The defaults keep declarative mode: the switch is absent in file form and `false` resolved, and no persona has an `invited` key. `makeFungiblePersona` writes no `channels` and no top-level `permission_prompts`, sends prompts to a channel ID as its fungible destination (`invited.permission_prompts`) and keeps DMs off; written under a switch set to `true`, it loads. `makePersonaConfigInput` and `makePersonaConfig` build their default persona with it when the overrides set the switch to `true`. The resolved builders fill each persona's `fungible_destination` and `sections` as the loader fills them in the mode the switch picks (`channelModeOf`): `sections` holds `channels`, `permission_prompts` and `invited` as written; in fungible mode the resolved `channels` are empty, `permission_prompts` is undefined and `fungible_destination` is `invited.permission_prompts`, `"dm"` when that is absent.

Use `makeMultiPersonaConfig` when a test needs several personas. Each `PersonaSpec` is any resolved `Persona` field except `index`; `overrides` sets the server-wide fields as for `makePersonaConfig`.

- It runs no validation, so a stand-in persona can set `key` to a channel ID directly.
- In declarative mode each persona defaults to its own `all` channel, with prompts to that channel and DMs off. A spec with no channels gets `permission_prompts: 'dm'` but DMs still off, a pair the loader rejects; set `dm` in that spec yourself.
- In fungible mode each persona is filled as the resolved builders fill it (above), keeping a spec's own `channels` and `permission_prompts`; DMs stay off. A spec's own `fungible_destination` or `sections` replaces the filled one.
- It throws when two personas share a name or a key.
- `baseDir` is required; pass the test's `mkdtempSync` directory.

`makeStandInPersonaConfig` is kept for older suites whose fixtures key personas by channel ID; new tests use `makeMultiPersonaConfig` with real persona names. Each record key is a channel ID that becomes the persona's `name` and `key`, its one `all` channel and its `permission_prompts` target (in fungible mode, its `invited.permission_prompts` instead); the record value sets further fields such as `working_directory`. Personas keep record order, and everything else, including the required `baseDir`, is as for `makeMultiPersonaConfig`.

For the other helpers, `baseDir` defaults to the OS temp directory; pass the test's own `mkdtempSync` directory when paths must exist or be unique to the test. `writeConfigFile` writes only into the caller-supplied temp directory and has no default location. Pass `loadPersonaConfig` a temp home as well, so `~` never expands to the real home. Remove both the temp directory and the temp home in `afterEach`.

Build agent-director rows in tests with the canned-row helpers in `tests/test-helpers/agent-director-stub.ts` (`cannedListRow`, `cannedGetResult`, `cannedGetResultPlural`), never by hand. Every row they build is persona-labelled (b.av2 SR-13.4 as amended by b.deo SRI-1203, SRI-1204):

- **Default form** — `cannedListRow({ instance_id })`: the row stands for the persona whose key is the instance ID without its `cscb_` prefix. Labels are exactly `service=cscb` and `persona=<key>` (no `config_dir`, no `channel`), tmux `slack_bot_<key>`, and `cwd` is `defaultCannedRowCwd()`, the working directory of the default persona fixture (`makePersona()`, `<tmpdir>/personas/test_bot/work`; nothing creates it). The stub client's default `get` row uses this form. Use it for permission-poller and click-handler rows, and pair it with a `getPersona` stub that knows that key.
- **Persona form** — `cannedListRow(overrides, persona, home)`, likewise `cannedGetResult` and `cannedGetResultPlural`.

A `channel` label appears only in explicit negative rows that test the rejection of pre-persona rows (the poller skipping a row with no `persona` label, the start sweep killing a live one and keeping it): override `labels` to carry only the old `channel` label.

For the persona form, pass a resolved persona (e.g. from `makePersonaConfig`). The row then carries what a spawn of that persona writes: `cwd` = its working directory, `cscb_<key>`, `slack_bot_<key>`, and the `service`, `persona` and `config_dir` labels. The collision ladder and the start sweep replace a row whose `cwd` or `config_dir` label doesn't match (b.av2 SR-6.2 as amended by b.jg5 SRJ-1503 and SRJ-1504, SR-6.3: the ladder with no row deleted, a finished row by a reuse spawn of the same id and a live row through the live-row sequence first), so a fixture meant to reach a resume, reconnect or wait path must use this form. `home` is required and the persona form throws without it; there is no OS-home fallback. The `config_dir` label must be computed under the same home the session manager's `_setSpawnHomeDir` seam uses, or it won't match the one the code computes, so pass that home. To test a guard, override `cwd` or `labels` explicitly. `spawnForPersona` joins a launch already in flight for the same key, so a suite that can leave a launch unsettled calls `_resetInFlightLaunches()` in `afterEach`.

To test that only one launch per persona is in flight, wrap the stub with `holdSpawns(stub, shouldHold?)` from the same file. Each `spawn` whose instance ID passes `shouldHold` (all by default) stays open until the test settles it; other spawns go to the stub's original `spawn`. The handle exposes `calls` (every spawn received), `held()` (instance IDs still open), `entered(id)` (resolves once a spawn for `id` is issued), `release(id)` and `fail(id, err)` (settle the oldest held spawn for `id`), and `releaseAll()` for teardown.

To keep a live-row sequence's `find-missing` run outstanding while a case drives other work (the start pass, an apply, a stop, a lost message; b.jg5 SRJ-706), wrap the stub with `holdFindMissing(stub)` from the same file (`FindMissingHold`). Every `find-missing` call stays open until the test settles it, oldest first: the handle exposes `calls` (every call received since the hold, in order; held calls are not in the stub's own call log), `heldCount()`, `entered(count?)` (resolves once that many calls, 1 by default, have been received), `release(result)` (resolve the oldest with a placement built with `cannedFindMissing`), `fail(err)` (reject the oldest) and `releaseAll()` (resolve every held call with the empty result) for cleanup.

To assert which agent-director calls a case made (for example, none for a persona that is not up), use the shared stub call-log helper in the same file rather than a hand-rolled recorder, so every suite reads the stub's calls the same way.

To stand in for `resolveSystemBinary` (the start gate's system-install lookup, the runtime version re-check's resolve, `/publish`'s SR-2.5 check), use `makeStubResolveSystemBinary(opts?)` from the same file; no test runs a binary to learn a version. It returns an `(opts?) => Promise<{ path, version }>` function. With no options every call resolves with `PHASE1_RC_VERSION`, which passes CSCB's Phase 1 floor, at `/usr/local/bin/agent-director`; `path` changes that path, `version` the version of every call, and `throws` rejects every call with that error. `outcomes` answers the calls in order, one entry per call, and once the list runs out its last entry answers every later call: `{ version, path? }` resolves with that version (at `path`, else the builder's `path`), `{ throws }` rejects with that error, and `{ never: true }` returns a promise that never settles, for a time-limit case. `outcomes` given with `throws` or `version`, or empty, throws when the stub is built. `calls` records each call's argument, a call that never settles included.

**agent-director errors.** Build an agent-director error in a test with the stub's builder wherever one exists; every builder builds from its class binding, the three Phase-1-only ones included. For an error the stub has no builder for, or a description or verb no builder takes, a test may use `new` on the client's own class imported from `src/agent-director-errors.ts` (for `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, `ErrTmuxKillFailed` and `ErrTmuxSessionConflict`, its binding there; never a named import, re-export or destructuring of one from `agent-director`). The classifier decides by class, so a base `AgentDirectorError` named like a class is not that class: use one only as a look-alike, expecting UNCLASSIFIED. A test of a site's error handling builds the error with the stub builders and imports the class it expects from `src/ad-error-class.ts` (`AD_ERROR_CLASS_*`), never a literal label. Assert a call-capture array with `toHaveLength`: Bun's `toEqual([])` also passes on `[undefined]`.

Each builder uses the installed client's own class, except:

- `errGeneric(verb, errName, message?)`, which builds the base `AgentDirectorError` for any `errName`; a name CSCB gives no handling is UNCLASSIFIED, a refusal at every launch site (b.jg5 SRJ-313), so a case that wants a launch failure uses `errTmuxSessionCreate`, never an invented name or `errGeneric` with a class's name;
- `errAmbiguousRequest()` and `errPermissionRequestNotFound()`, which build the base `AgentDirectorError` with the error's `errName` although the client exports a class for each, because the code under test recognises both by `errName`.

The builders of the three Phase-1-only errors (`errTmuxUnresponsive*`, `errTmuxKillFailed`, `errTmuxSessionConflict`) use `new` on `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, `ErrTmuxKillFailed` and `ErrTmuxSessionConflict` as `src/agent-director-errors.ts` re-exports them (the pinned client's own classes), each value carrying its verb, its name as `errName` (the `ERR_TMUX_*_NAME` constant) and its description.

The description words CSCB matches come from `src/ad-description-phrases.ts`, and the builders take them from there. A test that checks for one imports the constant from that module, never a literal. The builders' default names are the default persona fixture's: `STUB_TMUX_SESSION_NAME`, `STUB_INSTANCE_ID`, and `STUB_TMUX_SOCKET_PATH` for the socket.

- **`ErrTmuxUnresponsive` (class binding)**: `errTmuxUnresponsive(verb?, description?)` (verb `resume`; `status` only reads the store and never returns it; by default a tmux call that did not answer and did nothing). The variants CSCB tells apart: `errTmuxUnresponsiveLaunchTimeout(verb?, instanceId?)` (verb `spawn`, or `resume`; carries `LAUNCH_TIMEOUT_PHRASE`), `errTmuxUnresponsiveStillStopping(verb?, sessionName?)` and `errTmuxUnresponsiveStillStarting(verb?, sessionName?)` (verb `resume`, session `STUB_TMUX_SESSION_NAME`; the description names the quoted session name, as agent-director's does, and carries `STILL_STOPPING_PHRASE` or `STILL_STARTING_PHRASE`), `errTmuxUnresponsiveAfterDuplicateSession(verb?)` (verb `resume`; tmux answered "duplicate session" and the session holding the name could not be read, so nothing was started), and `errTmuxUnresponsiveNewRowEnded(verb?, sessionName?)` (verb `spawn`, session `STUB_TMUX_SESSION_NAME`; HO rev 26, b.jg5 SRJ-111: a plain spawn's `tmux new-session` answered "duplicate session" and its re-lookup of the holder did not answer, so agent-director ended the new row; the description names the quoted session and carries `NEW_ROW_ENDED_PHRASE` and the retry `NEW_ROW_ENDED_RETRY`, the stub's own export, "retry with reuse_finished once the session name is free", since `src/` keys nothing on it and holds no constant for it; it carries none of `LAUNCH_TIMEOUT_PHRASE`, `STILL_STOPPING_PHRASE` or `STILL_STARTING_PHRASE`, so it is no launch timeout).
- **`ErrTmuxKillFailed` (class binding)**: `errTmuxKillFailed(sessionName?, description?, pids?, options?)`, verb `kill`. The descriptions follow agent-director 0.12.0's wording (`pkg/api/kill_errors.go`): each is the full text the client delivers, starting `tmux: agent process still running: instance <id>: tmux session "<name>": ` (the id from the file-local `killFailedInstanceId`: `cscb_<key>` for a persona's session `slack_bot_<key>`, else `STUB_INSTANCE_ID`), and ends "retry kill later; never delete this row" (`RETRY_KILL_LATER_PHRASE`, `NEVER_DELETE_ROW_PHRASE`), and a kill sent is "a kill was sent to the agent's pane and to its labelled session" (the stub models only that opening). `description` is a `KillFailedDescription`: `'outlived-exit-wait'` (the default; a kill was sent, and "the agent process (pid N)" was still running after the kill exit wait of 5 s), `'pane-process-survived'` (a kill was sent, and the survivor clause was or were still running after it), `'unverifiable-session-present'` (a kill was sent, but the agent process cannot be checked and its labelled session is still there) or `'no-session-no-kill'` (no session or pane of this launch was found while its agent process still runs "(pid N)", so no kill was sent, with the "Operator actions" pointer). N is `STUB_WORKER_PID` (4194350, above Linux's largest pid and distinct from every pid of `STUB_SURVIVOR_PIDS`). Only `'pane-process-survived'` carries the survivor clause: for one of `pids`, `SURVIVOR_CLAUSE_ONE_PHRASE` then "(pid S)"; for several, `SURVIVOR_CLAUSE_MANY_PHRASE` then "(pids S1, S2)", joined with ", " (default `STUB_SURVIVOR_PIDS`, one fake pid above Linux's largest), the form `SURVIVOR_PID_PATTERN` matches and `survivorPids` lists in order; the other three ignore `pids` and name no survivor. `options` is `KillFailedOptions`: `workerAlsoRunning: true`, with `'pane-process-survived'` only, puts "the agent process (pid N)" and " and " before the survivor clause, with "were still running", the form agent-director writes when the worker and survivors both outlived the wait. The builder checks its own text with `survivorPids` and throws if the survivor pids it reads are not exactly those given (none for the other three, with or without the option for the survivor description), if `'pane-process-survived'` is given no pid, or if `workerAlsoRunning` is given with another description. `KILL_FAILED_DESCRIPTIONS` lists all four for `test.each`.
- **`ErrTmuxSessionConflict` (class binding)**: `errTmuxSessionConflict(verb, conflictCase, sessionName?, options?)`. `conflictCase` is a `ConflictCase`, one per case of ADSRD SR-1.4: `no-valid-id`, `different-id`, `another-store`, `own-id`, `leftover`, `scan-leftover` (the pre-spawn scan's refusal: "left over from an earlier life", the session's tmux id `STUB_TMUX_SESSION_ID`, "nothing was written and no row was created"), `duplicate-session-leftover` (a plain spawn at "duplicate session": "its label names this instance id", "left over from an earlier life", "the new row was ended"), `not-this-launch`, `pane-not-found` (also "this row's own id"), `conflicting-labels`, `never-reported-in` (also "this row's own id" and "no kill was sent"), and `unrecognised`, which carries none of the case words. `not-this-launch` from `kill` also carries "this row's own id" and "no kill was sent". `CONFLICT_CASES` lists all twelve. Every description carries the `agent-director list --tmux-session-name <quoted name>` line. `different-id` and `another-store` ("another agent-director store") say the session is another row's (another store's) agent and must not be ended, and point to no "Operator actions"; every other description points a human to "Operator actions". `options` is a `ConflictOptions` selecting the variants the positional form cannot: `plainSpawn` on `different-id` or `another-store` (a plain spawn at "duplicate session": also "its label does not name this instance id" and "the new row was ended"), `notAdopted` on `pane-not-found` (a lost create reply: also "the agent's pane was not adopted"), `scan` on `conflicting-labels` (the pre-spawn scan found an agent-director label value set at the server, global or global-window scope: also "nothing was written and no row was created"); an option set on any other case throws. No description names a command that ends a session; "no kill was sent" is the one mention of a kill. The case words and extras come from `src/ad-description-phrases.ts`.
