A test that iterates the latch's conflict cases uses `CONFLICT_CASE_ROWS` from `conflict-cases.ts` (under Other shared fixture builders), which covers every entry of `CONFLICT_CASES`.
- **0.10.0 classes**: `errTmuxNotAvailable(socketPath?, verb?)` (verb `spawn`; with `socketPath` that socket is not accessible to this user, without it tmux could not be run); `errTmuxNotAvailableDifferentServer(socketPath?, verb?)` (verb `resume`, socket `STUB_TMUX_SOCKET_PATH`; other single-row verbs that call tmux, such as `read-pane`, also return it, `status` never; carries `DIFFERENT_TMUX_SERVER_PHRASE`); `errTmuxNotAvailableNewRowEnded(verb?, sessionName?)` (verb `spawn`; the ENVIRONMENT form of `errTmuxUnresponsiveNewRowEnded`: tmux could not be run for the re-lookup, with the same words, `NEW_ROW_ENDED_PHRASE` and `NEW_ROW_ENDED_RETRY`, HO rev 26); `errTmuxCaptureFailed(sessionName?, verb?)` (verb `read-pane`; the session is gone); `errSpawnNotPausable(verb?)` (verb `pause`); `errSystemInstallDisappeared(verb?, binaryPath?)` (verb `status`, path `STUB_RESOLVE_DEFAULT_PATH`). Beside `errSpawnNotInteractive(verb?)`, `errSpawnNotInteractiveLeftover(sessionName?, verb?)` (the session is a leftover of an earlier launch) and `errSpawnNotInteractiveNoLaunchStart(verb?)` (a `pending` row with no launch start), both verb `send-keys`. `errSendKeysWhileRelayed()` builds the client's `ErrSendKeysWhileRelayed` (verb `send-keys`: agent-director refuses keys to a row that sits on a relayed permission prompt, and nothing was sent), which classes UNCLASSIFIED (b.jg5 SRJ-104, SRJ-118); use it rather than `errGeneric` with that name. `errInvalidFlags(verb?)` builds the client's `ErrInvalidFlags` (verb `decide`; pass `spawn` or `resume` for a launch verb's refusal). `errCwdNotFound(verb?, cwd?)` and `errCwdNotADirectory(verb?, cwd?)` (verb `spawn`, `cwd` `/x`) build the client's directory errors, and `errTmuxSessionCreateStaysPending(verb?)` (verb `spawn`) an `ErrTmuxSessionCreate` whose description ends with agent-director's sentence that the row could not be restored and stays pending (`RESTORE_SENTENCE_STAYS_PENDING`), built through `withRestoreSentence`. `RESTORE_SENTENCES` holds HO rev 28's four restore sentences that can end a failed `resume`'s or reuse's description (restored to its prior state; changed after the move and left as it is; removed after the move, so nothing restored; could not be restored and stays pending), and `withRestoreSentence(err, sentence)` gives `err` again, same class, verb and name, its description ending with `sentence`, so it classifies as `err` does. CSCB matches no part of any of them (the retry's read of the row decides), and a case's row stays as the case scripts it, never derived from the sentence.
- **No class in any client**: an unknown name arrives as `ErrUnknownErrorName`, built as the client builds it: `unknownName` is the name and `envelope` is the binary's `{ err_name, err_description }`. The client's own `verb` and `errDescription` stay as it sets them, so read the binary's description from `envelope.err_description`. `errUnknownErrorName(unknownName?, description?)` builds any name (default `ErrFromALaterBinary`); `errInternal(description?)` an `ErrInternal`; `errSchemaMismatch(description?)` an `ErrSchemaMismatch` (by default the store could not be opened), while the other two store-open names, `ErrSchemaMigrationRequired` and `ErrStoreOpen`, are built with `errUnknownErrorName` and the `ERR_*_NAME` constants from `src/agent-director-errors.ts`; `errUnusableName(fault?)` the unusable-recorded-name `ErrInternal`, carrying `UNUSABLE_RECORDED_NAME_PHRASE`, where `fault` is an `UnusableNameFault` (`'empty'`, the default, `'control-character'` or `'stored-differently'`, all in `UNUSABLE_NAME_FAULTS`; `tests/ad-error-class.test.ts` and `tests/conflict-latch.test.ts` iterate `UNUSABLE_NAME_FAULTS` directly, and the session-manager, server, restart and health-check cases take their UNUSABLE NAME answers from `UNUSABLE_NAME_CASE_ROWS` in `conflict-cases.ts`, which crosses it with every site kind); `errConfigMalformed(key?, value?, minimum?, unit?)` an `ErrConfigMalformed` in agent-director's refusal form, `config <path>: refused [tmux] values: [tmux] <key> = <value>, below its safe minimum <minimum> <unit>. A missing key, or 0, gives the default.` (default `starting_session_seconds` = `30` below `60` `s`; `unit` is `s` or `ms`).
- **Every UNAVAILABLE form**: `UNAVAILABLE_FORMS` is the one table of UNAVAILABLE forms (b.jg5 SRJ-104), each an `UnavailableForm` tuple `[label, make(verb), causeKind]`: `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` and its three variants (`ErrTmuxUnresponsive, still stopping`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive, still starting`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive, launch timeout`), `ErrCallTimeout`, `ErrUnknownErrorName`, `a wrapped UnknownError` (CSCB's `UnknownError` wrapper), `ErrTmuxKillFailed` (cause `kill-failed`; only a kill answers it) and `a plain Error`, every other form with cause `unavailable`. A file takes its subset with `unavailableForms(...picks)`, in the order given: a label picks its row as is, a `[label, as]` pair relabels it, and a label the table lacks throws. Never write a file-local copy of the table.

**Dead-session causes, escalate-dead verdicts and dead evidence (b.jg5 SRJ-611).** Tests import the causes (`DEAD_SESSION_CAUSE_*`), the prompt-row/reconnect verdicts (`ESCALATE_DEAD_ROW_ABSENT_AT_PANE_READ` included), the evidence texts (`ESCALATE_DEAD_EVIDENCE`) and the evidence helpers (`isDeadEvidence`, `carriedDeadEvidenceOf`, `CARRIED_DEAD_EVIDENCE_NONE`, `DEAD_EVIDENCE_NONE`, `describeDeadEvidence`) from `src/session-manager.ts`, and iterate the exported lists `DEAD_SESSION_CAUSES` and `ESCALATE_DEAD_VERDICTS` rather than a copy of their values, so a new cause or verdict is covered by every case that walks them. The expected answer of each is `DEAD_EVIDENCE_OF` (`tests/test-helpers/dead-evidence.ts`), the one table of which cause or verdict is dead evidence: keyed by `DeadEvidenceSource`, so a new value fails the typecheck until it has an answer there, and written out by hand, never asked of the code under test. `tests/session-manager.test.ts`'s SRJ-611 describe alone checks `isDeadEvidence` against it; other suites (`tests/restart.test.ts`) read it for what a cause or verdict leads to and never re-assert `isDeadEvidence`. Expected lines come from their builders (`deadSessionRouteLine`, `deadEvidenceVoidedLine` with `deadEvidenceVoidingRead` or a `DEAD_EVIDENCE_VOIDED_BY_*` text, `waitRowFinishedLine`, `waitRowAbsentLine`, `promptRowSweepFinishedLine`, `joinedLaunchDropsDeadEvidenceLine`, `relaunchWithoutKillLine` with its reason, `killVoidsVerdictLine`), each pinned once as a literal. A cause's GONE-ness (`DEAD_EVIDENCE_OF`) never changes with the path; whether it leads to a kill or a sequence also depends on the path's last read, since dead evidence covers one life: a read of the row as `ended`, `missing` or gone voids every piece the path holds, and a reconnect's `row-not-interactive` voids a carried verdict. So a session-manager case states, in its hand-written row, whether the path still holds live evidence at the not-resumable step (`liveEvidence`) and which pieces it voided, in line order (`voided`); a restart case crosses each verdict with each `dead` re-probe, and expects no kill after a first probe or re-probe that read a row: installation disappearance reads unknown and never kills or relaunches. Regression cases cover first probes, re-probes and legacy dead-shaped readings, preserving outage and recovery counters until fresh authoritative AD evidence arrives. A restart-path escalate-dead answer is built as the adapter builds it, `{ outcome: 'escalate-dead', deadEvidence: carriedDeadEvidenceOf(verdict) }` (`ReconnectEscalateDead`). Build every carried value through `carriedDeadEvidenceOf` (or `CARRIED_DEAD_EVIDENCE_NONE`): `CarriedDeadEvidence` carries a type-only brand, so an object literal fails the typecheck. The one exception is the `handBuilt` helper in `tests/session-manager.test.ts`, which casts past the brand to pin the builders' run-time shape and to hand the code a deliberately inconsistent value (an `evidence` flag its source does not prove); never copy it into another file.

**Phase 1 results.** The Phase 1 result fields `kill_sent`, `launch_started_at`, `liveness_note` and `pre_trust` are typed by the client's own result types; `src/ad-phase1-types.ts` names only the `liveness_note` values CSCB tells apart (`LivenessNote`) and the `pre_trust` type (`PreTrust`, the client's own). A binary older than Phase 1 omits `kill_sent` and `pre_trust`, which the client declares required, so the stub's `kill`, spawn and `resume` answers are typed `StubKillResult`, `StubSpawnResult` and `StubResumeResult`: the client's type with that one field optional (`MayLackPhase1Fields`). A Phase 1 field given as `undefined`, or left out, is absent from the built result, as from a binary older than Phase 1, with one exception: a `pending` row's launch start (below).

- `cannedKillResult(killSent?)`: `{ kill_sent }`, or `{}` without an argument. The stub's default `kill` result is `{}`.
- `cannedStatusResult(overrides?)`: `{ state: 'waiting' }` by default, which is also the stub client's default `status` answer (`cannedStatusResult()`). Its one Phase 1 override is `launch_started_at`: agent-director's `status` result has no `liveness_note`.
- `cannedGetResult` and `cannedListRow` take `launch_started_at` and `liveness_note` as overrides. A `pending` row from any of the three canned builders (`cannedStatusResult`, `cannedGetResult`, `cannedListRow`, and so the stub client's default answers) shows the sample launch start `SAMPLE_LAUNCH_START_DEFAULT` (`SAMPLE_LAUNCH_STARTS.fractional`) unless the overrides have a `launch_started_at` key, since a configured persona's own `pending` row with no launch start latches the persona (b.jg5 SRJ-513). "No launch start" is an explicit request: `launch_started_at: SAMPLE_LAUNCH_START_NONE` (`undefined`: the key is left out of the row) or `launch_started_at: null` (the key shown as `null`). A row in any other state has no launch start, and no row has a note, unless given one; an explicit override wins in every state. Launch starts: `SAMPLE_LAUNCH_START_FRACTIONAL`, `SAMPLE_LAUNCH_START_WHOLE` and `SAMPLE_LAUNCH_START_NONE` (`undefined`), all three by form in `SAMPLE_LAUNCH_STARTS` for `test.each`; take launch starts from these constants and never type a timestamp. Notes: `provenanceNote` (`provenance_conflict`, the only note that latches a persona, with the CONFLICT case "conflicting labels"; the stub's own spelling of the wire value, not `src/`'s constant, so a misspelt constant fails a test), `nonLatchingNotes` (every other note agent-director names: `tmux_server_changed`, `process_not_seen_session_present`, `process_not_seen_tmux_unchecked`, `probe_eacces`, `tmux_session_name_empty`, `tmux_session_name_control_char`, `tmux_session_name_rewritten`, typed as the client's free text) and `unknownNote` (a note CSCB does not know, `provenance_conflict_cleared`, so a prefix match of the latching note fails). Take note values from here, never type them; the note-rule cases in `tests/session-manager.test.ts` and `tests/conflict-latch.test.ts` iterate `nonLatchingNotes` and `unknownNote`: every entry at the pure decision, the collision `get` and the post-run `get`s, and a sample of them (one or two listed notes, `unknownNote` and no note) at the other sites, which share the one note rule.
- `cannedSpawnResult(instanceId?, preTrust?)` and `cannedResumeResult(instanceId?, preTrust?)`: `preTrust` sets `pre_trust` (`ok`, `skipped` or `failed`); leave it out for a result from a binary older than Phase 1, which carries no `pre_trust` key.
- `PRE_TRUST_VALUES`: the three `pre_trust` values (`readonly PreTrust[]`), for `test.each` with the two builders above; an absent field is their `preTrust` left out. It is built from a private record keyed and valued by every `PreTrust` member, so a member missing from it, an extra key or a misspelt one fails `bun run typecheck`. Tests take `pre_trust` values from this list and never type them.
- A recorded `spawn` may be a reuse spawn (b.jg5 SRJ-112, SRJ-708): `spawnCalls` and `holdSpawns`' `calls` are typed with the client's `SpawnParams`, so a test reads `reuse_finished` with no cast; the stub answers a reuse like any spawn, from the same queue and knobs.
- `cannedFindMissing({ rows })`: `rows` maps each row ID to where a sweep reports it, `'ids'`, `'unverified_ids'` or `'neither'` (`FindMissingRowPlacement`; `'neither'` on a row read `pending` models a row the run did not judge); both ID lists are sorted and `count` and `unverified` are their lengths. `cannedFindMissing(overrides?)` builds the result field by field (empty by default).

**Stub client knobs.** A `<verb>Queue` on `makeStubClient` holds `cannedOk(value)` and `cannedErr(error)` entries; N entries answer the next N calls in order. Once the queue is empty the verb falls through to `<verb>Error`, then `<verb>Result`, then its default. `killQueue` and `findMissingQueue` work this way (defaults `{}` and `cannedFindMissing()`).

- `readPaneQueue` wins over `readPaneError` and `readPaneResults` while it holds an entry. Once empty, the call falls through to `readPaneError`, then `readPaneResults` (whose last entry sticks), then `{ pane: '' }`; the queue's own last entry does not stick. The stub reads these knobs off its options object at each call, so a harness may pass `readPaneError` and `readPaneResults` as getters over its own options to change the answer between attempts (the reconnect adapter's `makeHarness` in `tests/server.test.ts`).
- `statusFn(params)` and `getFn(params)` compute each `status` or `get` answer from the call's params and take precedence over every other knob of their verb. Returning an `Error` rejects; the call is still recorded in `statusCalls` or `getCalls`. `getFn` and `statusFn` may each return `undefined`, which hands that call to the verb's other knobs (`getQueue`, `getError`, `getResult`, then the default; the same for `status`), as if it were unset, so a knob that answers for one instance leaves the others alone.
- `findMissingFn(params)`, `readPaneFn(params)`, `sendKeysFn(params)` and `killFn(params)` (b.jg5 SRJ-410) are per-call knobs like `spawnFn`: each answers a `StubCallAnswer`, may be async, and wins over its verb's queue, error and result; `undefined` falls through to the verb's other knobs, queue first, and an `Error` rejects. Every call is recorded in its capture list (`findMissingCalls`, `readPaneCalls`, `sendKeysCalls`, `killCalls`) before the knob answers; `find-missing` is also in `callLog`, as before. A `findMissing` that `holdFindMissing` held never reaches `findMissingFn`. Use them to script a judged, unjudged or marked-missing run, a pane with or without a dialog, or a `send-keys` error, per call.
- `spawnFn(params)` and `resumeFn(params)` (b.jg5 SRJ-407) are the per-call launch knobs: each answers a `StubCallAnswer` (a result, an `Error`, `undefined`, or a promise of one) and wins over its verb's queue, error and result. The call is recorded first (`spawnCalls`; `callLog` then `resumeCalls`), then the knob is awaited, so it may be async and move a fake clock inside the call, which is how a case sets a launch call's window; an `Error` rejects, a result resolves, and `undefined` falls through to the verb's other knobs. A reuse spawn reaches `spawnFn` too (`reuse_finished: true` in its params). Take a launch timeout's description words from `src/ad-description-phrases.ts` through the builders (`errCallTimeout(verb)`, `errTmuxUnresponsiveLaunchTimeout(verb, instanceId?)`), never a literal.
- `makeStubCreateClient({ calls })` records the options each client is built with in `calls`, typed `ClientOptions[]`, one entry per call in order (a rejected call included), so a test reads `calls[0].callTimeoutMs`. A field the caller did not pass is absent.
- `makeCloseCountingStubClient(opts?)` returns `{ client, closes }`: a `makeStubClient(opts)` stub whose `close()` is replaced by a counter, and `closes()` the number of calls so far.
- `makePassingGateDeps(overrides?)` returns `Partial<StartupGateDeps>` for a start gate run that passes every step: `createClient` from `makeStubCreateClient()`, `probeGetPermission`, `probeErrorCatalog` and `probeDecideArgv` passing, a `statSync` that throws ENOENT (the same-user check passes silently), `geteuid` 1000, a `recordStartupError` that swallows, and an `exit` that throws, since a passing run never reaches it. `overrides` wins over every default (a test that checks the exit code passes its own `exit`).

Other shared fixture builders in `tests/test-helpers/`:

- `ad-settings.ts`: agent-director's settings file for tests (b.jg5 SRJ-1304). `writeAgentDirectorConfig(home, input)` writes `.config/agent-director/config.toml` (the path `src/ad-settings.ts` exports as `AD_SETTINGS_RELATIVE_PATH`) under `home`, creating the directory, and returns the path; it throws, writing nothing, when `home` is the real home (`isRealHome`) or is not a directory under the OS temp directory (the temp directory itself included). `input` (`AdConfigInput`) is either `AdConfigTables` (`tmux`: any of the nine keys; `pauseTimeout`: `[pause] timeout_seconds`; `extraTmuxKeys`: unknown or misspelt `[tmux]` keys; `extra`: other top-level entries, which can replace `tmux` or `pause` with a plain value) or `AdConfigNotToml` (`{ notToml: true, embed? }`: fixed text smol-toml refuses, with `embed` on its offending first line so a leak case can put a fake token there; the one raw-text write). Every other file goes through smol-toml's `stringify` with `numbersAsFloat: true`, and a value (`AdConfigValue`) is a `bigint` for a TOML integer, a plain number for a float or a string. Without that option the serializer writes an integral number such as `60` as the integer `60`, so a float case could never be produced; with it every number is written as a float (`60` as `60.0`), and integers are written as `BigInt`, the type the reader's values have. `REFUSED_AD_CONFIG_FORMS` (rows of `RefusedAdConfigForm`: `name`, `input`, and the `key` the refusal concerns, undefined for text that is not TOML) is SRJ-1304's list of refused forms for `test.each`: text that is not TOML, a string, a float, a float with a zero fraction, a negative value, `stopping_window_seconds` and `starting_session_seconds` one below their minimums, `pending_grace_seconds` one below the minimum a raised `create_timeout_ms` gives, and that raised `create_timeout_ms` with `pending_grace_seconds` missing and 0. `RAISED_CREATE_TIMEOUT_MS` (the default grace period in milliseconds) and `RAISED_GRACE_MINIMUM_SECONDS` (the grace minimum it gives with the default pipe-close wait) are exported for the grace cases. `DEFAULT_AD_SETTINGS` and `AD_SETTING_MINIMUMS` are re-exported from `src/ad-settings.ts`, never copied, and every threshold in the forms is derived from them and `pendingGraceMinimumSeconds`, so a change to a default or a minimum moves every form. It holds no module-scope state and starts no process.
- `ad-value-reads.ts`: the TypeScript-parser matcher of reads of `agent-director` values, shared by `tests/host-safety.test.ts` (SRJ-121's call-site audit) and `tests/fmk-source-audit.test.ts` (the Phase-1-only class reads). `agentDirectorValueReads(sf, names)` finds every read of the named exports from `agent-director` in a file: a named import or re-export, a namespace property or element read (through a cast or a copy included), a destructuring, a default-import, `import x = require`, dynamic-import or `require` read, and a read it cannot name; `agentDirectorNamespaceArguments(sf)` finds the namespace, a dynamic import or a copy passed to a call. Also shared by both audits: `agentDirectorNamespaces(sf)`, the names a file binds to the agent-director namespace (a namespace or default import, `import x = require`, a dynamic `import()` or `require()` held in a variable, and copies of any of them; type-only imports bind none), which both matchers collect their namespaces with; `finding(sf, node, what)`, the `<1-based line>: <what>` finding format; and `AD_NS`, the `import * as ad from 'agent-director'` line the synthetic sources start with. The node helpers beside them (`forEachNode`, `unwrap`, `moduleLoadOf`, `propertyNameText`) serve both audits. It starts no process and reads no file.
- `ad-version-entries.ts`: `expectRuntimeEntryPointsToDebugSkill(message)`, the one check of a runtime version re-check's startup-errors entry of either class (b.jg5 SRJ-205, SRJ-208, SRJ-1013): it names `RUNTIME_RECHECK_PHRASE` and points to the debug skill (`DEBUG_SKILL_RUNTIME_STOP_POINTER`, `DEBUG_SKILL_PATH`), names neither the switch-over runbook nor any line of the install skill's block, and carries no instruction to upgrade or install agent-director (`UPGRADE_FORMS`, whitespace collapsed by `flat`). `tests/ad-version-gate.test.ts` (the re-check's record) and `tests/startup-errors.test.ts` (the entry the real recorder writes) both use it; don't keep a file-local copy.
- `agent-director-versions.ts`: the agent-director versions tests check against CSCB's Phase 1 floor, and the minimum Claude Code version (b.jg5 SRJ-1304). A test imports every agent-director version it checks from here or from `src/`, never as a literal, so a change to `PHASE1_FLOOR_VERSION` (`src/ad-version-gate.ts`) moves every case with it. `PHASE1_RC_VERSION` is the version the Phase 1 release candidate reports: the floor with `-rc.1` appended, built from the constant; the released client rejects it as below its own 0.12.0 minimum. `OLD_AD_VERSION` is the release below the current floor, which the released client and the floor refuse. `DEV_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION` is the client's own `DEV_SENTINEL_VERSION`, re-exported (never typed); the floor refuses it. `DEV_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION` is a non-SemVer defensive injected value that the own floor refuses; native resolution rejects it as unreachable first. `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` is the installed client's `min_binary_version`, read once at load from its `dist/version-floor.json` through the package's subpath export `FLOOR_SUBPATH` (exported: `agent-director/dist/version-floor.json`, the file `src/install-check.ts` reads); the module throws a clear error at load when the file cannot be resolved or read or holds no string `min_binary_version`. `BELOW_CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` is the plain release one step below the client minimum's core (one patch below, else the previous minor's or major's `999` release), which the client refuses as too old; the module throws at load unless `compareAdVersions` puts it below the minimum. `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE_VERSION` is the minimum Claude Code version agent-director states for its exec-form hooks and the version the fleet runs (the released 0.12.0 notes' "Required agents"). This helper is its one definition: there is no `src/` constant (no `src/` module reads a Claude Code version), and a test never writes a Claude Code version; it imports this constant (`tests/ci-live-docker.test.ts` checks `docker/Dockerfile.live`'s `ARG CLAUDE_CODE_VERSION` pin against it, with its one-patch-below negative control derived from it; a check of the runbook or the docs imports it the same way). `tests/ad-version-gate.test.ts` checks that it is strict SemVer and that no other `tests/` TypeScript file, and no `src/` TypeScript file, writes its value in code. It starts no process, holds no value import of `Client` or `resolveSystemBinary`, and imports only `src/ad-version-gate.ts` and the client's sentinel, so the agent-director stub imports it without a cycle.
- `archive-db.ts`: `buildTempArchiveDb(rows, defaultChannel)` builds a real message-archive SQLite file through the production `openArchiveDatabase` in its own `mkdtempSync` directory (rows at chosen timestamps, optional per-row channel, WAL checkpointed so a read-only open sees them) and returns `{ dbPath, cleanup }`; `messagesSince(startedAt, channelId, count)` gives `count` rows strictly after a spawn's `started_at`, the one place that boundary rule lives. Used by `tests/jsonl-persistence-check.test.ts`, `tests/session-manager.test.ts` and `tests/persona-routing.test.ts`; call `cleanup()` in teardown.
- `channel-delivery.ts`: the stored-choice file for tests (b.deo SRI-1204), built only through `src/channel-delivery.ts` (`channelDeliveryPath`, `serializeChannelDelivery`, `parseChannelDelivery`, and `channelDeliveryDeclarationOf` for the declaration). `writeChannelDeliveryRecord(stateDir, entries)` writes the record holding `entries` (by persona key: a `declaration` and its `channels` by channel ID, each with its `delivery` and optionally its `set_at`) and returns its path; a key with no channels is left out, as the server's writes leave it out. It refuses, writing nothing, the real home, the OS temp directory itself and any directory not under it. `readChannelDeliveryRecord(stateDir)` returns the record as the module's parser reads it, or `null` when there is no file; it throws naming the function, the path and the parser's problem when the parser refuses the file, and rethrows any other read failure. `SAMPLE_SET_AT` is the `set_at` a seeded channel gets when the test gives none, never typed per test; `declarationOf(persona)` is a resolved persona's declaration as the server records it; `channelDeliveryRecordOf(entries)` is the same entries as the in-memory record, for comparing with what a store or the reader answers. Tests never hand-write the file: no JSON literal of it and no file name typed as a string; a malformed record is the serialiser's output altered.
- `ci-live.ts`: the shared fakes for the `/ci-live` runner suites (`tests/ci-live-*.test.ts`). `memSecureFs()` is an in-memory `SecureFs`, the secret store's seam: files and directories with their modes, plus a log of every call, so a suite can assert the order of a private write (temp file, chmod, rename) and that a path was never read. `virtualClock()` is the runner's `Clock` over the shared fake clock: `sleep(ms)` advances virtual time by `ms`, so a bounded wait runs to its deadline with no real delay. It imports the runner only from the directories The /ci-live runner allows.
- `ci-run.ts`: the builders and fakes of the sharded `/ci` runner's suites (`tests/ci-run-*.test.ts` and `tests/ci-verdict.test.ts`; b.uqm SR-21.4), which drive `scripts/ci-run.ts` and `scripts/ci-verdict.ts` in process through their injected dependencies. One banner per owner, in this order: 1. the spawn recorder; 2. the process table, signals and `/proc`; 3. the lock probe; 4. the fake container interface, which follows Docker's image rules; 5. the worktree builder; 6. the run-directory builder; 7. the reader additions (E3); 8. the lock directory and password file (E5); 9. cgroups, `/ci-live` and readings (E6); 10. sample sequences (E7). A test hand-writes no status file, reservation, results file, result-file line or verdict: a valid one is built here through the runner's own writers, an invalid one from a valid one by a stated change. The helper types no runner constant or message, starts no child process and uses no `mock.module`; time moves only on `createFakeClock`. A lane adds only under its own banner.
- `clear-latch-terms.ts`: `CLEAR_LATCH_TERMS` and `clearLatchTermsIn`, the one list of the `clear-latch` spellings nothing offered to a bot may carry (b.jg5 SRJ-511, AC 47): the command and the route, imported from `src/clear-latch.ts` (`CLEAR_LATCH_COMMAND`, `CLEAR_LATCH_ROUTE`), and the underscore and camel-case forms derived from the command, so no spelling is typed in tests; `clearLatchTermsIn(text)` answers the terms found, case-insensitively, in list order. Used by registry.test.ts, shipped-docs.test.ts and notice-texts.test.ts (see Not-Offered Checks).
- `cli-teardown.ts`: fixtures shared by the CLI teardown's two suites (`tests/cli-teardown.test.ts`, the pure pieces, and `tests/cli.test.ts`, the commands; b.jg5 SRJ-901, SRJ-904, SRJ-907, SRJ-909, SRJ-1007). `UNKNOWN_STATE` is a row state CSCB does not know, which the precheck and the teardown take as live; `teardownKillFailedDescription(error)` is the raw description of an `ErrTmuxKillFailed` as the teardown's kill-failure alert decision quotes it, read through the checked kill under `TEARDOWN_KILL_OPTIONS` (it throws when the error gives no such outcome); `cliAlertLine(command, persona, content)` is the kill-failure alert line a CLI teardown gives a persona, built from `src/kill-failure-alert.ts`'s builders with `killFailureCliTeardownEntryContext(command)` and the CLI closing sentence. Pure: no client, process, timer or file.
- `dead-evidence.ts`: `DEAD_EVIDENCE_OF`, the one table of which dead-session cause and escalate-dead verdict is dead evidence (b.jg5 SRJ-611): true exactly for the GONE-based ones, keyed by `DeadEvidenceSource` so a new cause or verdict fails the typecheck until it has an answer, and written by hand rather than asked of `src/`. `tests/session-manager.test.ts` checks `isDeadEvidence` against it; `tests/restart.test.ts` reads it for what a verdict leads to. A cause's GONE-ness here is unchanged by the path; whether it leads to a kill or a sequence also depends on the path's last read (dead evidence covers one life: a finished or absent read voids it, and a reconnect's `row-not-interactive` voids a carried verdict), which each case states in its own row. Never keep a copy of it in a test file.
- `fifo.ts`: real FIFOs. Node and Bun have no mkfifo API; a test makes a FIFO only through this module, never by starting `mkfifo` itself. `mkfifoAvailable()` is probed once per process: `false` with no child started when `mkfifo` is not on `PATH`, otherwise whether `mkfifo --version` exits 0. Guard a real-FIFO case with `test.skipIf(!mkfifoAvailable())`, the reason in the test name. `makeFifo(path)` creates a FIFO at `path` and throws, naming the path, the exit status or signal and `mkfifo`'s stderr, when `mkfifo` fails; it neither removes an entry already there nor creates the parent directory. Each `mkfifo` child is bounded (`MKFIFO_TIMEOUT_MS`) and its `env` is a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call naming only `mkfifo`, over a fresh temp HOME removed as soon as the child exits. A `HostSafetyError` (for example `mkfifo`'s `PATH` directory holding an `agent-director`) is thrown, never read as "unavailable". `mkfifoAvailable()` runs while tests are collected (inside `test.skipIf`), so the error makes the whole test file fail to load rather than its FIFO cases skip; from `makeFifo` inside a test it fails that test. The reload harness's `mkfifoAvailable` and `h.makeFifo` delegate to it; `tests/fifo-helper.test.ts` pins it.
- `fungible-decision.ts`: the rows of the delivery decision table (b.deo SRI-1301, SRI-1204), read only by `tests/delivery-decision-fungible.test.ts`. A row (`FungibleDecisionRow`) holds one call of `decideDelivery`: the event from a Slack stub factory, the configuration from the persona-config helpers with the receiving persona P (`P_KEY`), the envelope flag as received, the stored choices and whether the store was readable, and the whole expected outcome, written in the row; a `ChannelDeliveryRow` adds `channelDeliveryFor`'s expected result. A row may also hand the decision what the loader never produces (a declarative section in fungible mode, `FAIL_IF_READ` for one that must not be read), to show that an input not in force plays no part. `decisionArgsOf(row)` and `channelDeliveryArgsOf(row)` build each function's arguments as `src/persona-routing.ts` builds them, and `FUNGIBLE_DECISION_GROUPS` holds the ten SRI-1301 groups in order. A new fungible-mode decision rule adds its rows here, in its group, not in the suite. Pure data: no file, timer or module mock.
- `host-safe-env.ts`: `hostSafeChildEnv(home, { tools, pathDirs, extras })`, the environment of every child process a unit test starts. Pass it as the child call's `env` option directly (not built earlier into a variable, spread, merged or wrapped); add variables through `extras`. It returns exactly `HOME` (`home` as given), `PATH`, `TMUX_TMPDIR` and the extras, nothing else from `process.env`, so no token reaches the child.
  - `PATH` is the absolute, test-owned `pathDirs` first, in order (stub or shim bins), then the directory each named tool resolves to on the current `PATH`, duplicates dropped. `tools` defaults to `DEFAULT_CHILD_TOOLS` (`bun`, `bash`, `sh`). With no tool and no `pathDirs` it is `EMPTY_CHILD_PATH` (`/dev/null`), which no lookup can search; pass `tools: []` for a child started by absolute path (`process.execPath`) that runs nothing by name.
  - The tmux fence: `TMUX_TMPDIR` is one directory under the OS temp directory whose name starts with `CHILD_TMUX_TMPDIR_PREFIX`, fixed on the first call and returned by every later call in the process, and no extra can set `TMUX` or `TMUX_PANE`, so a tmux the child starts never reaches the host's tmux server. The first call reuses the process's own `TMUX_TMPDIR` when a parent's `hostSafeChildEnv` set it (`inheritedChildTmuxTmpDir`: an existing directory, not a symlink, owned by this user, directly under the OS temp directory, named with the prefix and more); otherwise it makes one with `mkdtempSync`. So a `bun` child that loads the helper again (a harness run in a child) hands its own children the parent's directory.
  - The directory is never removed: tmux falls back to `/tmp`, the host's default socket directory, when `TMUX_TMPDIR` is missing, so it must outlive every child. Each `bun test` run leaves at most one, plus one for each child whose OS temp directory differs from its parent's: the helper does not pass `TMPDIR` on, and only a directory directly under the child's own OS temp directory is reused. A test that starts such a child and wants the parent's directory reused passes `TMPDIR` as an extra.
  - It throws a `HostSafetyError` whose `reason` is one of `HOST_SAFETY_REFUSAL`, and whose message names the path or name at fault without printing the environment, when: `home` is relative, is the real home or holds an agent-director install (`.config/agent-director`, or anything at the standard install path); a tool name is empty or holds `/` or the `PATH` delimiter, or the tool is not on `PATH`; a `pathDirs` entry is relative or holds the delimiter; any directory on the built `PATH` holds an entry named `agent-director` (a file, directory, symlink or dangling symlink); or an extra sets a reserved name (`RESERVED_CHILD_ENV_NAMES`: `HOME`, `PATH`, `TMUX_TMPDIR`, `TMUX`, `TMUX_PANE`) or is not a string. Fix the fixture, or take the offending directory off the test run's `PATH`; don't catch the error to skip a case.
  - The real home is the account's passwd home or the home the run started with, symlinks resolved. Test for it with the exported `isRealHome()`, never `os.homedir()` or `os.userInfo().homedir`: Bun fixes both at start-up from the launch-time `HOME`. `realHome()` and `passwdHome()`, which answer the real home's path, are referenced only in the guard's own files (`REAL_HOME_FILES` in `tests/host-safety.test.ts`: this helper, the preload, `tests/host-safety.test.ts` and `tests/launch-home-guard.test.ts`), so no other test can build a path under the real home. The module also exports the agent-director predicates (`dirHoldsAgentDirector`, `homeHoldsAgentDirectorInstall`), the discovery names, and the `PATH` lookups (`absolutePathEntries`, the absolute entries of a `PATH` value; `resolveToolDir`, the directory of a tool's first executable on it), so nothing else spells them. `osTempDir()` is the OS temp directory made absolute and normalized (`resolve(tmpdir())`), so `TMPDIR=/tmp//` or `/tmp/.` names the same directory as `/tmp`; every "directly under the OS temp directory" comparison uses it, never the raw `tmpdir()`. `runCli`, `runInFakeHome` and `fifo.ts` use it; `tests/host-safety.test.ts` pins it.
  - The preload guard's logic lives here too, so the guard and `tests/host-safety.test.ts` share one implementation. `preloadRedirectedEnv(inherited, home, tmuxTmpDir)` returns the guard's variables (`PRELOAD_ENV_NAMES`) computed from an inherited environment, and changes nothing: not `inherited`, not `process.env`, not the file system. `preloadCheckFailures(env)` lists why `env` is not an environment the guard produced, as `PRELOAD_CHECK` labels, or returns `[]`; it only `lstat`s and starts no process. The launch-home refusal lives here as well, shared with `tests/launch-home-guard.test.ts`: `launchTimeHomes()` lists the homes Bun fixed at start-up; `launchHomeRefusal(launchHome, passwdHome, probe)` returns a `LAUNCH_HOME_REFUSAL` label or `undefined` and asks the file system nothing except through `probe` (a `LaunchHomeProbe`: `exists`, `canonical` and `tempDir`; the guard passes `LIVE_LAUNCH_HOME_PROBE`, a test may pass a recording fake); `LAUNCH_HOME_DERIVED_PATHS` lists the `homedir()`-derived paths it follows (among them `XDG_CONFIG_DIR`, `.config`, where the guard points `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` under the new HOME, and `CSCB_XDG_CONFIG_PATH`, `.config/claude-slack-channel-bots`, the crontable's default directory; bug b.avm); `launchHomeRefusalMessage(reason, launchHome)` is the guard's one stderr line; `LAUNCH_HOME_REFUSED_EXIT_CODE` is its exit code; `passwdHome()` is the account's passwd home: `passwdHomeFrom` over `/etc/passwd` (read once per process) and the process's uid, or `undefined` when the file cannot be read or the process has no uid. `passwdHomeFrom(passwdText, uid)` is pure: the first line whose uid field equals `String(uid)` exactly decides, giving its home field when the line has at least seven fields and the home is absolute, and `undefined` otherwise; a malformed first match is never passed over for a later line, so the guard fails closed. It is `undefined` too when no line matches or `uid` is not a non-negative safe integer.
  - The preload imports this helper, so whatever the helper loads runs before the refusal: it imports only `node:` builtins (pinned by the load audit in `tests/host-safety.test.ts`). Never import a `src/` module, a package or another test helper here, not even for a type.
- `host-safety-preload.ts`: the `bun test` preload guard (see Host safety under Isolation). `bunfig.toml`, `tests/bunfig.toml` and `tests/integration/bunfig.toml` load it as their first `[test]` preload entry; it exports nothing and no test or helper imports it, and it loads only `node:` builtins and `./host-safe-env.ts`. It first refuses to start, with exit code 78 and one stderr line, under a launch-time home `launchHomeRefusal` refuses. It then makes the HOME directory (`PRELOAD_HOME_PREFIX` under `osTempDir()`), takes the `TMUX_TMPDIR` directory from `childTmuxTmpDir()`, applies `preloadRedirectedEnv` to `process.env` and throws when `preloadCheckFailures` reports anything; all of these come from `host-safe-env.ts`.
- `install-check-fixtures.ts`: fixtures for the install check, its callers and the tests that check versions against the client minimum. Version constants: `SATISFYING_VERSION` (the client-admitted development sentinel below CSCB's numeric floor: the check passes it with the note) and `STALE_VERSION` (below the client minimum: too old), both checked against the installed client's minimum and the floor when the module loads, which throws if either is out of place; `SENTINEL_VERSION` and the re-exported `DEV_SENTINEL_VERSION` (never type the sentinel); `CANNED_BINARY_PATH`, the path every canned result reports by default (equal to the stub resolver's `STUB_RESOLVE_DEFAULT_PATH`, pinned by `tests/agent-director-stub-resolve.test.ts` rather than imported, since the stub helper pulls in the session manager and persona modules); `UNREACHABLE_REASONS`, every `ErrSystemInstallUnreachable` reason agent-director defines in a fixed order, built from a `Record<UnreachableReason, true>` so the compiler holds the set complete. Version-floor JSON builders (`makeVersionFloorJson`, `makeMalformedFloorJson`, `makeMissingFieldFloorJson`) and the `tests/fixtures/version-floor/` loaders (`loadFixtureRaw`, `fixturePath`). Canned `runInstallCheck` results in its shapes and texts: `cannedSuccessResult(opts)` defaults to `CANNED_BINARY_PATH`, `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` and `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` (from `agent-director-versions.ts`) with no note; any success field overrides, a `binaryVersion` below the floor carries `buildInstallCheckPhase1Note(binaryVersion)`, `phase1Note: true` or `false` forces the note on or off, and an explicit `note` wins. `cannedFailureResult(label, opts)` takes an exported label value and builds that label's default detail and message (too old: `STALE_VERSION` against `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` at `CANNED_BINARY_PATH`, the message from `buildSystemInstallTooOldMessage`); `opts.detail` and `opts.message` replace them, the message otherwise built from the effective detail. The not-found, unreachable and floor-unreadable messages are copies of `src/install-check.ts`'s text, pinned by `tests/install-check.test.ts`, which compares real results whole with the canned ones; where a message names the publishing-host block, the switch-over runbook section or the client-package remedy, the copy builds that part from `src/install-check.ts`'s exports (`PUBLISHING_HOST_BLOCK_POINTER`, `RUNBOOK_SECTION_POINTER`, `CLIENT_PACKAGE_REMEDY`), never a literal. From `src/install-check.ts` it value-imports those three text constants and otherwise imports types only.
- `line-parts.ts`: a line builder's own text around the parts a case cannot rebuild (b.jg5 SRJ-1014: every asserted line comes from its exported builder, never typed). Hand the builder `LINE_HOLE` (a NUL) for each open part (a thrown value's description with its stack frames, a label the module does not export, a value the case leaves open): `lineParts(build)` answers the builder's text split at each hole, in order (one more part than holes), for matching a line by its head, tail or the words between; `builtAround(build)` answers a pattern of the builder's text before and after its one hole, anything in it. Use these instead of splitting a builder's output at a marker by hand.
- `old-life.ts`: the fixtures and line helpers the old-life hold and wait cases share (b.jg5 SRJ-809, SRJ-810, SRJ-811), over the recovery harness, so none is kept per file: the pre-persona row a hold is begun on (`PRE_PERSONA_ID`, `PRE_PERSONA_LABELS`, `PRE_PERSONA_SESSION`); start-sweep rows and the sweep (`listed(h, key, overrides?)`, a persona's own row as the `list` gives it; `absentRow(h, key, overrides?)`, an absent persona's row in the first persona's directory; `sweepOver(h, rows, config?, isShuttingDown?)`, the sweep as `main()` runs it, answering its result, the stub calls in order and what the first kill met); holds begun by hand (`beginApplyHold(h, key, dirOf?)`, apply step 1's hold on a persona's own row, at `dirOf`'s working directory, the key's own by default, so a case can hold `cscb_<P>` at another persona's directory as a destructive modify that moved P away; `holdOldAt(h, instanceId, oldKey, dirOf)`, an id held at a persona's directory as a start-sweep kill that did not succeed begins it); the old-life gate (`oldLifeGateLine` and `gateLine`, its launch line through `oldLifeHoldLaunchLine`; `gateLinesIn(h)`, the gate lines among the harness's errors; `heldBack(key)`, `spawnForPersona`'s answer for a held persona); the wait's end line (`waitEndLine(instanceId, oldKey, end, armed)` through `oldLifeWaitEndLine`, and `waitEndLinePrefix`, the head every end line of one old row shares); and `settledRetryLinePrefix` (the head of a persona's end-retry settled line). Every label, line and answer comes from `src/`, cut from the builders' own output where a prefix is needed; it keeps no module-scope state. A new old-life case imports these rather than defining its own copy.
- `pending-row-model.ts`: model authoritative AD pending/live/finished states, launch start, scripted verb outcomes, find-missing judgments and checked launch/kill transitions on the shared fake clock. Drive state changes through AD readings, not terminal content or automatic dialog answers. Keep latch refusal, row provenance and ordinary pending retry coverage.
- `delivery-queue.ts`: `makeQueue(stateDir, options?)`, `seedQueue`, `readQueue`, `queuePath`, `slackInput`, `injectedInput`, `sentRecord` and `deliveredRecord`. Pass the caller’s scratch directory and fake clock; seed raw string bytes for torn-last-line cases. Reuse these fixtures across queue, routing, interject, cron and reload tests. For preparation scaling, count item snapshots during concurrent acceptance/preparation at multiple queue sizes rather than relying on wall time; restore the clone spy in `finally` and stop preparation.
- `recovery-harness.ts`: production retry, launch, AD row, pending-rule, latch and live-row-sequence composition over one stub and fake clock. Drive launch/retry/latch/shutdown through the shared harness, advance the injected clock, and await in-flight work. The harness has no startup autoanswer loop. Own-launch fixtures use an authoritative AD launch start inside the call window; pending recovery keeps grace/bound and standard backoff.

- `reload-terms.ts`: `RELOAD_TERMS` and `reloadTermsIn`, the one reload-term list (see Unadvertised Reload Checks).
- `launch-calls.ts`: `observeLaunchCalls(stub, onCall)` wraps the stub's `spawn` and `resume`, reports each verb before delegating, and preserves the stub's result. It performs no directory I/O. Used by session-manager and restart tests.
- `retired-keys.ts`: the retired-key record for tests (b.jg5 SRJ-1304), built only through `src/retired-keys.ts` (`retiredKeysPath`, `serializeRetiredKeys`, `parseRetiredKeys`). `writeRetiredKeysRecord(stateDir, entries)` writes the record holding `entries` (by key: `cause`, and optionally `retiredAt` and `mark`, `true` for `SAMPLE_NEW_LIFE_BEGUN_AT`, a timestamp, or `false`, `null` or unset for none) and returns its path; it refuses, writing nothing, the real home and any directory not under the OS temp directory (the temp directory itself included). `readRetiredKeysRecord(stateDir)` returns the record as the module's parser reads it, or `null` when there is no file; it throws naming the file and the parser's problem when the parser refuses the file, and rethrows any other read failure. `retiredKeysRecordOf(entries)` is the same entries as the in-memory record, for comparing with a store's snapshot. `SAMPLE_RETIRED_AT` and `SAMPLE_NEW_LIFE_BEGUN_AT` are the default times, and `RFC3339_UTC` matches the form the server writes a time in, for a `retired_at` or a mark the server wrote. Tests never hand-write the record: no JSON literal of it and no file name typed as a string; a malformed record is the serialiser's output altered.
- `runbooks.ts`: the runbooks' titles and block headings (docs/migration.md), the form of their step headings, and the one reader of a runbook's steps (b.jg5 SRJ-1108, SRJ-1109; ruling Q13); tests take every one of them from here. `ROLLBACK_RUNBOOK_SECTION_TITLE` is the rollback section's title and `REFUSAL_BLOCK_HEADING` the switch-over runbook's refusal-block heading, both defined here because no `src/` code reads them. Two runbook names live in `src/ad-version-gate.ts`, and tests import them from there: the switch-over section's own title, `PHASE1_RUNBOOK_SECTION_TITLE`, which every refusal and note reads, and the publishing-host block heading, `PUBLISHING_HOST_BLOCK_HEADING`, which the install check's not-found message and SR-2.5's not-found and below-client-minimum diagnostics name (the startup gate's not-found message names `RUNBOOK_SECTION_POINTER` instead) (b.jg5 SRJ-211, SRJ-212). `stepHeadingPrefix(n)` builds the start of step n's heading title and `stepNumberOf(title)` reads the step number back (`undefined` for a title that is not a step's), so no reader re-types the form. The step reader: `runbookSteps(section, { count?, level?, name? })` reads a runbook section's body (its heading line excluded, as `findSection` and `requiredSection` return it) and returns one `RunbookStep` per step, in order: its `number`, its heading `title` as written, its heading's 0-based `line` in the section and its `text` as written (up to the next heading of its level or higher). Steps are found by number through `stepNumberOf`, never by title, at `level` (default: the section's shallowest heading level); `count` defaults to the highest step number found. A missing, duplicated, out-of-order, beyond-the-count or step-0 heading throws naming the step, after `<name>: ` when given; a section with no step fails as step 1 missing. Its users: `shipped-docs.test.ts` (every switch-over and rollback carrier, through `readRunbookCarrier`, and the switch-over step ranges), `fmk-switch-over-runbook.test.ts` (the runbook steps Test 13 must follow) and, in the image, `tests/integration/fixtures/switch-over.ts` (the step titles Test 13 enters). The Markdown contract it carries: each runbook is a `###` section under `## Migration`, its blocks and steps are headings one level below it, and each step heading's title starts with `stepHeadingPrefix(n)`. Pure. Its load path is image-safe: it imports only `markdown.ts`, which imports nothing, and nothing from `src/`, `agent-director` or any package, so it loads in the `/ci` image's `/tests`, which has no `src/` or `node_modules`; `shipped-docs.test.ts` guards that load path (see its row).
- `session-ending-commands.ts`: `SESSION_ENDING_COMMAND_FORMS` (each a `SessionEndingCommandForm`: `name` and a non-global `pattern`) and `sessionEndingCommandsIn(text)`, the session-ending command forms of ADSRD SR-1.4 that b.jg5 SRJ-1001 lists (`kill` named as a command to run, `pause`, `--include-finished`, `tmux kill-session`, `tmux kill-server`) and the names of the forms a text matches, in table order. Their one definition. It imports nothing, so the scenario value printer (`tests/integration/fixtures/fmk-texts.ts`, entry `sessionEndingCommandsIn`) loads it in the `/ci` image, where `tests/` is copied to `/tests` with no `src/` beside it and `conflict-cases.ts` cannot load. `conflict-cases.ts` re-exports both names and the type, and unit tests import them from there. Pure: reads nothing, writes nothing.
- `shell-header.ts`: a release script read as text (b.42j). `shellCode(text)` is the script without its comment lines, the shebang included; `shellHeader(text)` is its leading comment block, the shebang included. An exit-code entry is a header line `#   <code>  SR-x.y …` with its continuation lines, and its code column is a number or `--` for a step with no exit code of its own (`publish-prepare.sh`'s SR-3.2). `headerExitCodes(header)` answers the numbered codes in order, `--` entries left out; `headerEntries(header, sr)` answers every entry for one SR id, matched as a whole word (`SR-2.4` finds no `SR-2.4a`), each a `HeaderEntry` (`code`, `null` for `--`, and `text` as written); `joinCommentLines(text)` joins an entry's lines into one for a phrase check. Used by `tests/preflight-ad-check.test.ts`, `tests/publish-prepare-changelog.test.ts` and `tests/publish-promote-exit-table.test.ts`; a test that reads a script's header or code as text uses these rather than its own copy. Pure: reads nothing, writes nothing.
- `source-audit.ts`: the shared helpers of every source audit over `src/` (comment-stripped code, `stripComments`; `maskLiterals(code)`, that code with the text of every string, template and regex literal, quotes included, blanked to spaces at the same offsets and newlines kept, the code inside a template's `${…}` kept, so a search for a call or an identifier is never satisfied by literal text; both read the source with one small lexer that follows string literals, templates nested inside `${…}` to any depth, and regex literals, taking a `/` for a regex from the token before it (a punctuator or a keyword such as `return`), so a quote or backtick inside a regex or a nested template, or a `//` inside any literal, never throws it out of step; call and `main()` locators such as `callsOf`, `callArguments`, `splitTopLevel`, `objectProperties`, `mainBody` and `importSource`, whose bracket scanners skip string and template literals, nested templates included, but not regex literals or type arguments; the start's anchor `startResolution` (see server.ts wiring and call order under Stubbing External Dependencies); `objectProperties(text, spread?)` resolves a bare `...name` spread through the optional resolver, nested spreads included, merges its properties in and throws `duplicate property` on an override, and without a resolver any spread still throws `not a plain property`), and the runtime import walk: `srcModules()` reads every `src/*.ts` module's comment-stripped code by file name, `importedSpecifiers(code)` lists the specifiers a module imports or re-exports (static or dynamic), `runtimeSpecifiers(code)` drops the whole-statement type-only ones, and `runtimeLoads(modules, entry)` follows `entry`'s runtime imports transitively, answering each module reached with the import chain that reaches it and each package or builtin loaded on the way (`RuntimeLoads`); it throws on a relative import that names no `src/` module, so an import the walk cannot follow fails the audit rather than being skipped. The server-only boundary: `SERVER_ONLY_MODULES` is the pattern of the server-only `src/` modules (the notifier, outage state, the latch, the episodes, the server, the session manager and restart), and `forbiddenServerLoads(entry)` answers `{ loads, forbidden }`: what `entry` loads at run time (`runtimeLoads`) and each forbidden load among it as its import chain (a server-only module, a Slack module, one whose name has `slack` and that imports anything, or an `@slack/` package). A case asserts `forbidden` is empty and, so the walk is not vacuous, that `loads` reaches a module it names. `tests/ad-settings.test.ts` (no `smol-toml` outside the settings module's walk) uses the walk, and the three kill modules' tests (`tests/checked-kill.test.ts`, `tests/kill-retry.test.ts`, `tests/kill-failure-alert.test.ts`) use `forbiddenServerLoads`; keep no per-file copy. The one source audit's word rules: `DELETE_HELPERS` (the row-delete helpers, SRJ-716), `REMOVED_IDENTIFIERS` (SRJ-601's removed names), `wholeWord(names)` (a global pattern for any of `names` as a whole identifier) and `SOURCE_WORD_RULES`, one `[SourceWordRule, RegExp]` row per rule (`delete-helper`, `finished-row-option`, `ad-label`, `raw-tmux-subcommand`, `removed-identifier`). `tests/fmk-source-audit.test.ts` runs them over every `src/` file and `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts` reads some of them over the architecture doc and the engineering guide, so a name added to either list is checked in both places.
- `tree-snapshot.ts`: `treeSnapshot(dir)`, a snapshot of a directory tree for a case that asserts a call changed nothing under it (`expect(treeSnapshot(dir)).toEqual(before)`). It lists `dir`'s own mtime, then every entry depth first with names sorted, each with its kind, its size (or, for a symlink, its `readlink` target) and its mtime. It never follows a symlink, so a link out of the tree or a symlink loop is one entry and is not walked. It reads only under `dir` and writes nothing. The opt-in `treeSnapshot(dir, { extended: true })` (b.uqm SR-21.3) adds to every line, `.` included, the entry's permission bits as four octal digits and its change time, and for a regular file the SHA-256 of its content, so a `chmod`, a rename or a same-size content change shows; access time is never part of a snapshot, and without the option the output is unchanged. `tests/host-safety.test.ts` and `tests/launch-home-guard.test.ts` use it, as do the `/ci` runner's suites; a new "changed nothing" case uses it rather than its own walk.
- `upgrade-forms.ts`: `UPGRADE_FORMS`, the one list of the forms an instruction to upgrade or re-install agent-director, or an upgrade or install command, takes (b.jg5 SRJ-208, SRJ-1101), and its row type `ForbiddenForm` (label, pattern, a synthetic string the pattern matches). Check text with its whitespace collapsed (`flat`). Its re-install row matches "re-install" or "reinstall", in any case, followed within three words of the same clause by agent-director (its npm package included, quoted or backticked), and not re-installing a Slack app or CSCB itself: re-installing the host's agent-director outside the switch-over runbook could bring in Phase 1 without the switch-over. A suite that needs more forms appends its own rows (the install-cscb skill's `RUN_OFFER_FORMS` and `BRANCH_FORMS`); the shared rows, the re-install row included, are self-checked only in `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts`. `INSTALL_OR_REMOVAL_FORMS` is a second list, applied only to the operator messages `src/` and `scripts/` build (the install check's, the startup gate's and SR-2.5's; b.jg5 SRJ-208, SRJ-211, SRJ-212), never to the shipped docs, whose publishing-host block sends a host with no agent-director to agent-director's own install: an instruction to install agent-director, other than by following the switch-over runbook as the too-old message does, and the removal or re-creation of a file. Its rows are self-checked in `tests/install-check.test.ts`; a suite whose messages carry an allowed span removes that span before the scan (`tests/dependency-check.test.ts`'s `ALLOWED_SPANS`: the Bun-upgrade wording and the same-user ruling's never-remove wording). `UPGRADE_FORMS` is used by `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts`, `tests/install-cscb-skill.test.ts`, `tests/dependency-check.test.ts`, `tests/ad-version-gate.test.ts`, `tests/install-check.test.ts`, `tests/install-check-script.test.ts`, `tests/outage-state.test.ts`, `tests/postinstall.test.ts` (which scans `UPGRADE_FORMS` as is) and `tests/preflight-ad-check.test.ts`; `INSTALL_OR_REMOVAL_FORMS` by `tests/install-check.test.ts`, `tests/dependency-check.test.ts` and `tests/preflight-ad-check.test.ts`.

`makePersonaConfig` puts `mcp_config_path` under `baseDir`, but the loader defaults it to `~/.claude/slack-mcp.json` under the home you pass. When comparing against loader output, override `mcp_config_path` with the loader's value (e.g. `join(home, '.claude', 'slack-mcp.json')`).

### Launch and upgrade fixtures (b.omv SR-7, SR-13.6)


The stub defaults to `at-once`. Startup dialog modes remain explicit human/fault inputs: CSCB never reads or answers their terminal text. Pending recovery uses AD state, grace and normal retry cadence.

Test 17 delays SessionStart and follows authoritative AD `pending` to `waiting`, preserving session identity and ordinary launch/retry bounds. Its launch-timeout legs use persona-scoped `launch-reply-timeout` faults: run the real AD launch, then lose only its successful response after pane identity is persisted. Return the launch-timeout `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` envelope and verify its production classification and launch-window ownership for plain spawn, reuse spawn and resume; a send-keys uncertainty envelope must fail that control. Pass collisions and other errors through without consuming the rule; preserve the 23-second report delay, G=60, retry timing and combined polling deadline. Assert that the hold and launch-timeout recovery make no automatic `read-pane` or `send-keys` calls.

Test 26 waits for the exact asynchronous recovery-get result before checking hooks; retain delayed/missing/wrong/duplicate-result controls in `fmk-launch-pending-dialog-wait.test.ts`. Before each replacement launch, combine `ad.spawn.state_transition` and `ad.find_missing.tick` by timestamp for the exact instance and launch interval, ignore null-state ticks, require the latest state to be `ended` or `missing`, and retain the CSCB read-before-launch check. Fixture pane identity in test 18 uses the supported scenario tmux metadata helpers, validating session, pane and process IDs without reading terminal text.

Test 13 follows the maintained migration step 10: before current-build startup, seed each earlier persona conversation on the actual 0.10.0 path without marker or appended prompt, preserve memory, assert a colliding plain spawn followed by one fresh reuse with a new marked session, then retain the later restart/resume and version-floor checks. Runbook and shipped-doc audits keep the guide synchronized; frozen 0.11.0 release copies remain unchanged.

The session-manager guard cases hold the pre-resume/final reconnect reads unresolved while latches, lifecycle or holds change; positive controls must still launch or send the one delayed Enter. Health/restart liveness cases install the upgrade inspector and prove dead/no-row evidence bypasses it while live/pending inspection and unknown-read behavior remain intact.

### Delivery engine and model fixtures (b.omv SR-13)

Use the production engine/gate policy with injected AD rows, typed refusals, durable queue and fake clock. First send, retry, reconnect text and Enter require fresh AD `waiting` and the existing non-screen guards. Assert that a change between text and Enter blocks the latter, preserves queued work and retains retry/backoff. Test independent persona clients and lifecycle cancellation.

For uncertain own sends, assert durable `send_kind: enter` recovery after backoff, the receipt window, then full retype only if an exact receipt remains absent. Receipts confirm only CSCB's full own text; native queue, turn, task and compaction records supply no state. No screen fixtures, native glyph classifiers, draft ownership or leftover Enter tests belong in CSCB.

Drive a working-to-waiting cycle with exact receipts across 20 FIFO items and 20 multipart parts; require prompt progress after each durable `delivered` append. Cover failed delivered-append retry and launch replacement so `receiptWorking` cannot leak to another part or launch or clear before durability.

For test 33's restart reset, assert durable Enter/retype/Enter/retype records, matching part/session/launch and first-waiting evidence, actual persona key calls, and the swallowed text fault's process ID. Check the 10-second first stage and both 30-second stages separately, including the guarded text-to-Enter delay of at least 500 ms. A single less-than-60-second text-gap assertion cannot represent these two recovery stages.

For recovery Enter returning AD `GONE`, reopen a durable queue and drive the production engine and checked live-row sequence together. Cover restart delay zero, an ordinary restart already pending, stale `waiting`, a refused checked kill, local holds and cancellation; require lease release before the detached handoff, no keys during the sequence, conversation-preserving resume and exact-receipt FIFO drain. These adapter regressions extend no TLC claim.

For retained `GONE`, exercise both normal ticks and reconnect retries: require a fresh AD read despite the row cache, reject an unannounced session or launch-timestamp replacement, retain work on refused reads, and recheck guards changed during the read. Hold a read in flight to prove reconnect cannot start a second read or handoff; then release it and require eventual checked recovery. Use `KILL_RETRY_TRIES` for exhausted-kill coverage.

For fresh `ErrSpawnNotFound` after retained `GONE`, drive the real retry controller with permanently absent `get`/`status` rows and restart delay zero; require exact-receipt FIFO drain without a stale kill. Cover a replacement appearing before the controller acts, rejected transfer, guard/launch changes during the absence read and status refusal preserving normal backoff. Exercise the actual server callback: only `RAN` and `ARMED` transfer ownership; `RUNNING` and `CLOSED` retain evidence. Hold an overlapping pending-only retry on an older `waiting` read, then require eventual recovery through tick and reconnect. Accepted transfers must not repeatedly bypass the controller’s backoff.

Conformance renders AD observations and exact receipt facts through production policy. The final b.acu/b.aqj corpus contains 266 traces and 40,778 states: 265 canonical traces/40,757 states plus a separately proven 21-state, 20-edge original-action pullback prefix with no liveness claim. The 29 targeted conformance/verdict tests pass, including 23 injected scheduler-boundary cases. [The verification report](cardinal-delivery-verification.md) records all 84 local row results, 107 unchanged input hashes and bounded exhaustive versus sampled scope; local `certification: false` establishes no scheduler certificate. The [actual-file inventory](../tests/test-helpers/cardinal-trace-changes.json) gives one-line reasons for all 302 changed fixture/coverage paths. Keep no-loss/FIFO/durable queue and at-least-once controls; full `/ci` remains with the separate runner.

Real-agent action waits use AD state. Retain independent exact transcript input/output assertions, but omit pane text from diagnostics. Test outcome-only probes by AD success/error class; native display compatibility belongs to agent-director.

For replacement waits, require AD to report a new native session before asserting replacement; an old live row and historical connection log cannot finish that wait. For pending-window fixtures, use an explicit bounded report delay and AD transition records rather than assuming a one-second sampler sees every state. Release a persistent lost-create fault only after its exact timeout and pending-get proof, so the next launch can succeed under the normal AD-checked sequence.

Audit uncertain reconnects from the originating text through the first successful Enter, retaining intervening refused Enters after the unwedge mark. Select both call and outcome streams through that same success, with matching AD outcomes and unchanged launch/session identity. Exercise the actual scenario selector with refusal-boundary controls. The Codex gate must stop all observation owners and verify AD retirement before spawning in the shared workdir.

### Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks

Any test that touches Slack tokens or a persona credentials file uses the shared helper `tests/test-helpers/credentials.ts`. It builds fake tokens that all carry one marker, `LEAK_SENTINEL`, and checks captured output for that marker or any token-like value. A leak then fails a test instead of reaching a log.

| Export | Use it for |
|--------|------------|
| `LEAK_SENTINEL` | The marker every fake token embeds. It uses identifier characters only, so an error that quotes an identifier quotes the whole sentinel. Refer to the constant; never copy its value into a test. |
| `BOT_TOKEN_PREFIX`, `APP_TOKEN_PREFIX` | The Slack token prefixes, for building fakes and for asserting on rule text. A bare prefix is not a token. |
| `fakeToken(prefix, suffix?)` | One fake token built at runtime: the prefix, the digit `1`, the sentinel, then `-` and the suffix when one is given. The digit after the dash gives it a real token's shape, not just `TOKEN_LIKE`'s. Use distinct suffixes to tell two fakes apart; pass a wrong prefix or none for bad-prefix cases. |
| `sentinelInMessage(label, prefix?, marker?)` | The sentinel as a test error's *message* carries it: `fakeToken(prefix, label)`, a space, then `sentinelTicketUrl(STUB_WSS_ORIGIN, marker)`. `prefix` defaults to `BOT_TOKEN_PREFIX` (`APP_TOKEN_PREFIX` for a Socket Mode leg), `label` tells two fakes apart (`''` for none), and `marker` is the ticket (a Slack stub passes its `leakMarker`). Wrap it as the message needs, e.g. `` `failed (${sentinelInMessage('x')})` ``. |
| `REDACTED_SENTINEL_TAIL` | What `sentinelInMessage(…)` reads as after `redactSlackLogText` (`<redacted-token> <redacted-url>`), built from the redactor's own placeholders. Assert the logged `message="…"` holds it in the same wrapping as the thrown message. |
| `sentinelTicketUrl(origin?, marker?)`, `STUB_WSS_ORIGIN` | A Socket Mode WebSocket URL `<origin>/link/?ticket=<marker>`, the shape a real connect error quotes; `origin` defaults to `STUB_WSS_ORIGIN` (the Slack stub's `wss://` origin) and `marker` to `LEAK_SENTINEL`. For a URL on its own; a message uses `sentinelInMessage`. |
| `withoutName(captured, name, key)` | A copy of `captured` with a persona name and its key replaced by `<name>` / `<key>` (the longer first), with the same traversal as `assertNoLeak`: `assertNoLeak(withoutName(captured, name, key))`. Only for runs whose output legitimately holds a token-shaped name (built with `fakeToken`, so it carries the sentinel); anything else token-like, a credential value included, still fails. |
| `makeCredentials(overrides?)` | A credentials object. By default it is valid (exactly `bot_token` and `app_token`, distinct fakes); an override set to `undefined` removes that key, and any other override adds or replaces one. |
| `writeCredentialsFile(dir, name?, overrides?)` | Writes a credentials file with mode 0600 inside `dir` and returns its path. An object override merges over the valid defaults; a string override is the whole file content, for non-JSON or non-object cases. `dir` is required and `name` must stay inside it. |
| `writtenFile(path)` | Marks a file or directory the code under test wrote, so `assertNoLeak` checks its content. This is the only way to check a file: a plain string is always checked as text, never read as a path. |
| `TOKEN_LIKE`, `isTokenLike(text)` | The one definition of token-like that `assertNoLeak` applies: `xox` plus a lower-case letter, or `xapp`, then a dash and a letter or digit, with a boundary before the prefix (the start of the text or anything but an ASCII letter or digit). A bare prefix in rule text, a word such as "Xoxo bot" or a prefix glued to a word (`inboxapp-bot`) doesn't match. It needs no digit after the dash (a prefix followed by a letter is flagged too), so `assertNoLeak` errs on the strict side; `tests/persona-identity.test.ts` pins its rows. Not global: build a global copy from `TOKEN_LIKE.source` to find every occurrence. The secrecy audit uses the same matcher. |
| `assertNoLeak(captured, label?)` | Fails if the sentinel (any case) or a token-like value appears anywhere in `captured`. Strings, errors (message, stack, properties, `cause`, aggregated errors), arrays, objects, maps, sets, buffers and `writtenFile` marks are all checked, recursively. |

Rules:

- **Fakes only.** Tests use only tokens from `fakeToken`, `makeCredentials` or `writeCredentialsFile`, so every token embeds `LEAK_SENTINEL`. No test file contains a token literal, not even a fake one; build it at runtime from the prefix constants.
- **Check everything captured.** Every test that handles credentials runs `assertNoLeak` over its captured log lines, errors and results, including rejected calls and failure paths. Pass them together as one object so a failure names the leaking item (for example `captured.lines[2]`). Wrap any file the code wrote in `writtenFile`. A test of a log line b.jg5 SRJ-1014 lists (a latch, re-check round, retry timer, `tmux-unresponsive`, `ad-config-malformed`, kill-try, live-row sequence, old-life, retired-key, settings or version re-check line) runs `assertNoLeak` over the lines it captured too, even when it handles no credentials, with the line's text taken from its `src/` builder; where the line quotes agent-director, its error's description carries `sentinelInMessage(…)`.
- **Override the token environment.** Nothing in `src/` reads `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` or `SLACK_APP_TOKEN`, so tests normally leave them alone. A suite that sets either (for example to show that `start` or the connection manager ignores them) sets both to fakes for its whole run and restores the previous values afterwards, so an ambient real token can never reach a failure message.
- **Log errors by description.** Code under test logs a thrown value on a Slack or agent-director path through `describeThrownValue` (see the engineering guide's Error Handling), which keeps the message only as `message="…"` after `redactSlackLogText`. Log lines keep the redacted message, so a test error's message carries `LEAK_SENTINEL` only through `sentinelInMessage` (inside a `fakeToken()` and a `?ticket=` URL, the shapes redaction removes); its other properties (headers, `data`, `original`, bodies, extra fields CSCB must never read) carry it bare. The case asserts the line holds its type, safe `code` or `errName` and the redacted message (`REDACTED_SENTINEL_TAIL` in the message's wrapping), then runs `assertNoLeak` over everything logged. A line that skips redaction, or a redactor that loses either pattern, then fails `assertNoLeak`; a bare sentinel in a message would survive redaction, so it proves nothing about the describer.
- **Token-shaped persona names.** A persona name may be shaped like a Slack token (b.av2 SR-1.2 as amended by b.deo SRI-102, SRI-202: no format rule), and a test builds one with `fakeToken`, so it carries the sentinel. A run whose output must hold that name is checked with `assertNoLeak(withoutName(captured, name, key))`, never left out of the check: the name and key are masked and everything else, credential values included, is still checked.
- **Why the failure is safe.** `assertNoLeak` reports which item leaked and whether it held the sentinel or a token-like value, never the leaked text. Rule text such as "must start with" a bare prefix passes.

Isolation (SR-13.2) applies to credentials files, crontables, cron logs and working-directory fixtures exactly as it does to config files:

- Create them only under the test's own `mkdtempSync` directory, pass every path explicitly, and remove the directory in `afterEach`. `writeCredentialsFile` has no default location and refuses a name that escapes `dir`.
- Never read or write `~/.claude/channels/slack`, `~/.config/claude-slack-channel-bots` or `~/.config/agent-director`, and never point a credentials path at the real home.
- To test code that falls back to `os.homedir()`, use `runInFakeHome` from `tests/test-helpers/fake-home-subprocess.ts`. Setting `process.env.HOME` in a test doesn't work: Bun fixes `os.homedir()` at start-up from the launch-time HOME, which is also why the preload guard's HOME (see Host safety under Isolation) moves only what reads the environment. The helper runs the module call in a child `bun` process whose `env` is a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call: HOME is the fake home, `PATH` holds only the directories of `FAKE_HOME_CHILD_TOOLS` (`bun`, and `sh` for code that shells out), `TMUX_TMPDIR` is the process's one fenced temp directory, and `SLACK_STATE_DIR` and the JSON input are the extras. Nothing else from `process.env` reaches the child, so no token does. Assert `observedHomedir` equals the fake home before checking anything else. The helper refuses the real home (`isRealHome`) before any child starts, `hostSafeChildEnv` throws for a fake home or tool directory that holds agent-director, and a child that hangs past its timeout (30 s by default) or dies from a signal throws. `tests/trust-bootstrap.test.ts` and `tests/legacy-reply-hook-cleanup.test.ts` are the references.
- Code that can call `recordStartupError` writes `startup-errors.log` under `SLACK_STATE_DIR`, which the preload guard points under its shared temp HOME (unset, it would default to a path under the launch-time home). Point `SLACK_STATE_DIR` at the test's own temp directory for the whole file in `beforeEach` and restore the environment (the preload's value) in `afterEach`, as `tests/legacy-reply-hook-cleanup.test.ts` does. A test that reads the log points it at its own directory.
- When a test compares real paths (for example a symlinked credentials file shared with another persona), resolve the temp directory with `realpathSync` first; the OS temp directory may itself be a symlink.

Permission cases (an unreadable credentials file, an unreadable or unsearchable working directory) inject a failing operation through the check's file-system seam, so they pass under root in docker CI. The credentials seam is `openFile`, `fstatFile`, `readFileFd`, `closeFile` and `realpath`; the working-directory seam is `stat`, `access` and `realpath`, and the combined `PersonaBringUpFs` carries both. For an unreadable credentials file, make `openFile` or `readFileFd` throw an errno-style error (`EACCES`, `EPERM`, `EIO`). To test a credentials path that is not a regular file (a FIFO or a device), make `fstatFile` report `isFile()` false, assert `readFileFd` is never called and `closeFile` is called for the opened descriptor. Because the real open is non-blocking, a real FIFO, directory or `/dev/zero` is also safe to use as a control. The config reader (`readPersonaConfigBytes`, used for `config.json` and the last-applied record) has the same seam, `PersonaConfigFs`, and the same rule, reached through `configFs` on the reload controller and `readAppliedPersonaConfig`. A durable write whose directory open or fsync fails after the rename (`DurableWriteUnsyncedError`), or a durable delete whose directory open or fsync fails after the unlink (`DurableUnlinkUnsyncedError`), is driven through the `DurableWriteFs` seam. The reload detection tick's credentials reads take the same `CredentialsFs` seam, through `opts.credentialsFs` on a reload harness run. A variant that uses real permission bits is marked `test.skipIf(isRoot)`, with the reason in the test name. Restore any mode a test changed before `afterEach` removes the directory.

A test that could hang on a blocking open or read (a real FIFO with no writer) runs the risky call in a child `bun` process (`spawnSync` with a `timeout`, typically 10 s, and its `env` a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call) and asserts the child was not killed (`child.signal` is null), with a per-test timeout above that bound. A test timeout can't interrupt a blocking synchronous call, so in-process a regression would hang the whole suite instead of failing one test. Make the FIFO with `makeFifo` from `tests/test-helpers/fifo.ts` (the reload harness's `h.makeFifo` delegates to it), never by spawning `mkfifo` yourself, and skip the case where `mkfifo` is missing (`test.skipIf(!mkfifoAvailable())`, the reason in the test name). The reload harness (`tests/test-helpers/reload-harness.ts`, its `mkfifoAvailable` and `h.makeFifo`) is the reference.

#### Secrecy Audit

`tests/secrecy-audit.test.ts` enforces these rules across the suite, and the describe-don't-echo logging rule across `src/` (b.av2 SR-10.3 closing audit, as amended by b.deo SRI-901 to SRI-906; AC 20). It reads files under `tests/` and `src/` only, comment-stripped, and anchors on content, never line numbers.

- **Which suites it covers.** A suite touches config, credentials or reload when it has a value import (not `import type`, not a `type` specifier, not a SCREAMING_CASE constant) from a source module in its `SOURCE_SURFACES` list (the config loader, the credentials reader, the bring-up checks and controller, the `src/persona-slack-*.ts` client, logger and validation modules, the connection manager and its error describer, the Web API watch, the diagnostics, the start procedure, the persona lifecycle, the log redactor, the reload modules, and the modules whose output carries agent-director or Slack failure text: the MCP registry, the session manager, restart, the permission poller and click handler, the notifier, the destination modules, the health check, the CLI and its teardown commands' pure pieces (`src/cli-teardown.ts`: the precheck's lines and the teardown's verdicts and outcomes), the template module, the agent-director error classifier, the agent-director settings reader, the UNAVAILABLE retry timer, the persona episodes with their `tmux-unresponsive` condition, the outage state, whose `ad-config-malformed` onset quotes agent-director, the conflict latch, whose latch and
