| Checkpoints | Take `run.checkpoint()`, drive ticks, then `run.since(cp)` is `{logs, writes, removes, lifecycle, slackBuilds, slackCalls}` since the checkpoint. `run.since(cp)` equal to `NO_RUN_ACTIVITY` shows the ticks did nothing at all; spread `NO_RUN_ACTIVITY` and set only the expected fields to show they did exactly that. |
| Stop | `await run.stop()` stops detection, cancels every bring-up retry and the destination hold, closes the notice episodes (as `main()`'s shutdown does), closes the tool clients and stops every connection; it is idempotent. |

Rules for reload tests:

- **Use the harness.** Build every SR-8.1 path (as amended by b.deo SRI-401) through the harness (`h.paths`, `h.persona`, `h.write…`), never by joining `config.json.pending`, `.apply` or `.last-applied` onto a directory by hand. The harness keeps each path under its temp root and refuses one outside it.
- **Manual ticks only.** Drive detection with `run.ticks.tick()` / `ticks(n)`. Never use real timers, `sleep` or a timer-backed tick driver; bring-up retries run on `run.clock`, and advance only when the test moves it, so they never mix into a stretch of ticks. One exception: the test that shows the first check comes 5 s after `startDetection` passes the production driver on the run's fake clock through `opts.tickDriver` (a factory returning `createReloadTickDriver` over the `clock` and `log` it is given) and moves `run.clock`; it never uses a real timer. Every other reload test drives ticks by hand.
- **Assert effects through the recorder.** Which personas were brought up, launched, torn down, reconnected or updated in place, and with what outcome and class, is read from `run.lifecycle`, and file effects from `run.writes`, `run.removes`, `h.readRecord()`, `h.readConfig()` and `h.configDirEntries()`. Don't stub `ReloadLifecycleOps` by hand.
- **Detection applies nothing.** A detection test takes a checkpoint before the edit and asserts `run.since(cp)` in full: `NO_RUN_ACTIVITY` when nothing should happen, or `NO_RUN_ACTIVITY` spread with only the expected pending-file write or delete and log lines. At the least, `run.since(cp).lifecycle` is `[]`, which is how a test shows a tick made no lifecycle call (AC 55).
- **Held states come from the harness.** Put a persona in `retrying` or `broken` with `SLACK_UNREACHABLE`, `SLACK_AUTH_REJECTED` or a credentials or directory fixture, and revert a credentials edit with the function `h.saveCredentials` returned. Compare held content through `h.credentialsDigestOf` and `run.bringUps.credentialsDigest`, never by printing a digest or reading a token.
- **Fake tokens only.** Credentials files come from `h.writeCredentials` (sentinel-bearing fakes, rotated on each call) or `h.writeCredentialsContent`; a hand-shaped file that holds a token builds it with `fakeToken`.
- **Paths explicit, temp dirs only (b.av2 SR-13.2).** Pass every path explicitly and keep every file under the harness's `mkdtempSync` root. Never read or write `~/.claude/channels/slack`, `~/.config/claude-slack-channel-bots` or `~/.config/agent-director`, never let a path resolve to the real home, and never start a server or run the CLI against the real HOME.
- **Leak-check every run and assert no Slack post.** Call `assertNoLeak(run.captured())` for each run in every test, failure paths included, so the logs and reload files carry no token. Every detection test also asserts that `run.slackPosts()` is empty for each run, so no reload output reached Slack (b.av2 SR-7.2); `tests/reload.test.ts` wraps both in `expectNoPostNoLeak(...runs)`. `captured()` includes only files the run's writer wrote that still exist, so to check the pending file's content, call it while the file is there. A test-written file holding `LEAK_SENTINEL` (a malformed config with a pasted fake token) is left out of `captured()` on purpose; a test that checks what the code wrote passes it through `writtenFile` itself.
- **Sweep the whole tree for AC 20.** `captured()` misses a file the run's writer didn't write (a leftover confirmation, anything under the state directory or the home), so a secrecy case also checks `h.serverSideFiles(...operatorWritten)`: every regular file under the harness root but the operator's own (`config.json`, credentials files, a hand-edited record), keyed by root-relative path, which `assertNoLeak` checks by name and content. Pass it beside `captured()` (`assertNoLeak({ ...run.captured(), server: h.serverSideFiles(...) })`), as `tests/reload.test.ts` does, or through `tests/reload-apply.test.ts`'s closing `sweep`. It walks regular files only: the server writes nothing else, a symlink or FIFO under the root is a test fixture (a `claude_config_dir` link, `h.makeFifo`), a symlink's target is swept where it lives when it is under the root, and opening a FIFO would block the test. Don't use it to replace `captured()`: the writer's log and call records are only there.
- **Clean up.** Call `await h.cleanup()` in `afterEach`; it stops every run (detection included), resets the session registry and ack tracker when a run registered a session or delivered an event, and removes the root. A test that must show no timer is left calls `await run.stop()` and asserts `run.clock.pendingCount()` is 0.
- **Permission cases use seams.** Record and pending-file write failures go through `h.failWrites`, stored-choice write failures through `h.failChannelDeliveryWrites`, pending-file delete failures through `h.failRemoves`, an unreadable file through `h.replaceWithDirectory`, `h.makeCredentialsUnreadable`, `opts.configFs`, `opts.credentialsFs` or `opts.bringUpFs`, never through file modes, so the suite passes as root.
- **Real FIFOs run in a child process.** A case that puts a real FIFO at the config or a credentials path (`h.makeFifo`) builds its harness inside a child `bun` process with a 10 s bound and asserts the child was not killed (see Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks). In `tests/reload.test.ts` every such case goes through `runHarnessInChild(body)`, which runs `body` over a harness under the test's root, bounds the child, asserts it exited cleanly and returns the one JSON line it printed with its stderr; the case then checks from that result that nothing reached Slack (no Slack call, or no lifecycle record or write on a refused start) and runs `assertNoLeak` over it.
- **Use the reference file's helpers.** In `tests/reload.test.ts`, `running(names, opts?)` is a server running `names` from a byte-equal record and config file with detection started and its first check run (`opts` goes to `h.startDetecting`). `pendingWritten(logs?)` and `pendingRemoved(logs?)` are the `run.since(cp)` of a stretch that wrote or removed the pending file once with its log lines; by default `pendingWritten` builds the expected log from the pending file itself (`loggedPreview()`: each body line classed `reload-preview`, the header ending with the pending-file suffix), so the logged preview must be the file's body line for line. `previewHeader(counts)`, `NO_EFFECTIVE_CHANGE_PREVIEW`, `invalidPreview(error)` and `invalidLogged(error, written?, pendingFile?)` are the expected header, no-effect preview, INVALID body and `reload-invalid` line; `loggedPreview(lines?, written?, pendingFile?)` and `invalidLogged` point at `h.paths.pending` unless given another pending path (a child harness's, which the FIFO cases' child prints). `personaRef(name)` is how every preview line names a persona, and `removedLine(name)`, `destructiveLine(name, setting, path)` and `addedLine(name, causes?)` are a removal's, a destructive path change's and an addition's line; `credentialsChangedLine(persona, effect?, cause?)` is a credentials line by persona and path, with `effect` one of `reconnect` (the default), `kept`, `retry`, `bring-up`, `stays-broken` or `unchecked` (the bring-up state could not be checked); a `cause` gives the "but it cannot be used (<cause>)" form, required for `kept` and `stays-broken` and refused for `reconnect` and `bring-up`; and `credentialsChangeLines()` the pending preview's credentials lines. A line these helpers don't cover (an in-place change, say) is asserted on the tick path by the header's counts, its wording left to the pure tests. Pin a pending-file write or delete failure line with `fileFailureLine(code)`, which matches the `[slack] reload:` prefix, the pending path and the errno code, not the sentence; a line whose wording another task owns is matched by its prefix or its class only.
- **Confirm through the harness.** Make the operator's gesture with `h.confirm()` and any other apply-path content with `h.writeApplyBytes`; never rename or write `config.json.apply` by hand. Make a confirmation undeletable with `h.failRemoves`, a record write fail with `h.failWrites`, a stored-choice write fail with `h.failChannelDeliveryWrites`, and an oversized file with `h.writeOversized`.
- **An apply test shows the untouched persona is untouched.** Assert that a persona the confirmed change does not touch gets no lifecycle record (`run.lifecycle`), Slack client or Slack call from the apply. Assert the apply's step order and effects through recording `opts.applySteps` bodies and `run.appliedConfigs` / `run.appliedKeys()`, not by stubbing the controller's internals.
- **Step order through the default fan-out uses the timeline.** A test of what a confirmed change does to the personas runs without `opts.applySteps` and asserts step order through `run.lifecycle.timeline`, holding a call with `run.lifecycle.hold(op, key)` to show the next step waits for it. Record `opts.applySteps` bodies only when testing the step runner itself (step inputs, failures, order of the six slots). Assert the exact lifecycle records for the changed personas and none for any other, and use `opts.realLifecycle` when the case is about the teardown, in-place update or bring-up body (agent-director calls, dependency order).
- **An in-place test shows the recorder and the effect.** Assert `run.since(cp).lifecycle` is exactly the one `update-in-place` record (with its `settings`) for the changed persona, and that nothing else happened to it: no teardown, bring-up or launch, `stubCallCount(run.composition!.agentDirector)` 0, `run.composition!.calls` only the expected `destinations.forget` (none when no DM destination setting changed), the same `run.session(name)` and no socket closed or client built. Then show the new value's effect on the next event, tool call or post through `run.deliver`, `run.callTool` or `run.notice`, not only the stored configuration. Use the harness's consumers and drivers; never hand-build a routing, resolver, notifier, registry session or MCP client beside the run.
- **Use the apply file's helpers.** In `tests/reload-apply.test.ts`, `running(specs, opts?, prepare?)` is a server running `specs` from a byte-equal record and config file with detection started and its first check run. Each spec is a persona name, or `[name, overrides]` for `h.persona(name, overrides)`; `prepare(personas)` may break some after their files are created and before the record is written (a deleted working directory for `retrying`, deleted credentials for `broken`). `opts` goes to `h.startDetecting`, plus `sessions: true` to register an MCP session for every persona; `REAL_WITH_SESSIONS` (`{ realLifecycle: true, sessions: true }`) is what the AC 58 routing and in-place cases use. `confirmConfig(run, personas)` writes the config, ticks, confirms and returns the applying tick unawaited (to hold a step first); `applyConfig` awaits it. `twoOfEach()` runs four personas and returns a change with two personas in each of steps 2, 3 and 6, for the step-failure rows. The next-event drivers are `deliverTo(run, name, event)` (what the routing delivered for that event, as `{chat_id, via}`), `noticeCalls(run, name)` (the Web API calls one notice made, as `<method> <target>`) and `callsSince`; `slackSideOf(run, name)` snapshots a persona's sockets, `auth.test` calls, Web API calls and client builds, to show an in-place update touched none of them. A channel-mode case uses the file's mode rig: `runningIn` to start it, `probe` or `deliverNow` for the next event, `seedChoices`, `storedOnDisk` and `setChoice` for the stored-choice file, `watchKept` across a confirmed switch change, `promptRelay` for a permission prompt through the real poller and click handler, `restartOf(run, opts?)` for a restart (a second start over the same directories; see the Real composition row for when it needs `realLaunch`), and `expectModeNoLeak` for its leak check.
- **A reconnect test shows the overlap and the isolation.** Rotate with `h.rotateCredentials` (scripting the new set with `slack`), confirm with `run.confirmPending()`, and assert through the harness: `run.socketActivity(name)` shows the new set `connected` before the old one is `discarded` and `disconnected` (or, for a failure or a retry, the old one never closed); `run.lifecycle.of('reconnect')` is exactly one record, for that persona, with its `change`; `run.session(name)` is the same session and, with `opts.realLifecycle`, `stubCallCount(run.composition!.agentDirector)` is 0; every other persona gets no lifecycle record, client build or Slack call. Never script the old and new tokens on one stub: the credential sets keep them apart.
- **The 10 s bound runs on the fake clock.** Script the new set's `connect` with `open-never-answers` (or `never`), advance `run.clock` to just under 10 s to show step 4 still holds the apply, then to 10 s to show the attempt is abandoned and the apply moves on. Never wait in real time; the `AC 68` start-never-settles case in `tests/reload-apply.test.ts` is the reference.
- **Break a running persona through the harness.** Use `run.revokeBotToken` for a revoked bot token (bug b.ujn) and `run.refuseReopen` for a refused reopen, never a hand-scripted queue or a direct status change. Assert the persona's lines with `run.linesOf(name)`, its state with `run.isUp` and `run.bringUps.state`, and its instance's registration with `run.admitSession`. A case about the window before the detached socket's close gets the persona's turn builds `makeConnectionHarness` with `serialize` (see Connection Wiring and Bring-Up Tests); `tests/persona-credentials-revoked.test.ts` is the reference.
- **A destructive-modify test uses the real launch.** Move the persona's `working_directory` or `credentials_file` with `opts.realLaunch` and `personaConfigDirs`, and assert exactly one `teardown` and one `bring-up` record for it, `instanceCallsOf(name)` showing its row killed and kept (no `delete`), then a reuse spawn of the same id with no `resume` (`['kill ok', 'reuse-spawn ok']`; `lastSpawnOf(run, name)` with `reuse: true`), `run.socketActivity(name)` showing the old connection closed before the new one opened, and no call of any kind for the other persona. To show nothing starts it between the halves, hold its step-6 `bring-up` and drive a restart (`run.relaunch`) or a bring-up retry (`run.clock`) meanwhile. With the teardown kill failing after its tries (`failedKills()` on the kill queue), the new half's reuse collides and its live-row sequence ends the old life first: the launch answers `sequence-waiting`, `instanceCallsSince` reads the three failed kills, `reuse-spawn ErrInstanceIdCollision`, `kill ok`, `reuse-spawn ok`, and one `liveRowSequenceLaunchLine` names the retired-key reuse. Run that case with the old-life wait installed and assert no old-life wait starts on `cscb_<key>`: no `[slack] old-life-wait` line, the hold the sequence's read ended retrying no one (`oldLifeHoldEndRetryLine(id, [], [])`), no hold left and no retry timer armed.
- **A next-launch test shows no lifecycle call, then the new directory at launch.** For `claude_config_dir` (own or inherited), assert no per-persona lifecycle or instance call at confirmed apply (only `template-refresh` when the directory set changed), and the same connection, MCP session and row. Then seed an `ended` row and use `run.relaunch(name)` to verify the spawn directory and label, with a neighbouring persona unchanged. Reminder enablement (`stop_hook_bootstrap`) is an in-place change: assert its confirmed value affects subsequent observer checks without relaunch.
- **A server-wide test reads `run.serverConfig()`.** For every server-wide setting but the switch, which applies in place, assert the new value is in `h.readRecord()` and `run.appliedConfigs`, that `run.serverConfig()` still has the start-time one with no lifecycle call, and that a later `h.start()` over the same directories runs the new one.
- **A step-5 test uses the real composition.** Assert the `template-refresh` record, `run.composition!.agentDirector.makeTemplateCalls` equal to `installedTemplate` with only `allow` replaced, and one `[slack] template refresh:` line; a change that leaves the directory set as it was makes no `makeTemplate` call. Fail it with `failTemplateRefresh(err)`, spying on `process.exit`, and hold it with `run.lifecycle.hold('template-refresh')`; assert order across steps with `applyTimeline`.
- **The token environment is poisoned, not read.** To show an added persona's tokens come from its file, call `h.poisonTokenEnvironment()` and assert no read is added to its `reads` over the apply and no Slack client got its values; never set the variables by hand.
- **Preview wording is pinned in the pure tests.** Every sentence of the preview is pinned in `tests/reload-preview.test.ts`, against `src/reload-plan.ts` with facts injected as data. The two retired `DESTRUCTIVE:` lines (b.jg5 SRJ-1510) are written out literally in one case there; every other file builds them from `src/`'s exported clauses (`DESTRUCTIVE_PREFIX`, `REMOVED_RETIRED_CLAUSE`, `DESTRUCTIVE_RETIRED_CLAUSE`) or through `removedLine` / `destructiveLine`, never as a literal. The switch line and the recorded lines are pinned by equality with their builders (`modeSwitchLine`, `recordedLine`) plus a check of each element they must carry (b.deo SRI-803, SRI-804). A tick-path test in `tests/reload.test.ts` asserts the pending file's lines and compares the logged preview with the pending file (`pendingWritten()`, `run.lastPreviewText()`), rather than re-pinning every sentence.
- **Assert each preview line kind exactly.** A preview test asserts each SR-8.4 line kind (as amended by b.deo SRI-803, SRI-804) by its exact line or its exact prefix or keyword: the counted header (`previewHeader`), `DESTRUCTIVE:` (`h.pendingDestructiveLines()`), `INVALID:`, `no effective change`, the credentials line by persona and path, an added persona's causes, the switch line (`modeSwitchLine`, b.deo SRI-803) and the recorded line (`recordedLine`, b.deo SRI-804). Showing only that some text exists, or that the file is non-empty, is not enough.
- **Assert the log carries the same preview once.** Compare the whole emission, not a line count: `run.since(cp)` equal to `pendingWritten()`, one emission per pending-state change (`run.previewEmissionCount()`), and none for a rewrite caused only by a changed fact. An INVALID candidate logs exactly one `reload-invalid` line and no `reload-preview` line.
- **Leak-check previews that involve credentials.** Whenever a credentials file is involved (a rotation, a held state, an added persona's credentials cause), run `assertNoLeak(run.captured())` while the pending file still exists, so both the file and the log are checked. In the pure tests, `render()` does this for every rendered form.
- **A new config field fails the coverage test until it is classified.** `tests/reload-plan-coverage.test.ts` holds the loader's key lists in `src/config.ts` against the plan's setting classes. A key added to the loader fails it until the key is in exactly one class in `src/reload-plan.ts` (a new server-wide key also needs a row in `CHANGED_VALUES`, a new channel-entry key an entry in `CHANNEL_FIELD_SETTING`) and has a preview line in `tests/reload-preview.test.ts`. Put a field in `UNCOMPARED_PERSONA_FIELDS` only when its change genuinely has no effect; never loosen the test to make it pass.
- **No lifecycle call before confirmation.** A preview test shows the tick applied nothing: `run.since(cp)` in full, or at least `run.since(cp).lifecycle` empty, so there is no reconnect, bring-up or teardown before confirmation (AC 67), and a broken persona stays broken (AC 65, preview half).

Test `durableWriteFileSync` and `durableUnlinkSync` themselves in `tests/atomic-write.test.ts` through their `DurableWriteFs` seam, the production tick driver in `tests/reload-timer.test.ts` on `createFakeClock()`, the fingerprint and pending-file layout in `tests/reload-fingerprint.test.ts`, the change plan and the preview's wording in `tests/reload-preview.test.ts`, which class each config key lands in in `tests/reload-plan-coverage.test.ts`, and parsing and validation of the config bytes in `tests/config.test.ts`; `reload.test.ts` asserts what a start and a detection tick do with them. Where `server.ts` starts and stops detection is a source audit in `tests/reload-wiring.test.ts`, since `main()` can't run in a test.

### Unadvertised Reload Checks (AC 74)

Reload is triggered only by renaming `config.json.pending` to `config.json.apply` (b.av2 SR-8.8). The AC 74 cases pin that no CLI or MCP surface triggers or describes it: the `unknown subcommand` block in `tests/cli.test.ts` and the `tool list and instructions` block in `tests/registry.test.ts` (see their rows under Test File Organization).

- **One term list.** The reload terms are `reload` in any case, `config.json.apply`, `config.json.pending`, `.apply` and `.pending`, kept once in the shared helper `tests/test-helpers/reload-terms.ts` (`RELOAD_TERMS`, with `reloadTermsIn` returning the terms found). `tests/cli.test.ts`, `tests/registry.test.ts` and `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts` (its check that the exported `MCP_INSTRUCTIONS` carries no reload wording, AC 74's `shipped-docs` leg; the user docs and skills may describe the gesture and are not checked) all import it. Change the list there only; never keep a local copy or drop a term.
- **Assert the terms found, not a boolean.** Compare `reloadTermsIn(text)` with an empty list, so a failure names the term (`tests/shipped-docs.test.ts` runs one `test.each` case per term instead, so the failing case's title names it); for the tools, compare a per-tool map so it also names the tool.
- **Check whole definitions.** The tool check serializes each tool's full definition, so a hint in a schema property description is caught too; the CLI check parses the usage entries, so an added subcommand or flag fails even when its wording is clean.
- **A new CLI subcommand or flag updates the expected usage entries** in the no-arguments case, and a new MCP tool updates the tool-list case; neither may add a reload term. The expected entries include `clear-latch`, whose entry (`CLEAR_LATCH_USAGE_ENTRY`) carries no reload term.

### Not-Offered Checks (AC 47)

`clear-latch` is the operator's way to end a hold, and nothing offered to a bot names it (b.jg5 SRJ-511). Three checks pin it, each over the same terms:

- **Where each check lives.** `tests/registry.test.ts` (`tool list and instructions`): every tool's whole serialized definition and the instructions a session receives. `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts` (`AC 47: the MCP instructions and the reply reminder do not name clear-latch`): `MCP_INSTRUCTIONS` and the reply reminder text, one case per text and term. `tests/notice-texts.test.ts`: every Slack notice's rendered text (each exported SRJ-10xx builder and every other server notice in `NAMED_SLACK_NOTICES`), one case per catalogue entry.
- **One catalogue for SRJ-1001 too.** SRJ-1001's checks (forbidden terms, session-ending commands, the human-only sentence, "Operator actions" by title, the checked `agent-director kill`, redaction and cap) share `NOTICE_CATALOGUE` with this check; there is never a second catalogue.
- **One term list, from `src/`.** The terms (`clear-latch`, `/clear-latch`, `clear_latch`, `clearLatch`, matched in any letter case) come from `src/clear-latch.ts`'s exports through the one helper, `tests/test-helpers/clear-latch-terms.ts`; never type a spelling in a test or keep a local copy. `tests/notice-texts.test.ts` self-checks the finder, so the checks are not vacuous.
- **The operator's documents are not checked for absence.** The user docs, the skills, the Slack app manifest, the crontable header and the CLI usage text may name the command (the usage text lists it), and `clear-latch` is not one of `FORBIDDEN_TERMS`.
- **Every new Slack notice joins the catalogue once.** Add an entry to `NOTICE_CATALOGUE` for each new exported SRJ-10xx builder and variant, or a reason to `NOT_APPLICABLE`; the completeness guard fails an id that has neither. A new Slack notice outside SRJ-10xx also joins once, under SRJ-1001, with its builder named in `NAMED_SLACK_NOTICES`, and one `CAP_VARIANT` row if it quotes agent-director.

### Process-Level Listeners

`bun test` runs every file in one process, so a listener added to `process` is process-global state, with the same hazard as `mock.module` (see Module Mocks). A leaked `unhandledRejection` listener would change how every later file handles a stray rejection.

- **Call the handler, don't install it.** Tests call the exported `createUnhandledRejectionHandler(log)` result directly with each rejection reason. The `unhandledRejection handler (SR-3.3)` describe block is the reference. `bun test` already fails on an unhandled rejection, so no test needs a handler of its own.
- **Run code that rejects unhandled in a child process.** When the case under test is an unhandled rejection, or its absence (a throw inside a fire-and-forget tick), run it in a child `bun` process through `runInFakeHome` and have the child count rejections with its own listener and print the result, as the `a throw while a persona's retry is due leaves it retrying` describe block in `tests/permission-poller.test.ts` does.
- **Remove what you add.** A test that must register a process-level listener removes it in `afterEach`. No test leaves one installed. A spy on `process.exit` is restored in `finally`.
- **Check import side effects by count.** To show a module installs no listener at import, compare `process.listenerCount('unhandledRejection')` before and after a fresh import, as the `connection manager: no module-scope effects (SR-13.1)` describe block does.
- **Leak checks still apply.** Every log line the handler produced passes through `assertNoLeak`.

### Parametrization

When 3+ tests follow the same structure with different inputs, collapse them with `test.each`:

```typescript
test.each([
  ['', 'empty string'],
  ['no-prefix', 'missing type prefix'],
])('rejects invalid id %s (%s)', (id) => {
  expect(() => validateId(id)).toThrow()
})
```

Don't wrap simple domain strings in constants (`const STATUS_OPEN = 'open'`) — inline them. Constants earn their keep only for complex formats or values used in 5+ places.

**Look rows up and check helper answers when a case runs, never at collection or import.** A `test.each` table or a module-scope constant that picks a row out of a shared table (`rowWhere(pred)` over the case table) runs while the file is collected, so a predicate that matches nothing, or a helper whose stub answer drifted, would fail the whole file. Hold the lookup as a function instead (`tests/conflict-latch.test.ts`'s `lazyRow(pred)`, answering the row on first call; a table entry holds `() => row`), and put a helper's self-check where the answer is built (the conflict-case table's CONFLICT answers, the pending-row model's restore sentences), so a mismatch fails only the cases that use it.

### Delivery Reporting and Receipt Consumers (b.omv SR-13)

Use `makeDeliveryNoticeHarness` for queue/reporting/episode/notifier tests and compose it with `makeEngineHarness` for receipt-driven reactions. Assert no add before the last part has a durable receipt; inspect each persona's Slack stub to prove client identity. Drive thresholds, retries and episode boundaries on the shared fake clock, including blocked reconnect with health checks disabled; do not wait on real time.

Import notice examples, remedies, states, diagnostic labels and durations from their source exports. Use `assertNoLeak` on captured diagnostics and posts, plus a message-body sentinel assertion: token redaction alone cannot prove message-text exclusion. Replay the durable queue to distinguish `noticed` from memory-only episode and early-reply records. Catalogue and packaging suites own the shared notice inventory and debug headings; keep their existing builder pin rules.

### State Reset

Use `beforeEach` to reset module-scoped state between tests:

- Registry: `_resetRegistry()` (exported from registry.ts)
- Delivery reactions: `_resetAckTracker()` clears pending entries, successful early replies, completed adds and in-flight bookkeeping. Await held Slack calls before cleanup and restore any Date.now replacement in finally.
- Delivery queue/engine/reporting: create fresh instances per case. Stop engines and queue writes, cancel lifecycle reconnect timers and close notice episodes through the owning harness cleanup before removing its temp tree. Reuse a journal across replay assertions to prove durable noticed/corruption behavior; ephemeral episode and early-reply state starts fresh.
- Restart, backoff, health check and outage state: `_resetRestartState()`, `_resetBackoffState()`, `_resetHealthCheckState()`, `_resetOutageState()`
- Session-manager notice sink: install a recording sink with `setSessionNotifier(sink)` in `beforeEach` and clear it with `setSessionNotifier(undefined)` in `afterEach`, so no sink leaks into a later file
- Session-manager pre-launch trust patcher: production installs the real `.claude.json` patch (`trustPatchPersona`), but tests never do. A test that checks the patch runs before a launch installs a recording fake with `setPreLaunchTrustPatcher(fake)` and calls `_resetPreLaunchTrustPatcher()` in `afterEach` (the setter cannot clear it). Tests of `trust-bootstrap.ts` itself point the persona's `claude_config_dir` at a temp directory, never the real home (b.av2 SR-13.2)
- Session-manager `claude_config_dir` seams (bug b.g57): a test that makes a persona's directory unresolvable injects realpath and lstat with `_setConfigDirFs(fs)` (or uses a dangling symlink under its temp directory) and, when it installs a hold hook, `setConfigDirUnresolvableHook(hook)`; its `afterEach` calls `setConfigDirUnresolvableHook(undefined)` and `_resetConfigDirFs()`. A `makeReloadHarness` run with `realLaunch` installs and clears the hook itself (`h.cleanup()`)
- Per-persona notice episodes (`src/persona-episodes.ts`): the module holds no module-scope state and exports no reset function; build a fresh `createPersonaEpisodes` instance (over a fresh fake clock) in `beforeEach`. An instance's `forgetAll()` is the module's one test reset: it ends every episode silently and leaves the instance open, and no production path calls it (the server's shutdown calls `close()`). A harness that keeps one instance across cases, such as the recovery harness's `cleanup()`, resets it with `forgetAll()`
- Session-manager configured-persona query (b.jg5 SRJ-114): production installs it once in `main()` (`setConfiguredPersonaQuery`); no query means no key is configured and no note latches. A test that installs one calls `_resetConfiguredPersonaQuery()` in `afterEach`. `tests/session-manager.test.ts`'s global `afterEach` calls it and `_resetNotConnectedEpisodes()` (which also forgets the last non-latching note logged per persona). The recovery harness's `cleanup()` and a `realLaunch` reload run's `h.cleanup()` reset their own install
- Session-manager launches: `_resetInFlightLaunches()` forgets in-flight launch bookkeeping between cases; reset the shared production lifecycle/recovery registries as the harness does.
- Session-manager `findMissing` memo (b.jg5 SRJ-120): `_resetFindMissingMemo()` clears the in-flight run, the memoized result and the run counter and restores the default window; `_setFindMissingMemoTtlMs(ms)` overrides the window (`0` makes every ordinary caller run again). A test that counts `findMissing` or `get` calls calls `_resetFindMissingMemo()` in `beforeEach`, since a memoized result or an in-flight run from an earlier case would change both counts
- Session-manager kill retry (b.jg5 SRJ-702): the keep-going query is installed with `setPersonaKillKeepGoingQuery(query)` and removed with `setPersonaKillKeepGoingQuery(undefined)`; the recovery harness sets and undoes it. The kill retry has no module clock seam: each caller hands its clock in (the restart kill adapter, the live-row sequence's dependencies, the start sweep). `src/kill-retry.ts` itself holds no state: a pass budget is built per call (`createKillRetryPassBudget`)
- Session-manager kill-failure alerts (b.jg5 SRJ-704): production installs them once in `main()` (`setKillFailureAlerts`); with none installed a persona kill's alert decision is logged as one line and no own-row read ends an episode. A test that installs them calls `setKillFailureAlerts(undefined)` in `afterEach`, as `tests/server.test.ts`'s adapter cases do; the recovery harness's `cleanup()` removes its own install. `src/kill-failure-alert.ts` holds no state
- Session-manager retired-key store (b.jg5 SRJ-807): production installs `main()`'s one store once (`setRetiredKeyStore`); with none installed no key counts as marked and no own-row read clears an entry. A test that installs one (a store over its own `mkdtempSync` state directory, or a fake `isMarked`/`clear` pair) calls `_resetRetiredKeyStore()` (or `setRetiredKeyStore(undefined)`) in `afterEach`; the recovery harness installs its store and removes it at `cleanup()`.
- Session-manager old-life hold set (b.jg5 SRJ-809): production installs `main()`'s one set once (`setOldLifeHolds`); with none installed no hold begins, ends or is marked and every hook does nothing. A test that installs one (a `createOldLifeHoldSet` over a recording log) calls `_resetOldLifeHolds()` (or `setOldLifeHolds(undefined)`) in `afterEach`; the recovery harness and a `realLaunch` reload run install their own set and remove it at cleanup. The hold set itself holds no module-scope state: build a fresh one per case
- Conflict latch (`src/conflict-latch.ts`): the module holds no module-scope state and exports no reset function; build a fresh `createConflictLatch` instance per test. `forget(key)` drops one persona's latch silently; there is no forget-all
- Test-local Maps/Sets: reassign in `beforeEach`

### Stubbing External Dependencies

- **Slack clients**: The server builds every Slack client through the connection manager's injected `PersonaSlackClientFactory`, so persona Slack traffic is stubbed there: pass `makeStubSlackFactory()`'s `factory` and a fake clock to `createPersonaConnectionManager` (see Connection Manager Tests), and read calls from each persona's `makeStubSlack` stub. Code that takes a client directly (`clientFor`, a Web client argument) gets a stub's `web` client. Never build a real `WebClient` or `SocketModeClient`, with one bounded exception, library-integration probes (see the next bullet). A direct Web API stub that is not a Slack stub records each call in a capture array and returns a mock response
- **Library-integration probes (the one real-client exception)**: A test may build a real `WebClient` or `SocketModeClient`, through `PRODUCTION_SLACK_CLIENT_FACTORY` or the library constructor, only to check what the Slack library itself does with the options CSCB hands it: which logger a client and its own `apps.connections.open` client hold, and what the library's lines leave on the console or in the persona's log. `tests/persona-connection-wiring.test.ts` (the socket and redacting logger probes) is the model. Every rule holds:
  - **Loopback only.** Slack is replaced by local stand-ins on `127.0.0.1`, port 0: `Bun.serve` answering every Web API method (pointed at with `slackApiUrl`) and a raw `Bun.listen` WebSocket endpoint. Tokens are fakes from the credentials helper, and captured output is leak-checked.
  - **Events, not time.** The test awaits events (the client's `start()` settling, the first line reaching the log sink), never a `sleep` or a real-time poll. A library timer the probe relies on is shortened through an option (`clientPingTimeout: 30`).
  - **Library integration only.** CSCB behaviour (option contents, statuses, retries, log lines the manager writes) is tested on the stub factory; a probe covers only what a stub can't show, the library's own handling.
  - **Nothing left running.** Every client has closed or settled before the test ends, and every stand-in is closed and stopped in `finally`.
- **The CLI's agent-director calls**: `tests/cli.test.ts` builds `CliDeps` with its file-scope `makeDeps(overrides)`, whose bundle records every call; never a real client. The precheck's two calls are the `directorGet` and `directorReadPane` overrides, by default a live `waiting` row with no note (`LIVE_ROW`) and a pane (`FAKE_PANE`), so every `stop --stop-bots` and `clean_restart` case passes the precheck unless it overrides one. The bundle records `initClientCalls` and `initClientGates` (each `initClient` call's call timeout and gate options, `undefined` for the whole gate), `getCalls` (each `get`'s id), `readPaneCalls` (each `read-pane`'s id and line count) and `directorCallTimes` (each `get`, `read-pane`, `status`, `pause` and `kill` as `<verb>:<id>`, and each `list` as `LIST_CALL`, with its fake-clock time), beside `statusCalls`, `pauseCalls` and `killCalls`; its `events` log carries the same entries and `spawnSync:<subcommand>` for each `stop` or `start` spawn, so order checks read one list. The `directorKill` override answers the teardown's kill, by default `cannedKillResult(true)` (the Phase 1 success with a kill sent); the `directorList` override answers `clean_restart`'s answer check after a failed teardown, by default a successful empty list, so agent-director answers at the first try and the server is started again (override it to throw for the not-restarted path). `listTimesOf(bundle)` reads each `list`'s fake-clock time, `expectRestarted(bundle, lists?)` checks the `stop` then `start` spawns with the `list` calls between the teardown's last call and the start, and `clean_restart`'s start-failed line is `cleanRestartStartFailedLine(status)` from `src/cli-teardown.ts`; `callMs` makes every director call take that long on the fake clock before it answers, as a call that uses its whole call timeout does (default 0; the SRJ-908 bound cases). For the kill cases, `killCaseOverrides` scripts the kills and the reads between them, `expectKillTries` checks their order and times, and `opsKillRetryLines` reads the lines the Ops persona's kill retry logged under `opsKillLogPrefix()`. `makeLiveStopDeps(overrides)` is a bundle whose server is up at the liveness check and gone by the first poll, so a `stop` that goes ahead sends SIGTERM and "no server signal" shows nothing was stopped. `expectOnePrecheckEach(bundle, ids)` checks exactly one `get` and one one-line `read-pane` of each id and of no other. The file's `afterEach` fails any case whose bundle clock (`createFakeClock`) still has a timer pending.
- **Global `fetch`**: `tests/registry.test.ts` stubs `globalThis.fetch` for every test, so no test reaches the network. Its file-level `beforeEach` installs the stub and its `afterEach` restores the real `fetch`; never install it at file top level. The stub records each call's URL, `Authorization` header and `redirect` mode in the harness's `h.fetches`, then answers through `h.fetchHandler`, which throws by default. A test that expects a fetch sets `h.fetchHandler` and asserts on `h.fetches`; the bearer is the persona stub client's fake bot token (`fakeToken`)
- **Persona events**: Inbound `message` / `app_mention` handling is tested by handing events from the Slack stub's event factories to `persona-routing.ts` directly (see Inbound delivery below). To test what a connection does with its events, build the real `createPersonaEventRouter` with a spy `routing.receive` and `handleClick`, and call it with a persona key, or deliver through a stub socket (`deliver`, `deliverInteractive`) on a manager whose `onEvent` is the router; assert the receiving persona's key reaches `receive` and `receivingPersonaKey`
- **Persona notices**: Notice sites never post through a Web client of their own; they take a notify sink. Assert a site's notices through a recording sink: `initOutageState({ notify, getClient })`, `setSessionNotifier(sink)` for the session manager, the `notify` argument of `runJsonlPersistenceSafeguard`, or an injected recording sink for the owning module. The sink captures the key and the notice body, without the persona prefix. To prove destination, identity or hold-and-flush, use `makeNotifierHarness(config, opts?)` from `tests/test-helpers/persona-notifier.ts` instead of wiring stubs by hand. It builds the real `createPersonaNotifier` over one `makeStubSlack` stub per persona and installs nothing: pass `h.notifier.notify` to the site under test. It also builds the notifier's own `createPersonaDestinations` resolver (`h.destinations`) and a `createPersonaDestinationHold` over it (`h.hold`) on a fake clock (`h.clock`), so a failing notice is held with no real timer. Options: `validated` (`true` by default, `false`, or a key list), per-key `post` outcomes, `leakMarker`, `dryRun`, `clock` (a `FakeClock`; default a new `createFakeClock()`), `getPersonaConfig` (the configuration in effect the resolver reads the switch from at each attempt; by default `config`'s own `allow_invited_channels`, declarative when it has none). Drive held-notice retries with `h.clock` and check the hold with `h.hold.view(key)` (see Destination Failure Tests). Assert with `h.posts(key)`, `h.totalPosts()` and `h.logs`; `h.stub(key)` is the persona's stub, so a `dm`-destination notice scripts its open with `h.stub(key).script.open` and checks the open-then-post order with `h.stub(key).callLog`; `h.validate(key)` marks a client validated, `h.setDryRun(on)` flips dry run, editing `h.personas` simulates a reload, and `h.allPosts()` feeds `assertNoLeak`. The persona teardown window (b.jg5 SRJ-1003): the notifier's startup-errors recorder is the real `recordStartupError` over `h.logDir` (a `startup-errors` directory under the harness's own temp root, made at the first entry), and `h.startupEntries()` answers the entries written to `h.startupErrorsPath`, in order, each parsed into `{ classLabel, text }` (none when no file exists), through `readStartupEntries(logDir)`, the one reader of a test's startup-errors entries, which any test reads its own log directory with (it throws on a line that is not an entry); the `recordStartupError` option replaces it (`null` installs none, to show the missing-recorder line). `h.openTeardown(persona)` drives the window as `runTeardown` does (the submit, then the window opened) and answers a handle whose `close()` closes it and ends the submit; `h.duringTeardown(persona, body)` runs `body` inside one, closed however it ends. `teardownNoticeEntry(persona, text, occasion?, classLabel?)` is the parsed entry the window writes, built with `personaTeardownNoticeEntryText` and flattened as the recorder writes it (pass `PERSONA_KILL_SURVIVOR_LABEL` for the survivor version, `PERSONA_TEARDOWN_NOTICE_ALL_CLEAR_AFTER` for an all-clear after the window). `teardownNoticeLine(persona, text, occasion?, classLabel?)` is the window's log line for that notice, built with `personaTeardownNoticeWrittenLine` with the same arguments and defaults. To route a kill-failure or unclassified-error alert as the server does, build `makeNoticeAlertsRig(h, { logDir, omitStderr?, alertThresholdMs? })` from the same helper: it composes the notice episodes (posting through `h.notifier`, reading its teardown window, on `h.clock`), the kill-failure alerts and the unclassified-error episodes as `main()` does, a persona configured while it is in `h.personas`, each log-only route the real `recordStartupError` into `logDir` (the unclassified one through `personaUnclassifiedErrorEntryText`), and answers `{ episodes, alerts, unclassified, lines }`, `lines` holding every `[slack]` line they wrote; `tests/kill-failure-alert.test.ts` and `tests/startup-errors.test.ts` use it. Never compose them by hand. Call `h.cleanup()` in `afterEach`: it removes the temp root. `tests/persona-notifier.test.ts` is the reference. Don't stub a module-scope `WebClient` for notices.
- **Permission poller and click handler**: Use `tests/test-helpers/permission-relay-harness.ts`, not hand-built failing Web clients or errors. `startManualPoller(deps)` starts the real poller on a manual interval: nothing runs until the test calls `tick(settleMs?)` (fire once and let it settle) or `fire()`. Every persona is up unless the test passes its own `isPersonaUp`; a not-up case builds it with the real `createPersonaUpPredicate` rather than a hand-written check. `makePersonaClients(stubFor)` is the injected `clientFor`: each key gets its `makeStubSlack` stub's Web client, `setUnavailable(key)` makes the lookup return nothing (as before validation, in dry run or for an unknown key), and `calls` records every key asked for. `makeTrailCapture()` is the `emitTrail` sink. Read captured Slack calls with `posts(stub)`, `updates(stub)` and `slackCalls(...stubs)`. Script Slack failures on the stub (`post`, `update` and `open` queues). Each persona's client is a `RelaySlackClient` (`Pick<WebClient, 'chat' | 'conversations'>`), so a `dm` destination's `conversations.open` runs on the persona's own stub. Give each persona its own `makeStubSlack()`, so a call on the wrong client shows up as a non-empty `callLog` on another stub. To test a DM destination, give the persona `permission_prompts: 'dm'`, DMs on and a `dm.contact`. An unscripted open returns `stubOpenedDmId(contact)`; push `openedDm('D0X')` onto `script.open` to choose the ID, or `{ kind: 'platform', error: 'missing_scope' }` to fail the open. Assert the order with `stub.web.callLog.map((c) => c.method)` (`['conversations.open', 'chat.postMessage']`) and that the post names the `D…` ID, never the contact. The poller's deps take either `destinations` or `getPersonaConfig`, and `startManualPoller` takes the same choice: with `getPersonaConfig` (the configuration in effect, whose switch the resolver reads at each attempt) the poller builds its own destination resolver; pass a `createPersonaDestinations` instance as `destinations` to share a DM cache with a notifier, and call `_resetPollerState()` in `beforeEach` so the default resolver's cache and default destination hold don't carry over. A case whose prompt or stuck-prompt warning can fail at the destination passes `destinationHold` built on a fake clock (see Destination Failure Tests). To test an update after a destination change, post the prompt first, then edit the applied persona (DMs off, or a new `permission_prompts`) and assert the update names the recorded conversation with no post or open.
- **JSONL safeguard effects**: `runJsonlPersistenceSafeguard` takes every effect through its `deps` (mountinfo, stat, AD `getRow`, `archiveCountSince`, `recordStartupError`, `home`), so it needs no `mock.module`. An `archiveCountSince` stub has the `ArchiveCountSince` shape `(channelIds, sinceEpochSeconds, ref) => number | null` and receives the persona's `delivery: all` channel IDs in declarative mode. In fungible mode the persona's evidence scope (`personaArchiveEvidenceScope`) holds no channel ID and its zero is not attributable, so a zero count is no evidence (`FUNGIBLE_MODE_ZERO_REASON`; b.deo SRI-704). Layer 2 skips a row whose `cwd` or `config_dir` label doesn't match the persona, so build rows with `cannedGetResult(overrides, persona, home)` and pass the same `home` as `deps.home`
- **Inbound acceptance**: Keep pure channel/DM/fungible rules in `delivery-decision.test.ts`. Drive real intake and preparation through `makeRoutingHarness` (`persona-routing-harness.ts`) with temp `baseDir`, Slack event factories and `h.queued(name)`; assert exact target queues, including empty siblings. Check received text verbatim in the accepted record and journal; compare prepared tag bodies after only the wrapper's documented formatting and escaping. Use `h.order` for store/ack/lookup ordering and the shared queue helper for persistence. Inject `preparationClock` and use `createFakeClock` for hung lookup bounds; `h.receive` resolves after preparation. Retain `LOST_STATE_SETUPS`, `putAtRestartCap`, `waitOnKillFailedHold`, `rowRead`, `upCheck`, `holdLaunches` and the existing recovery inputs rather than duplicating their matrix. Observe row reads/restarts and queued survival, never infer a recovery state from notice text. Assert no intake notices, notification sends or reactions. Reset routing state in `beforeEach`, stop queues/preparation with harness cleanup, and keep every file in the test temp directory.
- **Importing server.ts**: A test may import `src/server.ts`'s exports directly (for example `isHttpVerbose`, the keep-alive helpers, `_buildIsSessionAliveAdapter`). Importing it reads no token, builds no Slack client and creates no directory; at import it only computes its state paths from `SLACK_STATE_DIR` or the home, and registers its SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers. Because the paths are computed at import, the SR-13.2 isolation rules still apply:
  - No test calls `main()` or runs `server.ts` as an entry point in-process.
  - No test sets HOME or `SLACK_STATE_DIR` process-wide in order to import `server.ts`.
  - A check that must exercise `server.ts`'s import or start behaviour against a HOME runs it in a child `bun` process with a `mkdtempSync` HOME and `SLACK_STATE_DIR` and a host-safe environment (`runInFakeHome` from `tests/test-helpers/fake-home-subprocess.ts`), as `tests/server-startup-wiring.test.ts` does.

  Put logic under test in an importable module and test it there, as inbound delivery, the start bring-up (`src/persona-start.ts`) and the event router do. Replicate server.ts logic in a self-contained test server only for HTTP handling that still lives inside `main()` (see Self-Contained Test Servers)
- **server.ts wiring and call order**: To prove a call is wired into `main()` exactly once, or runs before another, audit the source text instead of importing it. Read `src/server.ts` with `readFileSync`, pass it through `stripComments` from `tests/test-helpers/source-audit.ts`, and compare offsets from `indicesOf(regex, code)` (the regex must be global). To pin a call's arguments, use the same file's parsers rather than a hand-rolled regex: `callsOf` and `onlyCallArguments` (throws unless there is exactly one call), `callArguments` and `balancedAfter` for bracketed text, `splitTopLevel` and `objectProperties` to read an argument list or object literal (pass `objectProperties` a spread resolver when the literal spreads a `const` object, as server-startup-wiring.test.ts's `spreadConstObject` does for `retryRunGate`; never read around a spread by hand). To anchor on the start, use `startResolution(code)`: it returns the reload controller's and the start outcome's names, the applied-config variable (`loaded`) and the offsets of `createReloadController(`, `resolveStart(`, the `<loaded> = <outcome>.config` assignment and `runStartBringUp(`, and throws unless each is the only one of its kind. It only locates the start: what else assigns the applied config is checked by the refused-start test in `tests/server-startup-wiring.test.ts` alone, so don't repeat that check elsewhere. `loadedConfigName(code)` is its `loaded`. `mainBody(code)` gives the `[start, end)` of `main()`'s body and `insideMain(code, offset)` says whether an offset lies in it; `shutdownBody(code)` gives the same span for `async function shutdown(`, so a check that a call runs on the shutdown path uses it rather than locating `shutdown` by hand, as `tests/reload-wiring.test.ts`, `tests/cron-scheduler-wiring.test.ts`, `tests/permission-relay-wiring.test.ts` and `tests/server-startup-wiring.test.ts` do. A pass that must run over the applied config comes after `assignAt`; one that must precede the per-persona bring-up comes before `bringUpAt`, not before the `startupSessionManager` text, which now sits in the controller's construction. Always audit the stripped text: a comment or commented-out call that names the function would otherwise satisfy or skew the assertion. `tests/start-sweep-wiring.test.ts`, `tests/jsonl-safeguard-wiring.test.ts` and `tests/server-startup-wiring.test.ts` are the references. Anchor an audit on content that stays in `main()` (a call name, its arguments), never on line numbers or on code that has moved to a module: the Slack handlers now live in the event router, so what a connection does with an event is tested by calling `createPersonaEventRouter`, not by auditing `server.ts`. Audit only what cannot be driven by a behaviour test (a call's presence, its arguments, its order in `main()`)

### Module Mocks (mock.module)

**The hazard.** `bun test` runs every test file in a single process. `mock.module(...)` patches the module registry at the process level — it is process-global state. A top-level `mock.module(...)` call fires at import time and is never cleaned up, so the patched module leaks into every subsequent test file that imports the same module. If the mock factory omits an export that a later file imports, the later file crashes at runtime.

**Why a partial module mock breaks later files.** A top-level mock that replaces `child_process` with only `{ spawn: fakeSpawn }` stays in place for every later file in the run. A later file that does `import { spawnSync } from 'child_process'` then fails with:

```text
SyntaxError: Export named 'spawnSync' not found in module 'node:child_process'
```

**The rule.** Every `mock.module(...)` call must live inside a `beforeEach` or `beforeAll` block, paired with a matching `afterEach` or `afterAll` that calls `mock.restore()`. Never call `mock.module(...)` or `mock.restore()` at file top-level. This includes calls inside a `describe(...)` body but outside hooks — those run at import/registration time and leak the same way.

**Don't:**

```typescript
// BAD — top-level, leaks into all subsequent test files
mock.module('child_process', () => ({ spawn: fakeSpawn }))

describe('MyTest', () => { ... })
```

**Do:**

```typescript
import { mock } from 'bun:test'

describe('MyTest', () => {
  beforeEach(async () => {
    // Spread the real module so later imports get all original exports unchanged
    const real = await import('node:child_process')
    mock.module('node:child_process', () => ({ ...real, spawn: fakeSpawn }))
  })

  afterEach(() => {
    mock.restore()
  })

  test('uses fakeSpawn', () => { ... })
})
```

**Fake children must behave like real ones.** Under Bun 1.4, `mock.restore()` does not undo `mock.module`, so a `child_process` fake stays installed for later files. A fake `spawn` must return an EventEmitter-like child (`on`/`once`, `stdout`/`stderr` streams, `unref`, `kill`, `pid`) that emits `exit` and `close`. Prefer injection over a module mock: the CLI's daemon spawn is a `CliDeps` seam (`spawnDaemon`), so CLI tests pass a fake child there and need no `child_process` mock.

**Put the real exports back yourself.** Because `mock.restore()` leaves the module mocked, a file that mocks a package copies its real namespace at import (`const REAL = { ...ns }` from `import * as ns`) and, in `afterEach`, calls `mock.module(<name>, () => REAL)` before `mock.restore()`. Build each fake over the copy (`{ ...REAL, fn: fake }`) so every other export stays real, and to prove the real exports are back, compare the live namespace with the copy entry by entry in `afterAll`, never naming an export. For `agent-director`, prefer an injected seam: no test mocks the package, and the install check and the postinstall probe take their resolver and install check as injected dependencies. Spreading the namespace or its copy into a `mock.module` factory is allowed although it carries `Client` and `resolveSystemBinary`; reading either from it is not (see Host safety under Isolation).

**Enforcement.** `scripts/check-no-toplevel-mock-module.ts` runs as part of `pretest`. It scans all files under `tests/` and fails the test run if any line at column 0 (zero leading whitespace) contains `mock.module(` or `mock.restore(`.

### Throwaway Git Repos

Fixtures that create a throwaway git repo and commit in it use one canonical setup. Reference implementation: `initRepo` in `tests/publish-promote-bun-g-cwd.test.ts`. `tests/audit-finished-tickets-script.test.ts` follows it (temp HOME, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` on every child that runs git, the repo-local fake identity and `commit.gpgsign false`, one commit) with one difference: its HOME is a separate `audit-home-*` `mkdtempSync` directory outside the fixture project, so the audit script can't mistake it for a hive.

- **git runs under a temp HOME.** Every git child, and every script child that runs git, gets its `env` from a direct `hostSafeChildEnv(fixtureHome, { tools: ['git', …], extras: { GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM: '1' } })` call. In the reference file the fixture HOME is an empty directory inside the fixture's `mkdtempSync` root, next to the repo, removed with it; a fixture whose script scans the tree around the repo (as the audit script does) puts HOME in its own temp directory instead. The temp HOME keeps the host's global git configuration out and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` keeps the system one out, so no host identity, hooks path, signing key or credential reaches git.
- **The repo declares its own fake identity.** With no global configuration there is no identity, so the fixture sets a repo-local `user.name` and `user.email` (the reference file's constants: `Fixture`, `test@example.invalid`; never a real person or domain) and `commit.gpgsign false`.
- **Commit once.** Nothing on the host can reject or rewrite the commit, so it succeeds on the first attempt on any host. Don't wrap it in a retry.

```typescript
git(fx, 'init', '-q')
git(fx, 'config', 'user.name', FIXTURE_USER_NAME)
git(fx, 'config', 'user.email', FIXTURE_USER_EMAIL)
git(fx, 'config', 'commit.gpgsign', 'false')
...
git(fx, 'commit', '-q', '-m', 'init')
```

The reference file has a case asserting that the fixture commit's author is the repo-local fake identity, which fails if anything rewrote or overrode it. It does not prove that no host git configuration reached git: a repo-local `user.*` value overrides a global or system one, so the identity would match either way.

**Do not isolate git by other means.** No `core.hooksPath` redirects, no `--no-verify`, no `GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*` overrides, and no env built outside `hostSafeChildEnv`.

**To make a later commit fail on purpose,** write a failing `.git/hooks/pre-commit` into the fixture repo after the fixture's own commit, so only the commit under test meets it. To make a `git` subcommand fail, put a stub `git` first on the child's PATH that runs the real `git` for every other subcommand. Reference: the `commit-fails` and `add-fails` fixtures in `tests/publish-prepare-changelog.test.ts` (b.1ba).

### Self-Contained Test Servers

Test an HTTP endpoint through its real handler module, not a copy of its logic. `/interject` is the reference: `tests/interject.test.ts` imports `handleInterject` from `src/interject.ts` and injects its dependencies (a persona config from `makeMultiPersonaConfig`, durable queue acceptance, an injected clock and a capturing logger). When a case needs a real HTTP request, (the 32 KB cap and a loopback delivery), it serves the real handler from a port-0 `Bun.serve()`. `/clear-latch` follows the same pattern: `tests/clear-latch.test.ts` imports `handleClearLatch` from `src/clear-latch.ts`, injects the persona config, a recording clear by hand and a capturing logger, and makes its one real request against a port-0 `Bun.serve()` bound to `127.0.0.1` (`startClearLatchServer`); `tests/conflict-latch.test.ts` drives the same handler over the recovery harness's real clear by hand (`clearByHand`). The route name, the log lines, the `server.port` causes and the body cap are imported from `src/`, never written as literals. When logic still lives in `server.ts`, move it into an importable module. Its exported helpers can be imported directly (see Importing server.ts above), but the `/mcp` fetch handler is built inside `main()` with `Bun.serve`, which no test may run, so a stand-in server remains the way to test that branch until it moves (`sse-timeout.test.ts` mirrors the MCP GET/POST branch this way). Prove `server.ts` delegates to the handler with a source-text audit (see server.ts wiring above).

Cron tests drive the injected queue accept function directly: `cron-dispatch.test.ts` scripts storage results, and `cron-scheduler-interject.test.ts` composes the real scheduler, dispatcher and queue. They need no HTTP listener. For any `Bun.serve()` in a test:

- Bind to port 0 (random available port) to avoid conflicts
- Share Maps between test code and server handler via closure
- Stop the server in afterAll()

### Reading Shipped Markdown

Tests that read a user doc or a skill use the shared helper `tests/test-helpers/markdown.ts`, not a file-local heading scanner. It is fence-aware: a `# comment` in a shell block is never a heading, and a section ends at the next heading of the same or a higher level.

- `headings(text)`: every heading outside fenced code, with its line, level, title and `## Title` form.
- `sectionRange(text, match)` and `findSection(text, match)`: the line range or the body of the first matching section, undefined when none matches. `match` is the heading as written (`'## Title'`, exact) or a RegExp over that form, never with the `g` or `y` flag.
- `requiredSection(text, match, file)`: `findSection` that throws `<file> has no heading …`, so a moved or renamed section fails naming it instead of a check passing on an empty string. Prefer it to `findSection`.
- `splitFences(text)`: the prose outside fenced blocks, and each block's info word and exact body. Take a shipped command or example from its section's one block and fail on any other count or tag, as `tests/credentials-command.test.ts` does.
- `headingSlug(title)` and `headingAnchors(text)`: GitHub's anchor for a title, and every heading's anchor in order (a repeat gets `-1`, `-2`), for checking that a link's `#anchor` resolves.
- `flat(text)`: whitespace collapsed, so a phrase matches across a line wrap.
- `classHeading(label)`: a RegExp for the `###` heading naming a class label as a code span, with any title after it; the label is matched literally and a longer label does not match. Pass it to `requiredSection` to read a class's entry.

Find a section by its title, never by its step number (a whole-word RegExp such as `/^### .*\bAdd a persona$/`), so renumbering a step doesn't break the test. `tests/markdown-helper.test.ts` covers the helper.

### Shipped-Description Audit (AC 46)

`tests/shipped-docs.test.ts` audits the README, the ten `USER_DOC_FILES` pages, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, every file under `skills/`, the manifest, the credentials script, `MCP_INSTRUCTIONS`, the Slack reply reminder, `CRONTABLE_TEMPLATE_HEADER` and exported `SET_CHANNEL_DELIVERY_TEXTS` through `SHIPPED_TEXTS`. The user pages are shipped descriptions; the CHANGELOG and internal architecture/engineering guides are not in the forbidden-term scan. `OPERATOR_TEXTS` includes those internal texts for release-entry, runbook and other operator contracts. The five inline Slack tool descriptions have no exported list for this scan; review them manually. `set_channel_delivery` descriptions and result/refusal wording are read through their exports, with registry tests also checking the offered definition.

The audit also resolves links and checks `tests/README.md` for the integration inventory and retained-row guidance; that developer README is neither a shipped description nor an operator text. `docs/registry-install-runbook.md` has dedicated link/scope checks. R44 rows cover receiving, privacy, settings, destination notices, reload queues, scheduled fires, debug remedies and release notes. Their sensitivity controls omit each obligation in memory. AC64 rejects accepted-message loss claims across shipped descriptions while preserving legitimate initial-store failure, quarantined corruption and explicit downgrade-loss guidance.

R55 has exactly four numbered downgrade phases. Row and executable controls check intake/drain, confirmed `resume_enabled: false`, bot stop before earlier-build installation, and fresh channels launch before restoring and confirming resume. `test-40-fmk-real-downgrade.sh` calls `real_prepare 2.1.280` before either release starts; its helper binds and verifies the native executable and writes agent-director’s `claude_path`. That selection remains in force after the package downgrade; the script does not install a pin afterward. The operator procedure still restores the earlier build’s required pin before start. Omission/comment/reordering controls guard actual executable operations. The 0.12.0 downgrade copy must match the maintained section word for word after removing its introduction and normalising prescribed relative links; the 0.11.0 copies stay frozen. Measure page budgets manually against `docs/user-docs-guide.md`; the audit does not enforce their line counts.

- **One term list.** A retired term goes in `FORBIDDEN_TERMS`, in the group it belongs to with its SR cited, and gets a sample in `SAMPLES` (the self-check fails until it has one). Don't write a second forbidden-term check in a skill's own test; the wizard's shell-token checks were moved into the audit for that reason.
- **The exemptions.** `AUDIT_EXCEPTIONS` holds three entries, each a file, a heading and the terms allowed under it, and a pin case holds it to exactly these: `SR_1_7_EXCEPTION`, the debugging skill's `## Pre-persona configuration` entry, for the three pre-persona keys (SR-1.7); `SWITCH_OVER_EXCEPTION`, docs/migration.md's switch-over runbook section (its heading built from `PHASE1_RUNBOOK_SECTION_TITLE`), for `access.json` alone, since the runbook saves the previous CSCB's file (b.jg5 SRJ-1108, SRJ-1516); and `ROLLBACK_EXCEPTION`, docs/migration.md's rollback runbook section (its heading built from `ROLLBACK_RUNBOOK_SECTION_TITLE`), for `access.json` alone, since its step 8 restores that file (b.jg5 SRJ-1109, SRJ-1516). Every other term stays banned inside all three sections, the token variables' names included, and the exempt terms stay banned everywhere else; a missing heading fails the audit, never exempting the file. The SR-1.7 entry and the two runbook entries each have an "only allowed hit" case (the runbook case also fails if either entry is idle) and synthetic self-checks. These three are the only sanctioned entries: fix any other real hit by rewriting the shipped text in persona terms; never add an exemption, an allow-list entry or a narrower pattern to make it pass.
- **Operator texts and the CHANGELOG.** `OPERATOR_TEXTS` is the one list of the operator texts (the README and the user docs in `USER_DOC_FILES`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, the three skills, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/engineering-guide.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`); the README's row checks read the user docs as one text (`userDocsText`, each page opening with its `#` title) and write a link into another page from the page that holds it (`docLink`); read one with `operatorText(name)`, never a second `readRepoFile` of the same file. It is distinct from `SHIPPED_TEXTS`: user docs occur in both for different checks; do not add internal operator texts to the forbidden-term list. A release-entry rule is a row of `RELEASE_ENTRY_CHECKS` with a self-check in `REVERTS` that edits the CHANGELOG back to the wording the rule replaces; a name a release note introduces is a `HATCH_NOTE_NAMES` row imported from its `src/` export. The switch-over and rollback element tables run over both carriers in `SWITCH_OVER_CARRIERS` and `ROLLBACK_CARRIERS` (the docs/migration.md section and the CHANGELOG copy), so a runbook rule is written once and checked in both. A runbook step names what differs by host by its role, with an example (the host's sweep schedule, the shared orchestrator prompt), never by one host's path; `HOST_SPECIFIC_PATHS` holds the paths neither runbook may name, checked over both runbooks in both carriers and over docs/migration.md's Migration section. A runbook row that expects a link to a heading outside the runbook builds it with `readmeLink(title)`, so the CHANGELOG copy's `docs/…#…` form is checked too.
- **SRJ-1101 over the operator texts.** `OPERATOR_TEXTS` carries SRJ-1101's checks, every hit reported by file and line: `SRJ_1101_TERMS` (both spellings of the finished-row option included); the raw tmux session kill only in switch-over steps 5 and 6, with `=`, in both carriers, and nowhere else; every attach with `=`, apart from `PREFIX_KEY_REASON_SPANS` (SRJ-1102); the tmux-command allow-list (`TMUX_ALLOWED_FORMS` over `TMUX_COMMAND_NAMES`, a file-local list of tmux's own command names citing tmux(1): the attach with `=`, the read-only `ls`, `list-windows` and `display-message`, and the session kill with `-t =` only in steps 5 and 6; `list-sessions` and global flags are off it); the SRJ-613 proof claim; and the kill advice ("check the result" over all seven texts; "on an error, don't delete or respawn" over the README and the CHANGELOG). A finding is fixed by rewriting the text, never by an exemption, a narrower pattern or a new allow-list form. A backticked agent-director command line allowed where `UPGRADE_FORMS` applies is a `RUNBOOK_COMMAND_SPANS` entry under ruling C-1, removed exactly as written; the debugging skill's runtime-stop remedy is its one entry outside the runbook.
- **Wording that must stay legal.** If a term would catch legitimate persona wording, narrow its pattern and pin the wording both ways: a "not flagged" self-check on in-memory text, and a `NOT_HITS_BY_DESIGN` entry tied to the file and the reason when the wording is in shipped text.
- **Instructions through the export.** Read the MCP instruction text as the exported `MCP_INSTRUCTIONS`, never from `src/registry.ts`'s source text.
- **No persona-name rule.** Shipped text describes no persona-name format (b.av2 SR-1.2 as amended by b.deo SRI-102, SRI-202), so no case expects one.
- **Mutation checks off the tree.** Prove a case fails by editing an in-memory or `mkdtempSync` copy; a shipped file edited in place to probe a case is restored byte for byte.

## Assertions

- Use `expect(x).toBe(y)` for primitives
- Use `expect(x).toEqual(y)` for objects/arrays
- Use `expect(x).toBeUndefined()` / `toBeDefined()` for presence checks
- Avoid `.toBeTruthy()` / `.toBeFalsy()` — be specific about expected values
- When testing async behavior through closures, use non-null assertions (`resolved!`) if TypeScript narrows incorrectly
- A notice test imports its expected lines from the builders or exported constants in `src/` (or a helper assembled from them, such as `expectedConflictNotice`), never writes them as literals; one pin case per notice module holds the literal texts. Check the session-ending-command rule against CSCB's own lines (`cscbOwnLines`), not the quoted agent-director description, which may say "no kill was sent"
- A version is never written literally: an agent-director version comes from `src/` or `tests/test-helpers/agent-director-versions.ts`, and a Claude Code version is that helper's `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE_VERSION`, whose value appears in the code of no other `tests/` file and of no `src/` file (checked by `tests/ad-version-gate.test.ts`)

## What to Test

- Happy path for each public function
- Error cases (invalid input, missing data, API failures)
- Edge cases from the SRD/PRD
- Concurrent operations (e.g., multiple pending requests)
- Cleanup on abort/disconnect
- State isolation between tests (no leaking via module-scoped Maps)

### Bounded costs (b.jg5 SRJ-1204)

Each bound is asserted by the requirement it cites, with its number imported from `src/`; a bound with no number is a count rule, asserted as an exact call count. Only a pin case types the requirement's number, beside the constant it pins. SRJ-1201's and SRJ-1202's suites are kept; a case in them changes only where a named SRJ changed that behaviour.

| Bound | SRJ | Constant | Asserted in |
|---|---|---|---|
| Latch re-check interval | SRJ-505 | `LATCH_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS` | tests/conflict-latch.test.ts |
| Latch re-check: one read, then at most the one call its case names; the probe reads one line; a latched resume's or reuse's probe or retry runs only on a reading of its row ended or missing | SRJ-505 | count rule; `PROBE_PANE_READ_LINES` | tests/conflict-latch.test.ts |
| Version re-check interval | SRJ-204 | `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS` | tests/ad-version-gate.test.ts |
| Settings read: one per version re-check tick | SRJ-209 | `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS` (no timer of its own) | tests/ad-version-gate.test.ts, tests/ad-settings.test.ts |
| Live-row sequence's `find-missing` runs | SRJ-705 | `LIVE_ROW_SEQUENCE_MAX_RUNS` (spacing `LIVE_ROW_SEQUENCE_RUN_SPACING_MS`) | tests/live-row-sequence.test.ts |
| One live-row sequence per persona | SRJ-706 | count rule | tests/live-row-sequence.test.ts |
| Pending-row rule: at most one run per retry | SRJ-410 | `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_BASE_S` to `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_CEILING_S` | tests/pending-row.test.ts |
| One abort per stuck-launch episode | SRJ-412 | count rule | tests/pending-row.test.ts |
| Start sweep delays the start pass by at most one row's kill retries | SRJ-702 | `KILL_RETRY_TRIES`, `KILL_RETRY_SPACING_MS` | tests/session-manager.test.ts |
| The start pass never waits on a live-row sequence | SRJ-702 | count rule | tests/session-manager.test.ts |
| CLI teardown per persona | SRJ-908 | `teardownBoundMs` (`PRECHECK_TRIES`, `PRECHECK_TRY_SPACING_MS`, `KILL_RETRY_TRIES`, `KILL_RETRY_SPACING_MS`) | tests/cli.test.ts, tests/cli-teardown.test.ts |

The timers `LATCH_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS`, `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS`, `DIALOG_READY_TIMEOUT_MS`, `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_BASE_S`, `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_CEILING_S` and `DELIVERY_STUCK_AFTER_MS` can be shortened in an integration run by the test-only override block (see the integration scripts rules); the unit tests assert the exported defaults and the controllers' own interval, base and ceiling inputs, never a real shortened timer.

## What NOT to Test

- Internal implementation details (private helper functions)
- Exact log output (test behavior, not logging)
- Timing-dependent behavior with real delays — inject the clock and timers (see Backoff and Retry Tests); older suites use short configurable timeouts
- Test infrastructure itself (factories, stubs, reset helpers) — if a fixture breaks, the real tests that use it will fail anyway

## Keeping the Suite Lean

The goal is good coverage with as few lines of test code as possible. Apply the 80/20 rule: strong coverage of behavior does not require testing every input permutation — don't gold-plate.

- Test behavior, not implementation: assert on what a function produces (return values, state changes, captured calls), not on how it got there. A test needing 3+ stubs to run is usually testing implementation details and will break on harmless refactors.
- Prefer removing a redundant test over keeping it "just in case"
- Prefer `test.each` over copy-pasted test functions
- Prefer factory functions over repeated inline setup blocks
